# Writing a harness system

Everything needed to write a harness system, in one file: how a system is laid out, every builder, type, ref and
helper of `@harness/sdk` with its TSDoc and signature, the run context, renderers, packaging, testing, the mistakes
the compiler refuses, and complete compiled examples. An LLM writing a system needs only this file.

_Generated by `scripts/docs/authoring.ts` from the SDK's sources (`npm run docs:authoring`); do not edit it by hand._
_Every code sample is a compiled file of the repository, so it is correct for this version of the SDK._

## 1. Start here

A **system** is a Node process with a unique name that connects to the harness **hub** over one WebSocket and adds
things to it: fns (functions people, agents and other systems run), named types, context for the LLM, `@` mentions,
hooks on user messages, settings, resources, sectioners, actions, model providers (embedders among them) and Vue
renderers. The hub owns every
LLM call, user, permission, budget and record; a system only declares what it offers and implements it.

Everything a system contributes is namespaced by its name: the `notes` system's fn `add` is `notes/add`, its type
`Note` is `notes/Note`, a method of it is `notes/Note.pin`.

**To write a system, in order:**

1. Copy the template: `harness system new <name>` (it copies `systems/template` to `systems/<name>` and puts the
   name everywhere `template` stands). **The system folder and the template** shows every file of it.
2. Write the **contract** in `shared/index.ts`: `system('<name>', { description }).with({ ... })`. Every shape is a gin
   type made with `g` (**Types with gin**). The sections from **The contract** to **The hub contract** cover each part a contract can hold.
3. Implement it in `server/impl.ts`. The implementation's type is computed from the contract, so the compiler lists
   anything missing and refuses anything extra (**The server**, **The run context and services**, **Calling other fns**, **Config, events, lifecycle and data**).
4. Optionally draw it in `client/`: Vue components typed by the contract (**The client**).
5. Test it with the test kit (`testHub`, `mockModel`, `contextFolder` and `preview` from `@harness/sdk/testing`,
   **Testing**) and run the tests with `harness test`, which `fs/install_system` runs too.
6. Run it against a hub, pair it, or build, pack and install it (**Packaging**). An installed system is not live: an
   admin reviews it in the hub and presses Go live before anything it offers can be called, so tell the person to do
   that. Read **Common mistakes** before you finish.

Four rules hold everywhere; the compiler enforces the first three:

- **One source of types.** Every shape (args, results, config, payloads, settings, attachments, state, forms) is written
  once as a gin type; every TypeScript type is inferred from it. Never write a TypeScript type a gin type already
  describes: use `Infer<typeof X>` or `ValueOf<typeof T>`.
- **Refs, not strings.** Everything declared gets a typed ref (`notes.fns.find`, `notes.context.guide`,
  `notes.settings.editing`). Refer to things through refs, so a reference to something that doesn't exist doesn't compile.
- **The contract declares, the server implements.** Everything declared must be implemented, and nothing undeclared
  can be.
- **Data lives in the hub.** What a system keeps for people (notes, tasks, anything shared or looked up later) is
  records of a stored type (`type({ records })`), read and written with `ctx.data` and searched by the hub's database
  (**Stored types and the data system**). Never keep it in a file or database of the system's own, and never load a
  whole set to filter or search it in code: every read is a query with a page. A system's folder (`ctx.dataDir`) holds
  only its own config, keys and caches.

The worked example throughout is the **notes** system (`scripts/docs/example`, shown whole in **Worked examples**): notes with
titles and tags, kept and searched by the hub as records of a stored type. Every code sample in this guide is a region of a file
that `npm run typecheck` compiles, so each is correct for this version of the SDK.

**Imports.** Contracts and renderers import from `@harness/sdk` (pure, safe in the browser). Server code imports
`serve` and friends from `@harness/sdk/server`, tests import the test kit from `@harness/sdk/testing`, and a process
that builds its renderers imports `buildClient` from `@harness/sdk/build`. Wire shapes (`ResourceStat`, `RunInfo`,
`UndoOutcome`, …) are in `@harness/core`; **Core types** lists every one the SDK refers to, resolved to plain TypeScript.

## 2. The system folder and the template

A system is a folder in one shape, which is also what gets built, zipped and installed:

```text
systems/<name>/
  system.json      name, version, description, link protocol, server and client entries, one instance or many
  package.json     npm dependencies and scripts, for development only (its version is not the system's)
  tsconfig.json    extends the repository's base config; listed in the root tsconfig's references
  shared/index.ts  the contract: everything the system offers, with no implementation (browser safe: no Node imports)
  server/env.ts    the tunables, an @ayepi/env slice: hub URL, state folder, pairing token, and the system's own
  server/index.ts  the server's seams: an interface per outside dependency, the config type, re-exports
  server/impl.ts   createImpl(deps): the implementation, built from injected dependencies so tests use fakes
  server/main.ts   the process: parse the env, build the real dependencies, serve
  server/*.test.ts tests (vitest), over the in-memory hub
  client/index.ts  client(contract, renderers): Vue renderers, optional
  dist/            what `harness system build` makes and `harness system pack` zips
```

- **`system.json` is the one source of the name and version.** The contract's name (`system('<name>', …)`) must equal
  `system.json`'s `name`; `harness system build` and `serve()` check it. Its schema is in **Packaging**.
- **Config is not a file.** It lives in the hub's database, set by an admin and checked against the contract's config
  type. The system reads it as `ctx.config`.
- **Data has its own folders.** The hub gives each installed instance `state`, `data` and `cache` folders outside the
  package, so upgrades keep them; code reaches them as `ctx.dataDir` and `ctx.cacheDir` (**Config, events, lifecycle and
  data**). Never write into the system's own folder at runtime.
- **Name rules.** A system name is lowercase letters, digits and dashes, unique on the hub, and not `hub`, `memory` or
  `resource`. Local names (fns, types, context, …) are unique within their section. Never name a file `aux`, `con`,
  `prn`, `nul`, `com1`–`com9` or `lpt1`–`lpt9`: Windows reserves them and git cannot track them.
- **Code style the SDK expects.** Strict TypeScript, no `any`, no unchecked casts (`as` only for `as const`). Tunables in
  the env slice, never `process.env` in feature code. Structured logs: `ctx.log.info('notes.added', { id })`, a stable
  dotted key, then fields. An interface for each outside dependency, so tests inject fakes. TSDoc on every export.

### The template

`systems/template` is a system with nothing declared: every section of the contract and the implementation is a comment
naming what goes there and which builder declares it, ending in `TODO`. It typechecks and its test passes as it is.
Fill the sections you need and delete the comments of the ones you don't. These are its files, verbatim:

#### `systems/template/README.md`

````md
# template system

TODO: say what this system offers and who it is for, in a sentence or two.

A harness system: a process that connects to the hub over WebSocket and adds fns, types, context and renderers.
`harness system new <name>` made this folder from `systems/template`; everything still marked `TODO` is a placeholder.
The guide to every part of a system is `packages/sdk/AUTHORING.md` (in the chat: the `fs/system-authoring` context,
`/new-system`).

## Folder

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `system.json` | the package manifest: name, version, description, link protocol, entries, instances. The one source of the name and version |
| `package.json` | npm dependencies and scripts, for development only |
| `shared/index.ts` | the contract: everything the system offers, with no implementation |
| `server/impl.ts` | the implementation of the contract, built from injected dependencies |
| `server/env.ts` | the tunables (`@ayepi/env`): hub URL, state folder, pairing token, and the system's own |
| `server/main.ts` | the process: builds the implementation and serves it to the hub |
| `server/link.test.ts` | tests over an in-memory hub (`FakeHub`) |
| `client/index.ts` | the renderers (Vue), optional |

## Run it

```sh
npm run typecheck                                  # from the harness repository: every project, this one included
npx vitest run systems/template                    # its tests
HARNESS_HUB=ws://127.0.0.1:4300/link npx tsx systems/template/server/main.ts
```

The first time it connects, the hub has never seen the name, so the system waits to be paired: it prints a pairing
code and link (and a QR code). An admin opens the link, checks the code, fills in the config and approves. To skip
that, an admin makes a one-time token (Admin, Systems, Pairing token) and the system starts with
`HARNESS_PAIRING_TOKEN=<token>`. It keeps its key in `HARNESS_SYSTEM_STATE` (default `.harness/systems/template`), so
later starts sign in on their own.

## Build, pack and install

```sh
harness system build systems/template              # typecheck, name check, bundle server and client into dist/
harness system pack systems/template               # hash every file and zip dist/ to template-<version>.zip
harness system install template-<version>.zip      # install on a running hub, which pairs and starts it
```

Config lives in the hub (an admin sets it, checked against the contract's config type), never in a file here. An
installed system's data goes in the folders the hub gives it, outside this one, so upgrades keep it.
````

#### `systems/template/client/index.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The template system's client renderers: Vue components for what its contract declares, each typed by it. The chat
 * loads the built module from the hub. Without renderers the chat draws everything itself, so this can stay empty.
 */
import { client } from '@harness/sdk';
import { template } from '../shared/index.ts';

export default client(template, {
  // fns: how runs of a fn are drawn: `{ <fn>: Component }`, props `FnViewOf<typeof template, '<fn>'>`.
  // kinds: how fns of a kind are drawn when the fn has no renderer of its own: `{ <kind>: Component }`.
  // attachments: `{ <attachment>: Component }`, props `AttachmentViewOf<typeof template, '<attachment>'>`.
  // inputs: editors (and, read-only, viewers) of named types: `{ <Type>: Component }`, props `InputViewOf<typeof template, '<Type>'>`.
  // mentions: hover cards of mention providers: `{ <mention>: Component }`, props `MentionView<Target> & WithSystem<...>`.
  // resources: viewers of resource formats: `{ <format>: Component }`, props `ResourceView & WithSystem<...>`.
  // TODO: add renderers in .vue files beside this one and list them here, or delete these comments.
});
```

#### `systems/template/server/env.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The template system's tunables, an `@ayepi/env` slice: where the hub is, where the system keeps its state, and any
 * tunable of its own. Durations are milliseconds with no unit suffix. Code reads these through `templateEnv.parse()`
 * in `main.ts`, never `process.env`, and passes what it needs to `createImpl`.
 */
import { env } from '@ayepi/env';
import { systemFields, systemState } from '@harness/sdk/server';

/** The template system's env slice: the fields every system has (`systemFields`) and its own tunables. */
export const templateEnv = env({
  ...systemFields(),
  // TODO: add tunables, each a zod schema (`import { z } from 'zod'`) with its default and a TSDoc line saying what it
  // is, named with the system's name in capitals as a prefix (fs uses `FS_`):
  //   /** How long one request to the service may take. */
  //   <NAME>_TIMEOUT: z.number().int().min(1).default(30_000),
}).add(systemState('template'));

/** The template system's settings, resolved. */
export type TemplateSettings = ReturnType<typeof templateEnv.parse>;
```

#### `systems/template/server/impl.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The template system's implementation: everything its contract declares, and nothing else. Its type is computed from
 * the contract (`SystemImpl`), so a declared fn, method, kind, dynamic context, hook, mention, sectioner, action
 * or model provider left out does not compile, and neither does one the contract doesn't declare.
 */
import type { SystemImpl } from '@harness/sdk';
import type { template } from '../shared/index.ts';
import type { TemplateSettings } from './env.ts';

/** Everything the implementation is built from: the settings and each outside dependency (`index.ts`). */
export interface TemplateDeps {
  readonly settings: TemplateSettings;
  // TODO: add a field per outside dependency, or delete this comment.
}

/** Build the template system's implementation. */
export function createImpl(_deps: TemplateDeps): SystemImpl<(typeof template)['defs']> {
  return {
    // fns: one `{ async run(args, ctx) { ... return ctx.done(result); } }` per native fn, plus
    // `async undo(payload, ctx) { ... return { status: 'undone', summary }; }` for a fn that declares `undo`.
    // TODO: implement every declared fn, or delete this comment.

    // types: per named type with methods, `{ <method>: { async run(self, args, ctx) { ... } } }`.
    // TODO: implement every declared method, or delete this comment.

    // kinds: per kind, `{ async check(payload, ctx), async describe(payload, ctx), async run(payload, args, ctx) }`.
    // `describe` returns at least one example worked out from the payload (`examples: [{ args: sampleArgs(args), note }]`).
    // TODO: implement every declared kind, or delete this comment.

    // context: per dynamic context (declared without `content`), `async (args, ctx) => text`.
    // TODO: implement every dynamic context, or delete this comment.

    // hooks: per hook, `async (input, ctx) => contributions` (text blocks, suggested or loaded context and fns).
    // TODO: implement every declared hook, or delete this comment.

    // mentions: per mention provider, `{ async search(query, ctx), async hover(target, ctx), async llm(target, ctx) }`.
    // TODO: implement every declared mention provider, or delete this comment.

    // providers: per model provider, `(ctx) => provider`, an aeye `Provider` for the current config (made again when it
    // changes); an embedding model's vectors come from its `embed`.
    // TODO: implement every declared provider, or delete this comment.

    // sectioners: per sectioner, `async (text, stat) => sections`, keys made with `sectionKeys(paths)`.
    // TODO: implement every declared sectioner, or delete this comment.

    // actions: per action, `async (input, ctx) => result`.
    // TODO: implement every declared action, or delete this comment.

    // resources: a `ResourceProvider` ({ roots, list, stat, read, watch? }) if this system serves resources.
    // TODO: serve resources, or delete this comment.

    // events: hub events to hear, by type (`message`, `run`, `resources`, `system`, `fns`): `async (event, ctx) => {}`.
    // TODO: handle events, or delete this comment.

    // lifecycle: `installed`, `started`, `configured`, `stopping`, `upgraded`, `uninstalled`, each `async (ctx) => {}`.
    // TODO: handle lifecycle moments, or delete this comment.
  };
}
```

```ts
/**
 * The template system's server: its implementation (`impl.ts`) and the process that serves it (`main.ts`). Each outside
 * thing the implementation talks to (a service, a database, the clock) is an interface here, so `createImpl` takes
 * real ones in `main.ts` and fakes in tests.
 *
 * TODO: declare an interface per outside dependency, or delete this comment:
 *   export interface Service { fetch(id: string, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<Item | undefined> }
 *
 * TODO: once the contract declares config, name its type for the server's code (every context has it as `ctx.config`):
 *   export type TemplateConfig = ConfigOf<(typeof template)['defs']>;
 */
export { template } from '../shared/index.ts';
export { createImpl, type TemplateDeps } from './impl.ts';
```

#### `systems/template/server/link.test.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The template system with the SDK test kit (`@harness/sdk/testing`): `testHub` serves it to a fake hub in memory
 * (the real link, handshake and wire checks; every `ctx.*` call recorded). Keep these passing as the system grows, and
 * add a test of each sort beside them as it gets fns (the guide's Testing section has the details):
 *
 * - a fn call: `const { result, progress, hubCalls } = await kit.call('<fn>', args)`, typed by the contract;
 * - a mocked model: `testHub(template, impl, { model: mockModel(['a reply', toolCall('<tool>', args)]) })`;
 * - a real model: `realModel.it('…', async () => { … })`, skipped without `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`;
 * - a component preview: `await preview({ name, module, states })`, shot at phone and desktop, light and dark;
 * - a kind: `await kit.kindProps('<kind>', payload)`; a context folder: `await contextFolder(dir, template)`.
 */
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { SystemPackageManifest } from '@harness/core';
import { testHub, type TestSystem } from '@harness/sdk/testing';
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { template } from '../shared/index.ts';
import { templateEnv } from './env.ts';
import { createImpl } from './impl.ts';

const packageManifest = SystemPackageManifest.parse(JSON.parse(readFileSync(new URL('../system.json', import.meta.url), 'utf8')));

describe('template', () => {
  let kit: TestSystem<'template', (typeof template)['defs']> | undefined;
  afterEach(async () => {
    await kit?.stop();
    kit = undefined;
  });

  it('is named as system.json says', () => {
    expect(template.name).toBe(packageManifest.name);
  });

  it('connects, says hello and sends its manifest', async () => {
    // TODO: once the contract declares config, give a valid value here
    kit = await testHub(template, createImpl({ settings: templateEnv.parse() }), { config: {} });
    expect(kit.hub.hellos.map((hello) => hello.name)).toEqual([packageManifest.name]);
    expect(kit.hub.manifests).toHaveLength(1);
  });
});
```

#### `systems/template/server/main.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The template system's process: reads its settings, builds the implementation with the real dependencies, and serves
 * it to the hub, pairing the first time. `harness system build` bundles this file; the hub starts the bundle for an
 * installed system, and `npx tsx server/main.ts` starts it by hand.
 */
import { loggedHost, serve } from '@harness/sdk/server';
import { template } from '../shared/index.ts';
import { templateEnv } from './env.ts';
import { createImpl } from './impl.ts';

const settings = templateEnv.parse();
const { host, log } = loggedHost(template.name);

// TODO: build each outside dependency here and pass it to createImpl.
const impl = createImpl({ settings });

log.info('template.serving', { hub: settings.HARNESS_HUB });
const running = await serve(template, impl, {
  hub: settings.HARNESS_HUB,
  state: settings.HARNESS_SYSTEM_STATE,
  ...(settings.HARNESS_PAIRING_TOKEN === undefined ? {} : { pairingToken: settings.HARNESS_PAIRING_TOKEN }),
  // client: the folder of the built renderers (`buildClient` from `@harness/sdk/build`), once `client/` has any.
}, host);
running.onStatus((status) => { log.info('template.status', { status }); });

const stop = () => {
  running.stop().finally(() => { process.exit(0); }).catch(() => undefined);
};
process.on('SIGINT', stop);
process.on('SIGTERM', stop);
```

#### `systems/template/shared/index.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The template system's contract (`shared/`): its name and everything it offers, with no implementation. Other systems
 * import it to call its fns through typed refs, its server implements it (`server/impl.ts`) and its client draws it
 * (`client/index.ts`). It must stay safe to import anywhere, the browser included: no Node imports here.
 *
 * TODO: say in one sentence what this system offers, here and in `description` below (and in `system.json`).
 *
 * The full guide is `packages/sdk/AUTHORING.md` (the `fs/system-authoring` context, `/new-system`, in the chat).
 */
import { system } from '@harness/sdk';

// Shapes used by more than one declaration go here, each written once with `g` and named for what it is:
//   export const Thing = g.obj({ ... });
// TODO: add shared shapes, or delete this comment.

/** The template system's contract. */
export const template = system('template', {
  description: 'TODO: what this system offers, in one sentence.',
  // about: what the chat model is told about it in its <systems> list, in place of the description: what it is for,
  // what sort of fns it has, its fn kinds and what authoring one gives the person, and when to reach for it. Write it
  // for a model that has never seen this app. TODO: write it, or delete this comment.
  // TODO: what it may do through the hub (HUB_CAPABILITIES); the example agent uses a model and reads a setting
  grants: { hub: ['ai', 'settings'] },
  // groups: its API groups, each `{ name, title, description }`; the hub indexes its fns by them (`<system>/api`,
  // `<system>/api.<group>`) so a model finds a fn by walking the groups. Each fn names its own with `group`.
  // TODO: declare groups once there are fns, or delete this comment.
})
  .with({
    // config: what the system needs before it can be used, as one gin object type (`g.obj({ ... })`). An admin sets
    // the value in the hub (it is checked against this type); every implementation reads it, typed, as `ctx.config`.
    // TODO: declare config, or delete this comment.

    // types: named types other systems can use as fields, each made with `type({ description, shape, display, methods })`.
    // A type's methods are native fns called on a value of it (`methods: { open: fn({ ... }) }`).
    // TODO: declare types, or delete this comment.

    // settings: typed per-actor controls an admin sets for users, groups or agents, each made with
    // `setting({ description, type, appliesTo, merge, editableBy })`. Runs read them with `ctx.setting(ref)`.
    // TODO: declare settings, or delete this comment.

    // attachments: kinds of data this system's fns attach to assistant messages, each made with
    // `attachment({ description, data })` and drawn by a renderer in `client/`.
    // TODO: declare attachments, or delete this comment.

    // kinds: fn kinds this system runs, each made with `kind({ description, applicability, payload, approveChanges, status })`.
    // Fns of a kind are declared with `kinds.<name>.fn({ ... })` in a later `.with`.
    // TODO: declare kinds, or delete this comment.

    // fns: native fns, each made with `fn({ description, args, returns, ... })`. Optional parts change what the server
    // must implement: `undo` (an undo implementation), `resume` (it can pause), `progress` (`linear` or `steps`),
    // `handlers` (`handler({ ... })`), `deps` (fn refs it may call), `state`, `form`, `status`, `display`, `tags`,
    // `group` (one of the system's `groups`).
    // Every fn needs `instructions` written for the model (when to use it, how, what it returns, pitfalls), at least one
    // example in `examples` (`{ args, result?, note? }`, checked against its types; a fn or agent with none fails the
    // build and `serve`) and `effects` (`{ mode, reads, writes, free }`).
    // TODO: declare fns, or delete this comment.

    // hooks: user-message hooks that add information before the LLM sees a message, each made with
    // `hook({ description, order, timeout })`.
    // TODO: declare hooks, or delete this comment.

    // providers: model providers whose calls run in this system, each made with `provider({ description, models })`.
    // An embedder is one: a model with `capabilities: ['embedding']` (or `imageEmbedding`) and its `dimensions` is an
    // embedder the hub's index can use.
    // TODO: declare providers, or delete this comment.

    // sectioners: ways of cutting resources into sections, each made with `sectioner({ description, match })`.
    // TODO: declare sectioners, or delete this comment.

    // actions: typed messages this system's renderers send it, each made with
    // `action({ description, input, returns, state, expires })`.
    // TODO: declare actions, or delete this comment.
  });

// Declarations that refer to what is declared above go in another `.with`, given a function of the refs so far:
//   .with(({ fns, types, context }) => ({

//     mentions: { ... },  // `mention({ title, description, target, of })`: `@` mention providers

// TODO: chain a `.with` like this onto `template` above, or delete this comment.
```

#### `systems/template/system.json`

```json
{
  "format": 1,
  "name": "template",
  "version": "0.1.0",
  "description": "TODO: what this system offers, in one sentence.",
  "protocol": 1,
  "server": {
    "entry": "server/main.ts",
    "external": {}
  },
  "client": {
    "entry": "client/index.ts"
  },
  "instances": "one"
}
```

#### `systems/template/tsconfig.json`

```json
{
  "extends": "../../tsconfig.base.json",
  "compilerOptions": { "rootDir": ".", "outDir": "../../.types/system-template", "tsBuildInfoFile": "../../.types/system-template.tsbuildinfo", "jsx": "preserve", "types": ["vite/client", "node"] },
  "include": ["shared", "server", "client/**/*.ts", "client/**/*.vue"],
  "references": [{ "path": "../../packages/core" }, { "path": "../../packages/sdk" }]
}
```

## 3. The contract

`system(name, { description })` starts a contract; `.with(part)` adds sections to it and returns a new contract, any
number of times. A part is an object of sections, or a **function of the refs declared so far** that returns one, so a
later part can use earlier declarations (an agent's tools, a context's fns, a mention's type, fns of a kind):

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
// declarations that use refs declared above go in a later `.with`, given a function of the refs so far
.with(({ fns, types, kinds }) => ({
  context: {
    guide: context({
      description: 'How to keep notes here: search before adding, short titles, lowercase tags.',
      content: 'Search with notes/find before adding a note, so you do not add one twice. Keep titles short and tags lowercase.',
      fns: [fns.find, fns.add],
      // for the graph contextor: how much it counts, a short form for tight budgets, and what it relates to
      weight: 1.2,
      summary: 'Search before adding; short titles; lowercase tags.',
      links: [{ to: types.Note, relation: 'about' }, { to: fns.tidy, relation: 'suggests', weight: 0.3 }],
    }),
    // no `content`: dynamic, so the server makes the text; the command is `/notes [tag]`
    recent: context({
      description: 'The newest notes, optionally with one tag.',
      command: { name: 'notes', args: { tag: arg('Only notes with this tag', { required: false }) } },
    }),
  },
  mentions: {
    // `@` in the chat finds notes; chips and cards show them as Note (its label, icon, color and its pin method)
    notes: mention({ title: 'Notes', description: 'Any note, by its title.', target: g.obj({ id: g.text() }), of: types.NoteEntry }),
  },
  fns: {
    // a fn of the stamp kind: only its payload; the kind's `describe` works out the rest
    inbox: kinds.stamp.fn({
      description: 'Capture a thought in the inbox.',
      payload: { title: 'Inbox', tags: ['inbox'] },
      args: StampArgs,
      returns: NoteEntry.shape,
      group: 'notes',
    }),
  },
}))
.with(({ fns, context: ctx }) => ({
  fns: {
    // an agent: a fn of the hub's `llm` kind with instructions, tools and context
    librarian: agent({
      description: 'Answers questions from the notes, quoting their titles.',
      args: g.obj({ question: g.text() }),
      returns: g.text(),
      instructions: 'Answer {{args.question}} from the notes. Quote the titles you used. Say so when no note covers it.',
      tools: [fns.find, hub.fns.ask_user],
      context: [ctx.guide],
    }),
    // calls other fns through its deps, by ref: its own system's `review` and the hub's `ask_user`
    publish: fn({
      description: 'Share a note once the person approves it.',
      group: 'sharing',
      args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
      returns: g.obj({ published: g.bool(), note: g.optional(g.text()) }),
      deps: { review: fns.review, ask: hub.fns.ask_user },
    }),
  },
}));
```

The sections, all optional:

| Section | Holds | Made with |
|---|---|---|
| `config` | what the system needs before it can be used (one gin object type; a later `config` replaces an earlier one) | `g.obj({ … })` |
| `types` | named types other systems can use as fields, with display and methods | `type()` |
| `settings` | typed per-actor controls admins set for users, groups or agents | `setting()` |
| `attachments` | kinds of data fns attach to assistant messages | `attachment()` |
| `kinds` | fn kinds the system runs | `kind()` |
| `fns` | native fns, agents, and fns of a kind | `fn()`, `agent()`, `kinds.<k>.fn()` |
| `context` | text for the LLM, static or made by the server, optionally a `/command` | `context()` |
| `hooks` | additions to each user message before the LLM sees it | `hook()` |
| `mentions` | `@` mention providers | `mention()` |
| `sectioners` | ways of cutting resources into sections | `sectioner()` |
| `providers` | model providers whose calls run in the system; their embedding models are the hub's embedders | `provider()` |
| `actions` | typed messages the system's renderers send it | `action()` |

The contract is a value with typed refs for everything in it: `notes.fns.add` is a `FnRef<'notes/add', Args, Result,
Handlers>`, `notes.types.Note` a `TypeRef`, `notes.context.recent` a `ContextRef` whose command args are typed. `notes.defs`
holds the declarations themselves; `SystemImpl<(typeof notes)['defs']>` is the implementation's type, and
`ConfigOf<(typeof notes)['defs']>` the config's. A name declared twice (in one part or across parts) throws when the
contract is built.

The whole notes contract is in **Worked examples**. Keep `shared/` free of Node imports: other systems and the browser import it.

**Registries, contributions and secrets.** `system(name, info)` also takes (`SystemInfo`): `registries`, the
registries this system hosts by local name (`RegistryDef`: a description and the gin type of the data each entry
draws); `contributes`, the entries this system adds to any registry (`{ registry: 'hub/visuals', name:
'map', label: 'Map' }`), whose components are in its client bundle; and `configSecrets`, the dot paths of its config's
secret text fields (`connection.password`), which the hub seals and only `configure` carries in plain. A secret path
that is not a text field of the config fails the manifest. The admin fns that carry a config (`hub/configure_system`,
`hub/add_instance`, installing or pairing a system) seal those fields in their args before the run is stored, so a run,
its approval and its words never hold the plain value. Pass secrets as plain values: a `{ $secret }` ref written by a
caller is refused (`SECRET_KEPT` keeps the stored one).

**Grants and what the hub enforces.** `system(name, info)` also takes `grants` (`SystemGrants`): what the system may
do through the hub, and on its machine when fs hosts it. The hub checks every call a system makes over the link
against them. A system that declares none gets the base only: the handshake, its manifest and patches, uploading
content, its own runs (progress, outcomes, handlers, control, logs), calling its own fns, `ctx.types` and its fleet.
Anything else needs a capability in `grants.hub`:

| What the system calls | Grant it needs |
|---|---|
| `ctx.llm` (`text`, `object`, `stream`, `image`, `transcribe`, `speak`), `ctx.ai()`, `hubAi()` | `hub: ['ai']`, and `models` to limit roles, models and spend (`perRun`, `perDay`) |
| `ctx.llm.embed` | `hub: ['embed']` (or `ai`) |
| `ctx.resources` (`stat`, `text`, `links`, `sections`, downloading content) | `hub: ['resources']` |
| `ctx.fns.<dep>(…)` where the dep is another system's fn or a hub fn (`hub.fns.ask_user`) | `hub: ['fns']` and the fn (`hub/ask_user`) or its system (`database`) in `calls` |
| `ctx.searchFns`, `ctx.fnExamples`, `running.examples` | `hub: ['search']` |
| `ctx.decide` | `hub: ['decide']` |
| `ctx.propose`, `running.grantPairing`, `running.reportRefusal` (hosting other systems) | `hub: ['approvals']` |
| handing back refreshed credentials of a `connect` system | `hub: ['connections']` |
| `ctx.setting(ref)` | `hub: ['settings']` |
| `ctx.state` (`get`, `set`, `update`, `clear`) | `hub: ['state']` |
| `ctx.data` (`find`, `get`, `table`, `save`, `remove`) | `hub: ['data']` |
| a hub secret by name | its name in `secrets` (its own config secrets are always its own) |
| a host on the network (any supervised system: hosted by fs or installed under the hub) | the host in `network` |
| a path outside its folders (hosted by fs only) | the path in `files` |

`network` holds for the whole process, not only for calls through the hub: each supervised process gets an allowlist
proxy of its own (`HTTP(S)_PROXY`) and preloads the SDK's socket guard, so a raw socket or a client with its own agent
reaches only the proxy and the hub. A refused host is the instance's refusal like any other, and a grant approved while
the system runs applies to its proxy at once. So declare every host the code (and every library it uses) connects to.

Beyond the grants, the hub enforces what each fn declares in `effects`: a fn declared `read` can't call or propose a
fn that writes or deletes (a hub fn tagged `own`, which changes only what the system owns, aside), a `write` fn can't
call one that deletes, and a run whose fn declares the types it reads and writes asks `ctx.types` for no others. The
notes system declares:

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
// notes are hub records (data), find keeps its last query (state), remove reads the editing setting (settings), and
// share asks the person through hub/ask_user (fns, and that fn in calls); its agent runs on the hub, so it needs no ai
grants: { hub: ['data', 'fns', 'settings', 'state'], calls: ['hub/ask_user'] },
```

A refused call throws in the system: the hub answers 403 with the code `NOT_GRANTED` and a line saying what was
missing (`it didn't declare the state capability, which stateSet needs`), and a fn that doesn't catch it fails with
that text. Every refusal is logged where the admin reviews the system, and a system that keeps going past its grants
(`HARNESS_REFUSALS_PAUSE` refusals in `HARNESS_REFUSALS_WINDOW`) has its fns switched off until an admin turns them back
on. So declare everything the code calls, and nothing more: the review page and the install card show the grants to
the admin, who goes live on them.

### Reference: the contract

#### `system`

function · `import { system } from '@harness/sdk'`

Start a contract. The name must be unique among the hub's systems: lowercase letters, digits and dashes.

```ts
function system<const N extends string>(name: N, info: SystemInfo): SystemContract<N, EmptyContract>;
```

#### `SystemContract`

interface · `import { SystemContract } from '@harness/sdk'`

A system's contract, in `shared/`: its name and everything it offers, with no implementation. Safe to import from
anywhere, the browser included.

```ts
export const fs = system('fs', { description: 'Files and a shell.' })
  .with({ types: { Entry }, fns: { read: fn({...}), grep: fn({...}) } })
  .with(({ fns }) => ({ context: { guide: context({ description: '…', content: '…', fns: [fns.read, fns.grep] }) } }));
```

```ts
interface SystemContract<N extends string, C extends ContractShape> extends SystemRefs<N, C> {
  readonly description: string;
  /** What the chat model is told about it in its `<systems>` list (`SystemInfo.about`); absent, its description. */
  readonly about: string | undefined;
  /** Registries it hosts, by local name. */
  readonly registries: Readonly<Record<string, RegistryDef>>;
  /** Entries it adds to registries, its own or another system's. */
  readonly contributes: readonly ContributionDecl[];
  /** The dot paths of its config's secret text fields. */
  readonly configSecrets: readonly ConfigPath[];
  /** What it may do through the hub (and, hosted, on its machine); `undefined` gets the base only. */
  readonly grants: SystemGrants | undefined;
  /** Its own config component (a client export), drawn instead of or beside the gin form. */
  readonly configComponent: ConfigComponentDecl | undefined;
  /** Whole pages it adds to the web app (`/s/<system>/<segment>`), each drawn by a component in `client({ pages })`. */
  readonly pages: readonly SystemPageDecl[];
  /** Its API groups, each described once; a fn names its group with `fn({ group })`. */
  readonly groups: readonly ApiGroupDecl[];
  /** The declarations themselves. */
  readonly defs: C;
  /** Add declarations: any sections, given directly or built from the refs so far. */
  with<const P extends Partial<ContractShape>>(part: P | ((self: SystemRefs<N, C>) => P)): SystemContract<N, Merge<C, P>>;
}
```

Refers to: [`ApiGroupDecl`](#core-apigroupdecl), [`ConfigComponentDecl`](#core-configcomponentdecl), [`ConfigPath`](#core-configpath), [`ContributionDecl`](#core-contributiondecl), [`SystemGrants`](#core-systemgrants), [`SystemPageDecl`](#core-systempagedecl).

#### `SystemRefs`

interface · `import { SystemRefs } from '@harness/sdk'`

The typed refs of everything in a contract.

```ts
interface SystemRefs<N extends string, C extends ContractShape> {
  readonly name: N;
  readonly types: { readonly [K in keyof C['types']]: C['types'][K] extends TypeDef<infer T, infer M> ? TypeRef<Q<N, K>, T, M> : never };
  readonly settings: { readonly [K in keyof C['settings']]: C['settings'][K] extends SettingDef<infer T> ? SettingRef<Q<N, K>, T> : never };
  readonly attachments: { readonly [K in keyof C['attachments']]: C['attachments'][K] extends AttachmentDef<infer T> ? AttachmentRef<Q<N, K>, T> : never };
  readonly kinds: { readonly [K in keyof C['kinds']]: C['kinds'][K] extends KindDef<infer P> ? KindRef<Q<N, K>, P> : never };
  /** Fn refs; an agent's (a `hub/llm` fn's) is an `AgentRef`, so it can be an injection's target. */
  readonly fns: { readonly [K in keyof C['fns']]: C['fns'][K] extends FnDef<infer T> ? FnRefOf<Q<N, K>, T> : never };
  readonly context: { readonly [K in keyof C['context']]: C['context'][K] extends ContextDef<infer A, boolean> ? ContextRef<Q<N, K>, A> : never };
  readonly hooks: { readonly [K in keyof C['hooks']]: HookRef<Q<N, K>> };
  readonly mentions: { readonly [K in keyof C['mentions']]: C['mentions'][K] extends MentionDef<infer T, unknown> ? MentionRef<Q<N, K>, T> : never };
  readonly sectioners: { readonly [K in keyof C['sectioners']]: SectionerRef<Q<N, K>> };
  readonly actions: { readonly [K in keyof C['actions']]: C['actions'][K] extends ActionDef<infer I, infer R, object> ? ActionRef<Q<N, K>, I, R> : never };
  readonly providers: { readonly [K in keyof C['providers']]: ProviderRef<Q<N, K>> };
}
```

#### `ContractShape`

interface · `import { ContractShape } from '@harness/sdk'`

Everything a contract can hold, by section.

```ts
interface ContractShape {
  /** What the system needs before it can be used; see `ConfigOf`. */
  config: GinType<object>;
  types: Record<string, AnyTypeDef>;
  settings: Record<string, SettingDef<unknown>>;
  attachments: Record<string, AttachmentDef<unknown>>;
  kinds: Record<string, KindDef<unknown>>;
  fns: Record<string, AnyFnDef>;
  context: Record<string, AnyContextDef>;
  hooks: Record<string, HookDef>;
  mentions: Record<string, MentionDef<unknown, unknown>>;
  sectioners: Record<string, SectionerDef>;
  actions: Record<string, ActionDef<unknown, unknown, object>>;
  /** Model providers it brings (`provider()`), served by its implementation. */
  providers: Record<string, ProviderDef>;
  /**
   * A per-person system's `connect` declaration (manifest `connect`): each person connects their own account and its
   * fns act as them. `undefined` for a system everyone shares. The implementation then serves `connect`.
   */
  connect: ConnectDecl | undefined;
}
```

Refers to: [`ConnectDecl`](#core-connectdecl).

#### `ContractSection`

type · `import { ContractSection } from '@harness/sdk'`

The sections of a contract that add named declarations (all but `config` and `connect`).

```ts
type ContractSection = Exclude<keyof ContractShape, 'config' | 'connect'>;
```

#### `CONTRACT_SECTIONS`

const · `import { CONTRACT_SECTIONS } from '@harness/sdk'`

The contract sections, in the order the manifest lists them.

```ts
const CONTRACT_SECTIONS: readonly ["types", "settings", "attachments", "kinds", "fns", "context", "hooks", "mentions", "sectioners", "actions", "providers"];
```

#### `isSystemContract`

function · `import { isSystemContract } from '@harness/sdk'`

Whether a value is a contract `system()` made (a module's exports, read by a build).

```ts
function isSystemContract(value: unknown): value is AnyContract;
```

#### `EmptyContract`

interface · `import { EmptyContract } from '@harness/sdk'`

A contract with nothing in it yet. Its config is the empty object.

```ts
interface EmptyContract {
  config: GinType<{}>;
  types: {}; settings: {}; attachments: {}; kinds: {}; fns: {}; context: {}; hooks: {}; mentions: {}; sectioners: {}; actions: {}; providers: {};
  connect: undefined;
}
```

#### `Merge`

type · `import { Merge } from '@harness/sdk'`

A contract with more sections merged in: `config` and `connect` are replaced, every other section adds to what was declared.

```ts
type Merge<C extends ContractShape, P extends Partial<ContractShape>> = {
  [K in keyof ContractShape]: K extends 'config'
    ? (P extends { config: infer X extends GinType<object> } ? X : C['config'])
    : K extends 'connect'
      ? (P extends { connect: infer X extends ConnectDecl } ? X : C['connect'])
      : Simplify<C[K] & (K extends keyof P ? NonNullable<P[K]> : {})>;
};
```

Refers to: [`ConnectDecl`](#core-connectdecl).

#### `AnyContract`

type · `import { AnyContract } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyContract = SystemContract<string, ContractShape>;
```

#### `ConfigOf`

type · `import { ConfigOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

A system's config type: what it needs before it can be used. The value is in every context as `ctx.config`.

```ts
type ConfigOf<C extends ContractShape> = Infer<C['config']>;
```

#### `SystemInfo`

interface · `import { SystemInfo } from '@harness/sdk'`

What `system()` takes besides its name.

```ts
interface SystemInfo {
  description: string;
  /**
   * What a chat model that has never seen this app is told about the system in its system prompt's `<systems>` list,
   * in place of the one-line `description`: what it is for, what sort of fns it has, whether it defines fn kinds and
   * what authoring one gives the person, and when to reach for it (and when not). A few plain sentences; the hub
   * shows it as written. Absent, the list shows `description`.
   */
  about?: string;
  /**
   * Registries this system hosts, by local name. Any system, this one included, adds entries from its client bundle
   * (`client({ visuals })`) and lists them in `contributes`.
   */
  registries?: Record<string, RegistryDef>;
  /** Entries this system adds to registries (`{ registry: 'hub/visuals', name: 'map', label: 'Map' }`). */
  contributes?: ContributionDecl[];
  /**
   * The dot paths of its config's secret text fields (`connection.password`). The hub seals them when the config is
   * saved and only `configure` carries the plain value. Each must be a text field of the config type.
   */
  configSecrets?: ConfigPath[];
  /**
   * What the system may do through the hub beyond the base: the hub capabilities it calls (`ai`, `embed`, `fns`,
   * …), model roles and spend, secrets, the fns it calls, and (when fs hosts it) the hosts and paths it reaches. The
   * hub enforces them over the link, logs every refusal, and shows them on the review page. Absent, the base only.
   */
  grants?: SystemGrants;
  /**
   * Its own config component: the name of a Vue component its client exports (`client({ components })`), drawn
   * `replace` (instead of the gin form) or `beside` it on the review, system and pairing pages. The hub still checks
   * the value with the config's gin type before saving.
   */
  configComponent?: ConfigComponentDecl;
  /**
   * Whole pages it adds to the web app, each at `/s/<system>/<segment>`: `{ segment: 'sessions', title: 'Claude', icon:
   * 'code', nav: 'main', access: 'everyone', component: 'SessionsPage' }`. The component is drawn from the client build
   * (`client({ pages: { SessionsPage } })`), which must have every one named. The nav shows a `main` page while the
   * system is live; a `hidden` one is reached by link (a detail page). Segments are unique.
   */
  pages?: SystemPageInput[];
  /**
   * Its API groups: a name, a title and what the group's fns are for (`{ name: 'files', title: 'Files', description:
   * 'Read, write and search files.' }`). The hub keeps an index item for the system (`<system>/api`, the groups as
   * choices) and one per group listing its fns, so a model walks the API like a context folder. A fn names its group
   * with `fn({ group })`; a fn with none is in the general group. Every group a fn names must be declared here.
   */
  groups?: ApiGroupDecl[];
}
```

Refers to: [`ApiGroupDecl`](#core-apigroupdecl), [`ConfigComponentDecl`](#core-configcomponentdecl), [`ConfigPath`](#core-configpath), [`ContributionDecl`](#core-contributiondecl), [`SystemGrants`](#core-systemgrants), [`SystemPageInput`](#core-systempageinput).

#### `RegistryDef`

interface · `import { RegistryDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A registry a system hosts: what it is for and the data each entry is drawn with (a `ResultTable` for query visuals).

```ts
interface RegistryDef {
  readonly description: string;
  readonly data: GinType;
}
```

## 4. Types with gin

Every shape in a contract is a **gin** type made with `g`, the SDK's gin registry (`import { g } from '@harness/sdk'`).
A gin type is a real runtime type (it parses and checks values, serializes to JSON for the hub, becomes the LLM's tool
schema and the chat's input form) **and** it carries the TypeScript type of its values: `Infer<typeof T>` is exact. So
write each shape once with `g` and infer the TypeScript type from it.

A fn's args reach the model as a **strict tool call** built from their gin types (through aeye), and the hub checks the
call against the same types, so the shape a model is told is the shape it is held to. Give every arg a real type: an
arg the model sees should never be any JSON unless nothing else fits.

| Builder | Values (`Infer`) | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `g.text({ minLength?, maxLength?, pattern? })` | `string` | |
| `g.num({ min?, max?, whole? })` | `number` | `whole: true` for integers |
| `g.bool()` | `boolean` | |
| `g.timestamp()` / `g.date()` | `Date` | travels as an ISO string, arrives as a `Date` |
| `g.duration()` | `number` | milliseconds |
| `g.list(item, { minLength?, maxLength? })` | `T[]` | |
| `g.obj({ key: type, … })` | `{ key: … }` | an `optional` field is an optional key |
| `g.optional(t)` | `T \| undefined` (key may be absent) | for "may be left out" |
| `g.nullable(t)` | `T \| null` | for "present but empty" |
| `g.enum(['a', 'b'])` | `'a' \| 'b'` | |
| `g.literal('x')` | `'x'` | |
| `g.or([a, b])` / `g.and([a, b])` | union / intersection | |
| `g.map(key, value)` | `Map<K, V>` | |
| `g.tuple([a, b])` | `[A, B]` | |
| `g.field(t, { docs, default })` | (a field of `obj`) | per-field docs the LLM and the form show |
| `json()` | `Json` | any JSON; the LLM and the form get no structure, so prefer a real type |
| `g.void()` | `void` | an action that returns nothing |

The full builder (`TypeBuilder`) and its options are in this section's reference. Named types are fields too: a type a
contract declares (`notes.types.Note`, `hub.types.ContentRef`) is a gin type registered under its qualified name, so
`g.obj({ note: notes.types.Note })` works and serializes by name. `hub.types.LongText` is text whose input is a growing
text area: use it for bodies and plans.

Shapes used in more than one place are constants in `shared/`, named for what they are, with their value types
inferred beside them:

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
/** What undo needs to put a note back: its record id and the note as it was, or null when there was none. */
export const Before = g.obj({ id: g.text(), note: g.nullable(Note.shape) });

/** The search `find` ran last in this session, kept in session state so a later `find` can repeat it. */
export const LastSearch = g.obj({ lastQuery: g.text() });

/** A person's answer to `review`: the form its renderer submits and the fn's result. */
export const Review = g.obj({ approved: g.bool(), note: g.optional(g.text()) });

/** The payload of a `notes/stamp` fn: the title and tags every note it adds gets. */
export const Stamp = g.obj({ title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }), tags: g.list(g.text()) });

/** The args of every `notes/stamp` fn: the note's text. */
export const StampArgs = g.obj({ text: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) });
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
/** The notes contract's type. */
export type NotesContract = typeof notes;
/** A note, as it is stored. */
export type NoteValue = ValueOf<typeof Note>;
/** A saved note, as fns return it. */
export type NoteEntryValue = ValueOf<typeof NoteEntry>;
/** What undo needs to put a note back. */
export type BeforeValue = Infer<typeof Before>;
/** A person's answer to `review`. */
export type ReviewValue = Infer<typeof Review>;
/** A stamp fn's payload. */
export type StampValue = Infer<typeof Stamp>;
```

Doc a field the LLM must fill in carefully with `g.field(type, { docs })`:

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
// what the system needs before it can be used; an admin sets it in the hub, and `ctx.config` has it, typed
config: g.obj({
  defaultTags: g.field(g.list(g.text()), { docs: 'Tags every new note gets.' }),
}),
```

**Values at runtime.** Fns receive values already parsed by their gin types, in plain JS form (timestamps as `Date`).
To check a value yourself use `parseValue(type, value)` (throws a gin error naming the problem); to turn a value into
JSON for storage use `encodeValue(type, value)` (dates as ISO strings). Read a file the system keeps with its own type,
so a file that doesn't fit is refused instead of read wrongly: `parseValue(g.list(Entry), JSON.parse(text))` to read,
`JSON.stringify(encodeValue(g.list(Entry), list))`
to write.

To read values of another system's named type at runtime, ask the hub for its definition
(`ctx.types.definitions([name])`) and parse it with `loadTypes`. `ginOf(zodSchema)` makes a gin type from a zod
schema (the hub uses it to mirror core schemas): core's `GinExprDef` becomes gin's `fn` (an expression), and
`GinTypeDef` / `GinObjTypeDef` become gin's `typ` (a gin type, whose tool schema is gin's strict TypeDef schema), never
any JSON. A system's own shapes are written with `g` directly.

**Gotchas.** `g.optional` makes the key optional; with `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, build objects with conditional
spreads (`...(x === undefined ? {} : { x })`) rather than `x: undefined` where a type says `x?: T`. A `g.timestamp()`
field is a `Date` in code but a string on the wire. Never hand-write a TypeScript type that mirrors a gin type.

### Reference: types with gin

#### `g`

const · `import { g } from '@harness/sdk'`

The gin type builder: gin's registry, shared by every contract in this process, with `fnName` and `record` (`HarnessGin`).

```ts
const g: Registry & HarnessGin;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `GinType`

type · `import { GinType } from '@harness/sdk'`

A gin type whose values are `T`.

```ts
type GinType<T = unknown> = Type<T>;
```

#### `Infer`

type · `import { Infer } from '@harness/sdk'`

The logical TypeScript value of a gin type: `Infer<ListType<string>>` is `string[]`.

```ts
type Infer<X> = X extends Type<any, any> & {
    readonly [K in ValueSlot]: infer T;
} ? T : never;
```

#### `json`

function · `import { json } from '@harness/sdk'`

Any JSON. The LLM and the input form get no structure from it, so prefer a real type.

```ts
function json(): GinType<Json>;
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json).

#### `parseValue`

function · `import { parseValue } from '@harness/sdk'`

Check a value against a gin type and return its plain JS form (what fns receive: dates as `Date`). Throws a
gin error naming the problem when the value doesn't fit.

```ts
function parseValue<T>(type: GinType<T>, value: unknown): T;
```

#### `encodeValue`

function · `import { encodeValue } from '@harness/sdk'`

A checked value as JSON for the wire (dates as ISO strings, absent optional fields left out).

```ts
function encodeValue<T>(type: GinType<T>, value: T): Json;
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json).

#### `wireType`

function · `import { wireType } from '@harness/sdk'`

A gin type's JSON definition, as it travels to the hub.

```ts
function wireType(type: GinType): GinTypeDef;
```

Refers to: [`GinTypeDef`](#core-gintypedef).

#### `referencesOf`

function · `import { referencesOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The named types a gin type refers to directly (gin `typeReferences`), so a receiver can load them first. Every named
type in `g` is registered under its qualified name, so a reference that isn't one throws. The hub works these out
for itself from the wire definition (`TypeRecord.references`); this is for code holding a live type.

```ts
function referencesOf(type: GinType): QualifiedName[];
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `loadTypes`

function · `import { loadTypes } from '@harness/sdk'`

Parse the hub's type definitions as gin types. The hub sends them dependencies first, so each is bound in a gin scope
overlay on `g` before anything that refers to it; nothing is registered process-wide. Throws a gin error when a
definition is invalid.

```ts
function loadTypes(definitions: TypeDefinitions): LoadedTypes;
```

Refers to: [`TypeDefinitions`](#core-typedefinitions).

#### `LoadedTypes`

interface · `import { LoadedTypes } from '@harness/sdk'`

Named types loaded from the hub's definitions (`ctx.types.definitions`), as live gin types.

```ts
interface LoadedTypes {
  /** The gin type of a named type that loaded, ready to parse and encode values; `undefined` when it didn't. */
  get(name: QualifiedName): GinType | undefined;
  /** The names asked for (or referred to) that the hub doesn't know. */
  readonly missing: readonly QualifiedName[];
}
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `ginOf`

function · `import { ginOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The input-side gin type of a zod schema; throws for a schema with no gin counterpart (a function, a promise).

```ts
function ginOf(schema: z.core.$ZodType): GinType;
```

#### `ginAs`

function · `import { ginAs } from '@harness/sdk'`

`ginOf`, typed by the schema's input: the gin type is built from the schema's input side, so authors write
`z.input<S>` values into it. Records, `unknown` and lazy parts are any JSON in gin, so a reader parses the value with
the schema itself before trusting it (which also fills in defaults). Use it where a gin field must carry a core
schema's type, pinned with an `Equal` test.

```ts
function ginAs<S extends z.ZodType>(schema: S): GinType<z.input<S>>;
```

#### `toolArgsOf`

function · `import { toolArgsOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn's args read off a zod schema. Tool args are always an object: an object schema is taken as it is, and a union
of objects (an import from a URL or from an upload, one provider of several) is flattened into one object with
every member's fields, so the model passes plain args either way; the schema itself picks the member when `read`
parses them. Any other schema is taken as the field `input`. Fields only a person sets (core's `peopleOnly`, such as
a memory's `inject`) are left out of the args, and a field with a narrower model form (`forModels`) takes it, so a
model is never shown them and the hub refuses them as unknown args; `read` parses with the whole schema, which the web endpoint's body keeps.

```ts
function toolArgsOf<S extends z.ZodType>(schema: S): { readonly args: GinType<ToolArgs<S>>; readonly read: (args: unknown) => z.output<S>; };
```

#### `ToolArgs`

type · `import { ToolArgs } from '@harness/sdk'`

The args a tool for a zod schema takes: the schema's input when it is an object, a union of objects flattened into
one, else that input as `input`.

```ts
type ToolArgs<S extends z.ZodType> = S extends z.ZodObject ? z.input<S> : S extends z.ZodUnion<readonly z.ZodObject[]> ? FlatArgs<z.input<S>> : { input: z.input<S> };
```

#### `FlatArgs`

type · `import { FlatArgs } from '@harness/sdk'`

A union of objects as one object: every member's fields, each one optional (the schema checks the member on read).

```ts
type FlatArgs<U> = { [K in KeysOf<U>]?: FieldOf<U, K> };
```

#### `sampleOf`

function · `import { sampleOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

A sample value of a wire type, for an example call a kind works out from a payload's args (`KindDescription.examples`):
every required field filled (text with its field's name, a number in range, the first enum value or union option, one
list item), optional fields left out. A shape it can't fill by itself (a named type) gets gin's own zero value
(`create`), which its type accepts. A patterned text is best effort, as in gin: no value is synthesized from a pattern.

```ts
function sampleOf(def: GinTypeDef, hint?: string): Json;
```

Refers to: [`GinTypeDef`](#core-gintypedef), [`Json`](#core-json).

#### `sampleArgs`

function · `import { sampleArgs } from '@harness/sdk'`

Sample args for a fn's args type (an `obj`, filled by `sampleOf`); open args (`any`) or any other shape give `{}`.

```ts
function sampleArgs(args: GinTypeDef): Record<string, Json>;
```

Refers to: [`GinTypeDef`](#core-gintypedef), [`Json`](#core-json).

#### `TypeBuilder`

interface · `import { TypeBuilder } from '@aeye/gin'`

TypeBuilder — factory surface for constructing runtime Type instances.

The Registry implements this. Code that builds types (including other
Type classes building their sub-types in props/get/call/init) takes a
TypeBuilder so it never imports concrete type classes directly. No
circular dependencies, no hardcoded type-name inspection.

Every builder returns its concrete class carrying the TypeScript value it
describes, so `Infer<typeof t>` is exact — see `infer.ts`:

```ts
const Read = r.obj({ path: r.text(), offset: r.optional(r.num()) });
type Read = Infer<typeof Read>;   // { path: string; offset?: number | undefined }
```

```ts
interface TypeBuilder {
    any(): AnyType;
    void(): VoidType;
    null(): NullType;
    bool(options?: BoolOptions): BoolType;
    num(options?: NumOptions): NumType;
    text(options?: TextOptions): TextType;
    list<V>(item: Type<V>, options?: ListOptions): ListType<V>;
    map<K, V>(key: Type<K>, value: Type<V>): MapType<K, V>;
    tuple<const E extends readonly Type[]>(elements: E): TupleType<TupleValue<E>>;
    /** An object over `props`; a field typed `optional<T>` is an optional key. */
    obj<const P extends FieldsInput>(props: P): ObjType<ObjValue<P>>;
    /** A field with docs and/or a default, keeping its exact type for inference. */
    field<X extends Type>(type: X, options?: FieldOptions): FieldSpec<X>;
    optional<T>(inner: Type<T>): OptionalType<T>;
    nullable<T>(inner: Type<T>): NullableType<T>;
    not(excluded: Type): NotType;
    or<const V extends readonly Type[]>(variants: V): OrType<Infer<V[number]>>;
    and<const V extends readonly Type[]>(parts: V): AndType<IntersectValues<V>>;
    /** Named constants of `value`'s type: `enum({ low: 'low', high: 'high' }, r.text())`. */
    enum<const E extends Record<string, B>, B>(values: E, value: Type<B>): EnumType<E[keyof E], B>;
    /** Text constants whose labels are their values: `enum(['low', 'high'])`. */
    enum<const L extends readonly string[]>(labels: L): EnumType<L[number], string>;
    /** A type whose only valid value is `value` of type `inner`. */
    literal<const T extends B, B>(inner: Type<B>, value: T): LiteralType<T, B>;
    /** A literal of the matching primitive type: `literal('ok')`, `literal(3)`, `literal(true)`. */
    literal<const T extends string | number | boolean>(value: T): LiteralType<T, Widen<T>>;
    date(options?: DateOptions): DateType;
    timestamp(options?: TimestampOptions): TimestampType;
    duration(): DurationType;
    color(options?: ColorOptions): ColorType;
    /** A fn type: NOTE a single options object, not positional args. */
    fn<A extends object, R = void, E = never>(opts: FnSpec<A, R, E>): FnType<A, R, E>;
    iface<const P extends FieldsInput = {}>(spec: IfaceSpec<P>): IfaceType<ObjValue<DataFields<P>>>;
    /** A reference by name, resolved at use time — `T` is the value it stands for. */
    alias<T = unknown>(name: string): AliasType<T>;
    /** "A type that satisfies `constraint`" — its values are types. */
    typ<T>(constraint: Type<T>): TypType<T>;
    /** A named subtype of `base`; stored-data props it adds extend the value. */
    extend<T, O, const P extends FieldsInput = {}>(base: Type<T, O>, local: ExtendLocal<T, O, P>): Extension<WithData<T, P>, O>;
    /** A named subtype of the type registered or built in under the name `base`. */
    extend<const N extends string, const P extends FieldsInput = {}>(base: N, local: ExtendLocal<NamedValue<N>, any, P>): Extension<WithData<NamedValue<N>, P>>;
    parse(json: unknown): Type;
    /** Field-style Prop: its type is whatever `type` resolves to, and access
     *  triggers the named native. Use for things like `length: num`. */
    prop<T>(type: Type<T>, nativeId: string, docs?: string): ComputedProp<T>;
    /** Method-style Prop: its type is a Fn(args) → returns, and when called
     *  the named native is invoked. Use for things like `eq`, `add`, `map`.
     *  `options.generic` declares method-level type parameters (e.g. `map<R>`);
     *  the values are the constraint Type (use `any` for unconstrained). */
    method<const A extends Record<string, Type>, R>(args: A, returns: Type<R>, nativeId: string, options?: {
        docs?: string;
        generic?: Record<string, Type>;
    }): ComputedProp<FnValue<ObjValue<A>, R>>;
}
```

#### `BoolOptions`

interface · `import { BoolOptions } from '@aeye/gin'`

BoolType options.

```ts
interface BoolOptions {
    /** Custom text for true when serializing (e.g. "yes"). */
    trueText?: string;
    /** Custom text for false when serializing. */
    falseText?: string;
}
```

#### `NumOptions`

interface · `import { NumOptions } from '@aeye/gin'`

NumType options.

```ts
interface NumOptions {
    min?: number;
    max?: number;
    whole?: boolean;
    minPrecision?: number;
    maxPrecision?: number;
    prefix?: string;
    suffix?: string;
}
```

#### `TextOptions`

interface · `import { TextOptions } from '@aeye/gin'`

TextType options.

```ts
interface TextOptions {
    minLength?: number;
    maxLength?: number;
    /** Regex source (no leading/trailing slashes). */
    pattern?: string;
    /** Regex flags accompanying pattern. */
    flags?: string;
}
```

#### `ListOptions`

interface · `import { ListOptions } from '@aeye/gin'`

ListType options (item type lives in generic.V).

```ts
interface ListOptions {
    minLength?: number;
    maxLength?: number;
}
```

#### `DateOptions`

interface · `import { DateOptions } from '@aeye/gin'`

DateType options.

```ts
interface DateOptions {
    min?: string;
    max?: string;
    /** Interpret parsed dates as UTC. */
    utc?: boolean;
}
```

#### `TimestampOptions`

interface · `import { TimestampOptions } from '@aeye/gin'`

TimestampType options. Extends DateOptions with time precision.

```ts
interface TimestampOptions extends DateOptions {
    /** 'ms' (default), 's', 'us'. */
    precision?: 'ms' | 's' | 'us';
}
```

#### `ColorOptions`

interface · `import { ColorOptions } from '@aeye/gin'`

ColorType options.

```ts
interface ColorOptions {
    hasAlpha?: boolean;
}
```

#### `FieldOptions`

interface · `import { FieldOptions } from '@aeye/gin'`

What {@link TypeBuilder.field} adds to a field beyond its type.

```ts
interface FieldOptions {
    /** Shown to authors and models wherever the field is described. */
    docs?: string;
    /** The value a `new` fills in when its payload omits the field. */
    default?: Expr | ExprDef;
}
```

## 5. Named types and methods

`type({ description, shape, display, methods? })` declares a **named type**: a shape with a name (`notes/Note`), shown to
people in a consistent way, that other systems can use as a field and that `@` mentions can point at.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
/**
 * A note: a titled text with tags. A stored type (`records`): the hub keeps the notes, one record each, checks them
 * against this shape, versions every change and searches them, by words in the title and text and by meaning. Notes
 * are shared by everyone who can see the type (`owner: 'shared'`), so hooks and mentions read them too; a type whose
 * records are each one person's (`owner: 'person'`) is read in runs, as that person. Its record carries its id,
 * version and times; the value doesn't.
 */
export const Note = type({
  description: 'A note: a titled text with tags.',
  shape: g.obj({
    title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    text: hub.types.LongText,
    tags: g.list(g.text()),
    pinned: g.bool(),
  }),
  display: { label: 'Note', plural: 'Notes', icon: 'note', color: '#d9a400', title: 'title' },
  records: { shape: 'table', owner: 'shared', search: { text: ['title', 'text'], embed: true } },
});

/** A note as fns return it and cards show it: the stored note with its record id and when it last changed. */
export const NoteEntry = type({
  description: 'A saved note: its record id, when it last changed, and the note.',
  shape: g.obj({
    id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    text: hub.types.LongText,
    tags: g.list(g.text()),
    pinned: g.bool(),
    updatedAt: g.timestamp(),
  }),
  // chips and cards name a note by its title, with its id under it
  display: { label: 'Note', plural: 'Notes', icon: 'note', color: '#d9a400', title: 'title', subtitle: 'id' },
  methods: {
    // offered on a note's mention card; people and code can call it, the LLM can't (`visibility: 'user'`)
    pin: fn({ description: 'Pin or unpin this note.', args: g.obj({ pinned: g.bool() }), returns: g.bool(), visibility: 'user' }),
  },
});
```

- **`shape`** is any gin type; `ValueOf<typeof Note>` (or `Infer<typeof Note.shape>`) is its value type.
- **`display`**: `label` and `plural` name the type for people, `icon` and `color` mark chips and cards, and `title` and
  `subtitle` name the fields that name a value. `title` and `subtitle` must be text or number fields of the shape; a
  name that isn't one doesn't compile.
- **`methods`** are native fns called on a value of the type. Declare each with `fn()`; its qualified name is
  `notes/Note.pin`, and its args are the value as `self` plus its own args (`{ self: Note, pinned: boolean }`). A person
  can run a method from a mention's card (when the mention provider declares `of: types.Note`), code calls it through
  `notes.types.Note.methods.pin` as a dep, and the LLM sees it only when its `visibility` says so (`'user'` hides it).

The server implements every method under `types`, getting the value as `self`:

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
types: {
  NoteEntry: {
    pin: {
      async run(self, args, ctx) {
        const note = await noteById(ctx.data, self.id);
        if (note === undefined) { return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'self', message: `Note ${self.id} no longer exists.` }]); }
        await ctx.data.save(Note, { ...note.value, pinned: args.pinned }, { id: note.id });
        return ctx.done(args.pinned);
      },
    },
  },
},
```

Use a named type for things with identity that people point at or other systems take as input (a file entry, a row, a
ticket). For a one-off shape, a plain `g.obj` constant is enough.

### Reference: named types and methods

#### `type`

function · `import { type } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a named type. Methods are native fns called on a value of the type: a person can run them from a mention's
card, and code can call them through the type's ref. They are offered to the LLM only when their `visibility`
says so.

With `records`, the type is stored: the hub keeps its records, checks each against the type, versions every change
and searches them in its database (`ctx.data.find`, the Data page, `hub/records_*`). Shared data lives there, never
in a system's own folder.

```ts
function type<T, const M extends Methods = {}>(def: { description: string; shape: GinType<T>; display: Omit<TypeDisplay, "title" | "subtitle"> & { title?: NamingKeys<T>; subtitle?: NamingKeys<T>; }; methods?: M; records?: RecordsInput<T>; }): TypeDef<T, M>;
```

Refers to: [`TypeDisplay`](#core-typedisplay).

#### `TypeDef`

interface · `import { TypeDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A named type in a contract: its shape, how it is shown, and its methods.

```ts
interface TypeDef<T, M extends Methods> {
  readonly description: string;
  readonly shape: GinType<T>;
  readonly display: TypeDisplay;
  readonly methods: M;
  /** Set when the hub keeps records of the type (the data system): read and write them with `ctx.data`. */
  readonly records: RecordStorage | undefined;
}
```

Refers to: [`RecordStorage`](#core-recordstorage), [`TypeDisplay`](#core-typedisplay).

#### `AnyTypeDef`

type · `import { AnyTypeDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyTypeDef = TypeDef<unknown, Methods>;
```

#### `ValueOf`

type · `import { ValueOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The value type of a declared type.

```ts
type ValueOf<D> = D extends TypeDef<infer T, Methods> ? T : never;
```

#### `NamingKeys`

type · `import { NamingKeys } from '@harness/sdk'`

Fields of T that can name a value (text or number fields).

```ts
type NamingKeys<T> = { [K in keyof T]-?: T[K] extends string | number | undefined ? K : never }[keyof T] & string;
```

#### `TypeRef`

type · `import { TypeRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A typed handle to a named type (`fs.types.Entry`). It is a real gin type itself (a named gin extension, registered
in `g`), so other types can use it as a field: `g.obj({ entry: fs.types.Entry })`. Its methods are typed fn refs
that code calls with the value as `self`.

```ts
type TypeRef<N extends QualifiedName, T, M extends Methods = {}> = Extension<T> & {
  readonly ref: 'type';
  readonly name: N;
  readonly display: TypeDisplay;
  readonly description: string;
  readonly methods: MethodRefs<N, T, M>;
};
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname), [`TypeDisplay`](#core-typedisplay).

#### `AnyTypeRef`

type · `import { AnyTypeRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyTypeRef = TypeRef<QualifiedName, unknown, Methods>;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `Methods`

type · `import { Methods } from '@harness/sdk'`

A type's methods by name. Each is declared with `fn()` and implemented with the value it is called on.

```ts
type Methods = Record<string, FnDef<FnTypes>>;
```

#### `MethodName`

type · `import { MethodName } from '@harness/sdk'`

A method's qualified name: `fs/Entry.reveal`.

```ts
type MethodName<N extends QualifiedName, K> = N extends `${infer S}/${infer L}` ? QualifiedName<S, `${L}.${K & string}`> : never;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `MethodArgs`

type · `import { MethodArgs } from '@harness/sdk'`

The args of a method call: the value it is called on, as `self`, plus the method's own args.

```ts
type MethodArgs<T, A> = Simplify<{ self: T } & A>;
```

#### `MethodRefs`

type · `import { MethodRefs } from '@harness/sdk'`

The fn refs of a type's methods.

```ts
type MethodRefs<N extends QualifiedName, T, M extends Methods> = {
  readonly [K in keyof M]: M[K] extends FnDef<infer F> ? FnRef<MethodName<N, K>, MethodArgs<T, F['args']>, F['result'], F['handlers']> : never;
};
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `MethodImpl`

type · `import { MethodImpl } from '@harness/sdk'`

A method's implementation: a native fn that also gets the value it was called on.

```ts
type MethodImpl<V, T extends FnTypes, Cfg = unknown> =
{ run(self: V, args: T['args'], ctx: FnCtx<T, Cfg>): Promise<Outcome<T>> } &
([T['undo']] extends [never] ? { undo?: never } : { undo(payload: T['undo'], ctx: UndoCtx<Cfg>): Promise<UndoOutcome> });
```

Refers to: [`UndoOutcome`](#core-undooutcome).

## 6. Stored types and the data system

**Where data lives.** Data a system keeps for people (notes, tasks, contacts, anything shared or looked up later)
lives in the hub as **records of a stored type**, never in a file or database of the system's own. The hub keeps them,
checks each against the type, versions every change, controls who sees them, searches them in its database (by words
and by meaning) and shows them on the Data page, where people browse, filter, edit and turn them into widgets. A
system's own folder (`ctx.dataDir`) holds only what is the system's alone: its keys, a cache, a working copy of
something it syncs. An external database stays where it is (the database system reads it).

A type is **stored** when it declares `records`:

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
/**
 * A note: a titled text with tags. A stored type (`records`): the hub keeps the notes, one record each, checks them
 * against this shape, versions every change and searches them, by words in the title and text and by meaning. Notes
 * are shared by everyone who can see the type (`owner: 'shared'`), so hooks and mentions read them too; a type whose
 * records are each one person's (`owner: 'person'`) is read in runs, as that person. Its record carries its id,
 * version and times; the value doesn't.
 */
export const Note = type({
  description: 'A note: a titled text with tags.',
  shape: g.obj({
    title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    text: hub.types.LongText,
    tags: g.list(g.text()),
    pinned: g.bool(),
  }),
  display: { label: 'Note', plural: 'Notes', icon: 'note', color: '#d9a400', title: 'title' },
  records: { shape: 'table', owner: 'shared', search: { text: ['title', 'text'], embed: true } },
});

/** A note as fns return it and cards show it: the stored note with its record id and when it last changed. */
export const NoteEntry = type({
  description: 'A saved note: its record id, when it last changed, and the note.',
  shape: g.obj({
    id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    text: hub.types.LongText,
    tags: g.list(g.text()),
    pinned: g.bool(),
    updatedAt: g.timestamp(),
  }),
  // chips and cards name a note by its title, with its id under it
  display: { label: 'Note', plural: 'Notes', icon: 'note', color: '#d9a400', title: 'title', subtitle: 'id' },
  methods: {
    // offered on a note's mention card; people and code can call it, the LLM can't (`visibility: 'user'`)
    pin: fn({ description: 'Pin or unpin this note.', args: g.obj({ pinned: g.bool() }), returns: g.bool(), visibility: 'user' }),
  },
});
```

- `shape`: `table` for many records (the usual), `value` for exactly one (a system's shared list or settings). A save
  to a `value` type leaves out `id`.
- `owner`: `shared` (everyone who can see the type sees every record) or `person` (each record its writer's; only they
  and admins see it). A `person` type is read and written in runs, as the run's person; hooks, mentions, context and
  lifecycle code have no person, so they can't read it.
- `search.text`: the top-level text fields text search matches, in order of weight; `search.embed`: whether records
  are embedded too, so search finds them by meaning (a model call the hub makes and pays for).

The record carries the id (`rec_…`), version, times and who changed it; the value is only the type's fields. A type
that cards and methods need with its id (the notes' `NoteEntry`) is a separate named type the fns return.

**Reading and writing: `ctx.data`.** Every context has it (it needs `grants: { hub: ['data'] }`). In a run it acts as
the run's person, elsewhere as the system. Every read is a query with a page: filters and sorts on the type's
top-level fields (`ListQuery`: `filters`, `sort`, `limit`, `cursor`) and `search`; the hub runs them in its database.
**Never load every record to filter, count or search in code.**

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
find: {
  async run(args, ctx) {
    // `find` declared `state: LastSearch`, so it sees `lastQuery` and nothing else
    const query = args.query ?? (await ctx.state.get()).lastQuery;
    if (query === undefined && args.tag === undefined) {
      // the caller's mistake: an agent gets the problem back and tries again
      return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'query', message: 'Give a query or a tag; there is no earlier search to repeat.' }]);
    }
    ctx.progress.set(0.1, 'Searching');
    // the hub searches by words and meaning and filters by tag in its database; one page comes back
    const limit = args.limit ?? s.NOTES_FIND_LIMIT;
    const page = await ctx.data.find(Note, {
      ...(query === undefined ? {} : { search: query }),
      query: { filters: tagFilter(args.tag), limit },
    });
    if (query !== undefined) { await ctx.state.set({ lastQuery: query }); }
    return ctx.done({ notes: page.items.map(entryOf), more: page.cursor !== undefined });
  },
},
```

- `ctx.data.find(Type, { search?, query? })`: one page, best match first when searching; `cursor` when there are more.
- `ctx.data.get(Type, id)`: one record, `undefined` when it is gone or not the asker's.
- `ctx.data.save(Type, value, { id?, expect? })`: add (no `id`) or replace. With `expect` (the version read), the save
  fails when someone changed the record meanwhile, instead of losing their change.
- `ctx.data.remove(Type, id)`: remove it; its versions stay.
- `ctx.data.table(Type, { search?, query?, offset?, limit? })`: the same records as a `ResultTable` (a column per field,
  then `id` referring to the type), which widgets draw.

Work over many records a page at a time, following `cursor` with a stable sort, as `tidy` does:

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
tidy: {
  async run(_args, ctx) {
    // a resumed run starts at the page it paused on
    let saved = ctx.resume?.state.saved ?? 0;
    let cursor = ctx.resume?.state.cursor;
    // asked to pause: say where to resume from; the run settles as paused and `ctx.signal` aborts
    ctx.onPause(() => ({ saved, ...(cursor === undefined ? {} : { cursor }) }));
    ctx.progress.step('scan', 'active');
    let merged = 0;
    // a page at a time, in a stable order: every note is seen once and never all held at once
    do {
```
