Refers to: [`RunStatus`](#core-runstatus).

## 11. Kinds and agents

### Kinds

A **kind** decides what a fn's definition looks like and how it runs. A native fn's author declares everything because
the code is the definition; a fn of a kind carries only a **payload** (the kind's definition data), and the kind works
out the rest from it. Declare a kind with `kind({ description, applicability, payload, approveChanges?, status? })`:
`applicability` tells an agent when to pick this kind for a new fn, `payload` is its gin type, `approveChanges`
(default `true`) makes declaring or changing a fn of the kind at runtime wait for approval, and `status` gives the
words of its fns that declare none (they may use `{fn}`, `{n}`, `{args.…}` and `{output.…}`).

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
kinds: {
  stamp: kind({
    description: 'A fn that adds a note with a fixed title and tags; its args are the text.',
    applicability: 'Use for quick capture: a journal, an inbox, a standing list.',
    payload: Stamp,
    // declaring or changing a stamp fn at runtime needs no approval: it can only add notes
    approveChanges: false,
  }),
},
```

The server implements each kind with three functions, and fns of the kind are **not** implemented one by one:

- `check(payload, ctx)`: problems beyond the payload's type (an unknown fn, a program that doesn't compile), as
  sentences; `[]` when it is fine.
- `describe(payload, ctx)`: everything else about a fn with this payload, as a `KindDescription`: its `args` and
  `returns` (gin definitions, made with `wireType(type)`), the fns it calls (`deps`), the named types and resources it
  refers to, its operations, at least one `examples` entry (`sampleArgs(def)` fills one from the args type), and optionally handlers, progress, status words, limits and display. `ctx.type(name)` and
  `ctx.fn(name)` look up other declarations while describing.
- `run(payload, args, ctx)`: run one fn of the kind. The args arrive as JSON the hub checked against `describe`'s args;
  read them with the same gin type (`parseValue`). The result is JSON (`encodeValue`). `ctx.call(name, args)` calls the
  deps `describe` returned, by name; `ctx.handler(name, args)` asks a handler; `ctx.progress(...)` reports either shape.
- `undo(payload, undo, ctx)`, optional, for kinds whose fns are undoable.

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
kinds: {
  stamp: {
    // problems beyond the payload's type
    check: (payload) => Promise.resolve(payload.tags.filter((tag) => tag !== tag.toLowerCase()).map((tag) => `Tag "${tag}" must be lowercase.`)),
    // everything else about a stamp fn, worked out from its payload
    describe: (payload) => Promise.resolve({
      args: wireType(StampArgs),
      returns: wireType(NoteEntry.shape),
      deps: [],
      operations: { cancel: true, pause: false, undo: false, background: true },
      // every fn needs an example; a kind works its fns' examples out from the payload
      examples: [{ args: { text: 'Shipped the release' }, note: `Saves a note titled "${payload.title}: Shipped the release".` }],
    }),
    async run(payload, args, ctx) {
      // a kind's args arrive as JSON the hub checked against `describe`; read them with the same gin type
      const input = parseValue(StampArgs, args);
      // titles are unique, so a stamp's note is titled by the stamp and the start of its text
      const title = `${payload.title}: ${input.text.slice(0, 40)}`;
      const note = await create(ctx.data, ctx.config, { title, text: input.text, tags: payload.tags });
      if (note === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', taken(title), { code: 'taken' }); }
      return ctx.done(encodeValue(NoteEntry.shape, note));
    },
  },
},
```

Fns of a kind are declared with the kind's ref, `kinds.<kind>.fn({ description, payload, args?, returns?, … })`, by
its own system or by any other. Give `args` and `returns` to type the ref; the hub checks them against what `describe`
derives. Without them the ref takes and returns `Json`. The fn runs on the system that defines its kind.

### Agents

An **agent** is a fn of the hub's `llm` kind: an LLM with instructions, a model, tools and context, callable like any
fn. `agent({ description, args, returns, instructions, … })` declares one; any system can ship its own (a reviewer, a
planner) with tools from any system.

- `instructions` is a Handlebars template; `{{args.question}}` reads the args.
- `returns: g.text()` gives a chat-style answer; any other type asks the model for a structured answer of that type.
- `tools` are fn refs it can use; `canLoad` lets it add more while running (`{ fns?, systems?, tags? }`, default
  `'none'`); `maxTools` (default 32) caps the toolset.
- `context` is context it always has; `hooks` run on each user message it gets.
- `model` picks a model (`{ model: 'openrouter/…' }`, `{ category: 'fast' }` or `{ auto: { cost, speed, accuracy } }`);
  without one the hub's default is used.
- `mode` and `contextor` choose how fns and context reach the model (strict tools or the static toolset; plain,
  suggest or graph context), `maxOutput` caps one answer, `maxIterations` (default 16) caps the steps,
  `toolExecution` is `parallel` (default) or `sequential`.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
.with(({ fns, context: ctx }) => ({
  fns: {
    // an agent: a fn of the hub's `llm` kind with instructions, tools and context
    librarian: agent({
      description: 'Answers questions from the notes, quoting their titles.',
      args: g.obj({ question: g.text() }),
      returns: g.text(),
      instructions: 'Answer {{args.question}} from the notes. Quote the titles you used. Say so when no note covers it.',
      tools: [fns.find, hub.fns.ask_user],
      context: [ctx.guide],
    }),
    // calls other fns through its deps, by ref: its own system's `review` and the hub's `ask_user`
    publish: fn({
      description: 'Share a note once the person approves it.',
      group: 'sharing',
      args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
      returns: g.obj({ published: g.bool(), note: g.optional(g.text()) }),
      deps: { review: fns.review, ask: hub.fns.ask_user },
    }),
  },
}));
```

### Authoring agents

Some fns write something hard: a query, a form's gin type, an API call or code. Don't hand that to the chat model
(it would carry every table or type in its context and guess at the shape; the database's chat fns `get_query` and
`edit_query` take a description in words, never query JSON), and don't make it an `agent()` (a free
loop of tools can draft before it has looked, and never has to check). Write a native fn that runs an **authoring
agent**: `authoringAgent({ name, description, instructions, tools, draft, check, save, reuse? })` from
`@harness/sdk/server`, run with the run's `ctx.ai()`. The chat model passes only a description of what it wants and
gets back an `AuthoredFn`: the saved fn, its signature, what it does, a short preview and how to call it again. Never
the working context. Use `agent()` for a helper that answers or acts with tools; use `authoringAgent` when the result
is a new fn kept in the hub.

The agent works through fixed stages: **find** (search what exists; `reuse` answers with an existing fn), **inspect**
(load the details of what the search found), **draft** (the built-in `draft` tool, whose args are your typed `draft`
schema), **check** (your `check`, run on every draft: each problem on its own line, in words the model can act on),
**fix** (a draft with problems sends the model back, up to `HARNESS_AUTHORING_FIXES` times) and **save** (offered only
once a draft checked clean; your `save` keeps it as a fn). A tool of a stage is offered only once every stage before
it is done, so the model can't draft before it has searched, and until something is saved or reused every round must
call a tool (`toolChoice: 'required'`), so a model can't stop in words with a clean draft unsaved.
`authoringTool({ name, stage, description, schema, when?, strict?, call })` declares a find or inspect tool; `when`
narrows it further. aeye sends tool args as strict tool calls when the model can take them; `strict` is aeye's setting
for one tool (`true` requires strict, a number is how much it is wanted, `false` sends it non-strict). The built-in
`draft` tool is sent non-strict: strict decoding of a whole program or query (a recursive grammar, optional fields at
every level) made models fill every absent optional with null and pick wrong branches, so the schema guides the draft
and your `check` names each problem by path. `session.disclosure` (`Disclosure`) records what the model has seen
(`markAware`), loaded (`markLoaded`) and searched (`markSearched`), per level (`fn`, `type`, `table`): a load tool is
offered while something is aware but not loaded, and nothing is shown twice. The run's own state (deps, the instance,
what it chose) is `session.state`, the `AuthoringSession`'s second type parameter. Write `instructions` as the job step
by step, with a worked example of a good draft.

**Programs.** The `code` and `api` systems author gin programs with `@harness/sdk/program` (`ProgramScope`,
`programTools`, `testTool`, `ProgramAuthoring` on `authoringAgent`). A program's schemas are gin's strict ones
(`buildSchemas(registry, { newStrict: true })`): a `{ kind: 'new', type, value }` is typed by the type it names. In its
value a plain field (text, a number, a bool, an enum or literal, a date) takes its value as is (`"GET"`) or an
expression; every other field (a list, an object, a body, any JSON) takes an expression, usually a `new` of its type.
A type also accepts a named extension of a type it accepts (`list<obj{ key, value }>` takes `list<api/HttpParam>`).
Write the examples in an authoring agent's `instructions` the same way, and test that each fits the schemas the tools
are sent (`examples.test.ts` in the api, code and database systems).

An agent handed what it builds on has nothing to find: `stages: { find: false }` starts it in **inspect** (a find
tool or `reuse` is then refused). `toolNames: { draft, save }` names the built-in tools for the job (`{ draft:
'report_check', save: 'report_save' }`).

The input system's `get_input` writes a form this way. Its find tool:

_From `systems/input/server/authoring.ts`:_

```ts
/** `find_forms`: the saved forms the person may use that may fit. */
const findForms = authoringTool<FormAuthoring, FormDraft, { text: string }>({
  name: 'find_forms',
  stage: 'find',
  description: 'Search the saved forms the person may use by what the request asks for.',
  instructions: 'Call it first, with what is asked in a few plain words.',
  schema: z.object({ text: z.string().min(1).describe('What the form asks for, in plain words.') }),
  async call({ text }, session) {
    const page = await session.state.deps.search({ text, kinds: [FORM_KIND], items: ['fn'], limit: 6 });
    session.disclosure.markSearched('fn');
    session.disclosure.markAware('fn', page.items.map((hit) => hit.name));
    session.disclosure.markAware('guide', ['fields']);
    return page.items.length === 0
      ? 'No saved form fits. See how forms are written with field_types.'
      : ['Saved forms that may fit:', ...page.items.map((hit) => `- ${hit.name}: ${hit.description}`)].join('\n');
  },
});
```

The agent, its draft schema, check and save:

_From `systems/input/server/authoring.ts`:_

```ts
/** The form authoring agent. Run it with the run's `ctx.ai()` and a `FormAuthoring`. */
export const formAuthoring = authoringAgent<FormAuthoring, FormDraft>({
  name: 'form_author',
  description: 'Find or write the form that asks a person for what a request describes.',
  instructions: INSTRUCTIONS,
  tools: [findForms, useSaved, fieldTypes, loadForm],
  draft: { description: 'a form: title, description, submit (optional) and its gin object type.', schema: FormDraftSchema },
  check(draft, session) {
    const form = formOf(draft);
    if (form === undefined) { return Promise.resolve({ problems: ['The type is not a gin type: write it as { "name": "obj", "props": { … } }.'] }); }
    return Promise.resolve({ problems: checkForm(form, session.state.deps.maxFields), note: `${fieldsOf(form.type).length} fields.` });
  },
  async save(draft, session) {
    const form = draftForm(draft, session);
    const kept = await session.state.deps.keep(form, session.request);
    session.state.chose(form);
    return { fn: kept.fn, version: kept.version, signature: `${kept.fn}()`, about: form.description === '' ? form.title : form.description, reused: false, call: `${kept.fn}({})` };
  },
});
```

Its fn runs it and turns a run that gave up (`AuthoringError`, with the last problems) into a failure the chat model can
act on:

_From `systems/input/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
try {
  const { result } = await formAuthoring.run(await ctx.ai(), args.detailedDescription, state);
  const form = state.form;
  if (form === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', `The form authoring agent kept ${result.fn} without its form.`); }
  return ctx.done({ ...asked(ctx, form, result.fn), call: result.call });
} catch (error) {
  if (!(error instanceof AuthoringError)) { throw error; }
  const last = error.problems.length === 0 ? '' : ` Its last problems: ${error.problems.join(' ')}`;
  return ctx.fail('caller', `No form was made: ${error.message}${last} Describe the fields differently or more exactly.`);
}
```

Test it with a scripted model (`mockModel` and `toolCall` from `@harness/sdk/testing`): each step is the tool call the
model makes, and each forward shows which tools were offered at that stage.

_From `systems/input/server/authoring.test.ts`:_

```ts
const model = mockModel([
  toolCall('find_forms', { text: 'project details' }), toolCall('field_types', {}), toolCall('draft', { draft: tooWide }), toolCall('draft', { draft: PROJECT }), toolCall('save', {}), replyChunks('Saved.'),
  toolCall('find_forms', { text: 'project details' }), toolCall('use_saved', { fn: 'input/project_details' }), replyChunks('Reused.'),
]);
const kit = await testHub(input, createInputImpl({ settings: inputEnv.parse() }), { config: {}, model });
try {
  const watched = kit.hub.runInfo(QualifiedName.parse('input/get_input'), { watched: true, inChat: true });
  const made = await kit.call('get_input', { detailedDescription: 'The project: its name and how many hours.' }, { run: watched });
  expect(made.result).toMatchObject({ form: { title: 'Project details' }, saved: 'input/project_details', call: 'input/project_details({})', next: WAIT_FOR_ANSWERS });
  expect(kit.hub.fns.kept.get('input/project_details')?.saved).toMatchObject({ kind: FORM_KIND, payload: PROJECT, share: { visibility: 'private', with: [] } });
  const forwards = made.hubCalls.forwards;
  // find first; the field guide and draft only after; save only after a clean draft
  expect(offered(forwards[0])).toEqual(['find_forms']);
  expect(offered(forwards[1])).toEqual(['field_types', 'find_forms']);
  expect(offered(forwards[2])).toEqual(['draft', 'find_forms']);
  expect(offered(forwards[3])).not.toContain('save');
  expect(JSON.stringify(forwards[3]?.request.messages)).toContain('at most 3');
  expect(offered(forwards[4])).toContain('save');
```

### Reference: kinds and agents

#### `kind`

function · `import { kind } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a kind. `estimates: true` says its implementation has an `estimate` hook that works out a fn's cost from its
payload (a query is free; a form costs nothing until it asks a model), which the hub shows before a run.

```ts
function kind<P>(def: { description: string; applicability: string; payload: GinType<P>; approveChanges?: boolean; status?: StatusTemplates; estimates?: boolean; }): KindDef<P>;
```

#### `KindDef`

interface · `import { KindDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn kind as declared in a contract: what its payload looks like and when to use it.

```ts
interface KindDef<P> {
  readonly description: string;
  /** When an agent should pick this kind for a new fn. */
  readonly applicability: string;
  readonly payload: GinType<P>;
  /** Whether declaring or changing a fn of this kind at runtime needs approval. */
  readonly approveChanges: boolean;
  /** The status words of its fns that declare none. They may use `{fn}`, `{n}` and `{args}` / `{output}` paths. */
  readonly status: StatusTemplates | undefined;
  /** Its implementation's `estimate` works out what a fn of this kind costs from its payload (`FnFacts.cost`, basis `payload`). */
  readonly estimates: boolean;
}
```

#### `KindImpl`

interface · `import { KindImpl } from '@harness/sdk'`

A kind's implementation. The payload is typed; a fn's args and result aren't (they depend on the payload), so they
arrive as JSON the hub has already checked against `describe`'s types.

```ts
interface KindImpl<P, Cfg = unknown> {
  /** Problems with a payload beyond its type (an unknown fn, a program that doesn't type-check). */
  check(payload: P, ctx: KindCheckCtx): Promise<string[]>;
  describe(payload: P, ctx: KindCheckCtx): Promise<KindDescription>;
  run(payload: P, args: Json, ctx: KindRunCtx<Cfg>): Promise<Outcome<UntypedFn>>;
  undo?(payload: P, undo: Json, ctx: UndoCtx<Cfg>): Promise<UndoOutcome>;
  /** What a run of a payload's fn would do, for a fn `describe` gave an `approval` with a preview (a write query). */
  preview?(payload: P, args: Json, ctx: PreviewCtx<Cfg>): Promise<PreviewAnswer>;
  /**
   * What a fn of this payload costs, for a kind declared with `estimates: true`: an estimate with basis `payload` (or
   * `free`), blending in `history` when it helps, or `undefined` to leave it to the history.
   */
  estimate?(payload: P, input: KindEstimateInput, ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg>): Promise<CostEstimate | undefined>;
}
```

Refers to: [`CostEstimate`](#core-costestimate), [`Json`](#core-json), [`KindDescription`](#core-kinddescription), [`PreviewAnswer`](#core-previewanswer), [`UndoOutcome`](#core-undooutcome).

#### `KindEstimateInput`

interface · `import { KindEstimateInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a kind's `estimate` is asked about: one call's args (absent for the fn in general) and the fn's run history, if any.

```ts
interface KindEstimateInput {
  readonly args?: Json;
  readonly history?: CostEstimate;
}
```

Refers to: [`CostEstimate`](#core-costestimate), [`Json`](#core-json).

#### `KindCheckCtx`

interface · `import { KindCheckCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a kind can look up while checking a payload.

```ts
interface KindCheckCtx {
  type(name: QualifiedName): Promise<GinTypeDef | undefined>;
  /** A fn's shape: this system's own from its table, another's from the hub (absent when it may not see it, or before the hub welcomed the system). */
  fn(name: QualifiedName): Promise<KindFnShape | undefined>;
}
```

Refers to: [`GinTypeDef`](#core-gintypedef), [`KindFnShape`](#core-kindfnshape), [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `KindRunCtx`

interface · `import { KindRunCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

A kind's run context: like `FnCtx`, but calls are by name (limited to the deps `describe` returned).

```ts
interface KindRunCtx<Cfg = unknown> extends Omit<FnCtx<UntypedFn, Cfg>, 'fns' | 'handlers' | 'progress'> {
  call(fn: QualifiedName, args: Json, options?: { key?: string; background?: boolean }): FnCall<Json>;
  handler(name: string, args: Json): Promise<Json>;
  progress(state: { fraction: number; message?: string } | { step: string; status: StepStatus; detail?: string }): void;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json), [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname), [`StepStatus`](#core-stepstatus).

#### `KindRef`

interface · `import { KindRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A typed handle to a fn kind. `kinds.api.fn({...})` declares a fn of that kind.

```ts
interface KindRef<N extends QualifiedName, P> {
  readonly ref: 'kind';
  readonly name: N;
  readonly payload: GinType<P>;
  /**
   * Declare a fn of this kind. Only the payload is needed; the kind derives the rest. Give `args`/`returns` to type
   * the ref (the hub checks them against what the kind derives); without them the ref takes and returns JSON.
   */
  fn<A = Json, R = Json, const T extends StatusTemplates = StatusTemplates>(def: KindFnInput<P, A, R, T>): FnDef<KindFnTypes<KindRef<N, P>, P, A, R>>;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json), [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `AnyKindRef`

type · `import { AnyKindRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyKindRef = KindRef<QualifiedName, unknown>;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `kindFn`

function · `import { kindFn } from '@harness/sdk'`

The fn def of a kind fn, built by `KindRef.fn`. The kind's `describe` fills in the rest when the hub imports it.

```ts
function kindFn<N extends `${string}/${string}`, P, A, R, const T extends StatusTemplates = { active: { one: string; many: string; }; retry: { one: string; many: string; }; done: { one: string; many: string; }; failed: { one: string; many: string; }; }>(kind: KindRef<N, P>, def: KindFnInput<P, A, R, T>): FnDef<KindFnTypes<KindRef<N, P>, P, A, R>>;
```

#### `KindFnInput`

interface · `import { KindFnInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What `kinds.x.fn()` takes. Only the payload is required: everything else a fn has is worked out by the kind's
`describe`. `args`/`returns` are optional typing hints: when given, the hub checks them against what the kind
derived, and the ref is typed by them (otherwise by `Json`).

```ts
interface KindFnInput<P, A, R, T extends StatusTemplates = StatusTemplates> {
  description: string;
  instructions?: string;
  payload: P;
  args?: GinType<A>;
  returns?: GinType<R>;
  /** What its runs say they are doing; the kind's words when absent. Tokens are checked against `args` and `returns`. */
  status?: StatusInput<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, T>;
  visibility?: Audience;
  tags?: string[];
  /** Example calls, of the `args` and `returns` types when given (JSON otherwise). */
  examples?: FnExampleOf<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>>[];
  /** For a fleet: always one instance (a saved query of one database), or whichever is first available. */
  routing?: FnRouting;
  /** Checks its system runs before a run starts and after it is done (an agent's answer checked, retried with why). */
  validate?: FnValidators<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, {}>;
  /**
   * What it does to data: its mode (`read`, `write`, `delete`), the named types it reads and writes, and whether its own
   * work is free (reading a file). The hub works out the rest and folds in what it uses (`FnFacts`).
   */
  effects?: FnEffects;
  /** How many of its examples go into the model's prompt with it (the hub's default when absent); the rest page with `hub/fns_examples`. */
  promptExamples?: number;
  /** The API group it is listed in (`SystemInfo.groups`); the general group when absent. */
  group?: string;
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience), [`FnEffects`](#core-fneffects), [`FnRouting`](#core-fnrouting).

#### `KindFnTypes`

interface · `import { KindFnTypes } from '@harness/sdk'`

The types of a fn of some kind. Its deps, handlers and progress come from the kind at runtime.

```ts
interface KindFnTypes<K extends AnyKindRef, P, A, R> {
  kind: K; payload: P; args: A; result: R; deps: {}; handlers: {}; progress: NoProgress; resume: never; undo: never; state: {}; form: never;
}
```

#### `UntypedFn`

interface · `import { UntypedFn } from '@harness/sdk'`

The types of a fn known only at runtime (what a kind's implementation runs).

```ts
interface UntypedFn {
  kind: AnyKindRef; payload: unknown; args: Json; result: Json; deps: {}; handlers: Handlers; progress: ProgressDef; resume: Json; undo: Json; state: Record<string, Json>; form: Json;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json).

#### `agent`

function · `import { agent } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare an agent: an LLM with instructions, tools and context, callable like any fn. A system can ship its own
agents (a code reviewer, a planner) with tools from any system.

```ts
function agent<A, R, const T extends StatusTemplates = { active: { one: string; many: string; }; retry: { one: string; many: string; }; done: { one: string; many: string; }; failed: { one: string; many: string; }; }>(def: AgentInput<A, R, T>): FnDef<AgentFnTypes<A, R>>;
```

#### `AgentInput`

interface · `import { AgentInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What `agent()` takes.

```ts
interface AgentInput<A, R, T extends StatusTemplates = StatusTemplates> {
  description: string;
  args: GinType<A>;
  /** Text for a chat-style agent; any type for a structured answer. */
  returns: GinType<R>;
  /**
   * The agent's own prompt (Handlebars; `{{args.x}}` reads the args): its job, how to use its tools step by step,
   * what to answer with, and worked examples. Write it for the model.
   */
  instructions: string;
  /** What callers read (the fn's `instructions`): when to use it, how to call it, what it returns, pitfalls. */
  usage?: string;
  model?: ModelChoice;
  tools?: AnyFnRef[];
  /** Fns the agent may add to its toolset while running. Default `none`. */
  canLoad?: { fns?: AnyFnRef[]; systems?: string[]; tags?: string[] } | 'none';
  /** Default 32. */
  maxTools?: number;
  context?: AnyContextRef[];
  hooks?: AnyHookRef[];
  /** Default `parallel`. */
  toolExecution?: 'parallel' | 'sequential';
  /** Default 16. */
  maxIterations?: number;
  /** How fns reach the model: strict tools (the hub's default) or the static toolset. */
  mode?: NonNullable<LlmPayload['mode']>;
  /** What goes into each prompt: plain (the hub's default), suggest, or graph (for small local models). */
  contextor?: NonNullable<LlmPayload['contextor']>;
  /** The most tokens one answer may take (never past the model's own limit); the hub's `HARNESS_AGENT_MAX_OUTPUT` when absent. */
  maxOutput?: number;
  visibility?: Audience;
  tags?: string[];
  /** The API group it is listed in: one of the system's `groups` (`system(name, { groups })`); the general group when absent. */
  group?: string;
  /** Example calls (args of its own args type), shown to other agents on its card. */
  examples?: FnExampleOf<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>>[];
  /** Checks its system runs on the args before it starts and on its answer after (`retry` sends the model back with why). */
  validate?: FnValidators<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, {}>;
  /** What its runs say they are doing; the hub's agent words when absent. Tokens are checked against `args` and `returns`. */
  status?: StatusInput<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, T>;
  /** For a fleet: always one instance, or whichever is first available (get_query routed to the database it serves). */
  routing?: FnRouting;
  /** What it does to data and the registry: its mode, the types it reads and writes, and the fns it creates (`FnFacts`). */
  effects?: FnEffects;
  /** How it takes the context injected into it (`{ detail: 'summary', maxTokens: 600 }`), or `none`. Default: every item in full. */
  injected?: AgentInjected | 'none';
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience), [`FnEffects`](#core-fneffects), [`FnRouting`](#core-fnrouting), [`ModelChoice`](#core-modelchoice).

#### `AgentFnTypes`

type · `import { AgentFnTypes } from '@harness/sdk'`

The types of an agent fn.

```ts
type AgentFnTypes<A, R> = KindFnTypes<LlmKind, LlmPayload, A, R>;
```

#### `AgentInjected`

interface · `import { AgentInjected } from '@harness/sdk'`

How an agent takes its injected section. Its instructions may hold `{{injectedContext}}`; without it the section follows them.

```ts
interface AgentInjected {
  /** `summary` gives each item at its summary, for a small window. Default `full`. */
  readonly detail?: 'full' | 'summary';
  /** The most tokens the section may take; items past it fall back to their summary, a listing, then out. */
  readonly maxTokens?: number;
}
```

#### `LlmPayload`

type · `import { LlmPayload } from '@harness/sdk'`

The payload of a `hub/llm` fn, as stored.

```ts
type LlmPayload = Infer<typeof LlmPayloadType>;
```

#### `LlmKind`

type · `import { LlmKind } from '@harness/sdk'`

The ref type of the hub's `llm` kind.

```ts
type LlmKind = typeof hub.kinds.llm;
```

#### `AgentRef`

type · `import { AgentRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

An agent fn's ref: a fn of the hub's `llm` kind (`agent()`, `hub.kinds.llm.fn()`), marked with its kind so only
agents fit where an agent is asked for (an injection's target). A plain fn's ref has no `kind` and doesn't compile.

```ts
type AgentRef<N extends QualifiedName = QualifiedName, A = unknown, R = unknown, H extends Handlers = Handlers> = FnRef<N, A, R, H> & { readonly kind: 'hub/llm' };
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `FnRefOf`

type · `import { FnRefOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The ref of a fn as its contract declares it: an agent's carries its kind (`AgentRef`), any other fn's is a `FnRef`.

```ts
type FnRefOf<N extends QualifiedName, T extends FnTypes> = T['kind'] extends { readonly name: 'hub/llm' }
? AgentRef<N, T['args'], T['result'], T['handlers']>
: FnRef<N, T['args'], T['result'], T['handlers']>;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `authoringAgent`

function · `import { authoringAgent } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Declare an authoring agent: a forced find → inspect → draft → check → fix → save workflow on aeye.

```ts
function authoringAgent<S, D>(def: AuthoringAgentInput<S, D>): AuthoringAgent<S, D>;
```

#### `AuthoringAgent`

interface · `import { AuthoringAgent } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A declared authoring agent: run it inside a fn with the run's `ctx.ai()`.

```ts
interface AuthoringAgent<S, D> {
  readonly name: string;
  run(ai: HarnessAi, request: string, state: S): Promise<{ result: AuthoredFn; session: AuthoringSession<S, D> }>;
}
```

#### `AuthoringAgentInput`

interface · `import { AuthoringAgentInput } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What `authoringAgent` takes.

```ts
interface AuthoringAgentInput<S, D> {
  readonly name: string;
  readonly description: string;
  /**
   * The agent's job, step by step, with worked examples. Write it for the model. The current stage, the request and
   * the stage guide are added after it each round.
   */
  readonly instructions: string;
  /** Find and inspect tools (search, load, examples, …). */
  readonly tools: ReadonlyArray<AuthoringTool<S, D>>;
  /** The draft's args: the typed authoring schema (`buildQueryTool`'s for queries, gin's `buildSchemas` for types). */
  readonly draft: { readonly description: string; readonly schema: z.ZodType<D> | ((session: AuthoringSession<S, D>) => z.ZodType<D>) };
  /** Check a draft: every problem, one per line, in words the model can act on; a preview when it is clean. */
  check(draft: D, session: AuthoringSession<S, D>): Promise<DraftCheck>;
  /** Save a clean draft as a fn. */
  save(draft: D, session: AuthoringSession<S, D>): Promise<AuthoredFn>;
  /** Answer with an existing fn instead (found in `find`); a string is why it can't be reused. */
  reuse?(fn: string, session: AuthoringSession<S, D>): Promise<AuthoredFn | string>;
  /** The model, the aeye way (`role/chat`, `openrouter/…`); the hub picks when absent. */
  readonly model?: string;
  /** Failed checks it may fix; `HARNESS_AUTHORING_FIXES` when absent. */
  readonly fixes?: number;
  /** Model rounds; `HARNESS_AUTHORING_ROUNDS` when absent. */
  readonly rounds?: number;
  /** The most tokens one model step may write; `HARNESS_AUTHORING_MAX_TOKENS` when absent. */
  readonly maxTokens?: number;
  /** `find: false`: the agent is handed what it builds on, so it starts in `inspect` (no find tools, no `reuse`). */
  readonly stages?: { readonly find?: boolean };
  /** Names for the built-in tools when the job wants its own words (default `draft` and `save`). */
  readonly toolNames?: { readonly draft?: string; readonly save?: string };
}
```

#### `authoringTool`

function · `import { authoringTool } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Declare an authoring tool.

```ts
function authoringTool<S, D, I extends object>(def: AuthoringToolInput<S, D, I>): AuthoringTool<S, D>;
```

#### `AuthoringTool`

interface · `import { AuthoringTool } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

An authoring tool with its args type erased: it makes its aeye tool for a session.

```ts
interface AuthoringTool<S, D> {
  readonly name: string;
  readonly stage: ToolStage;
  make(ai: HarnessAi, session: AuthoringSession<S, D>): AeyeTool;
}
```

#### `AuthoringToolInput`

interface · `import { AuthoringToolInput } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

One tool of an authoring agent, typed by its args; `authoringTool` erases the args for the agent's list.

```ts
interface AuthoringToolInput<S, D, I extends object> {
  readonly name: string;
  /** What it does, for the model. */
  readonly description: string;
  /** How and when to use it, for the model (added to the prompt's tools section). */
  readonly instructions?: string;
  readonly stage: ToolStage;
  /** Its args: a zod schema, or one built from the session (a typed schema per instance). */
  readonly schema: z.ZodType<I> | ((session: AuthoringSession<S, D>) => z.ZodType<I>);
  /** Offered only while this also holds (a load tool while something is aware but not loaded). */
  readonly when?: (session: AuthoringSession<S, D>) => boolean;
  /** aeye's strictness for its args (default: prefer strict). */
  readonly strict?: boolean | number;
  /** Do it; the text is what the model reads. A thrown error is shown to the model as the tool's failure. */
  call(input: I, session: AuthoringSession<S, D>): Promise<string> | string;
}
```

#### `AuthoringSession`

class · `import { AuthoringSession } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

One run's working state: the stage reached, what was disclosed, the draft and its last check, the result.

```ts
class AuthoringSession<S, D> {
  readonly disclosure: Disclosure;
  /** What the caller asked for, word for word. */
  readonly request: string;
  /** The authoring fn's own state (the database, the instance, the type registry, …). */
  readonly state: S;
  /** `skip`: stages the agent has no use for, done from the start (`find` for an agent handed what it builds on). */
  constructor(request: string, state: S, skip: readonly SkippableStage[] = []);
  /** The current stage: the first one not done, `fix` while the last check failed, `save` once it passed. */
  get stage(): "check" | "find" | "inspect" | "draft" | "fix" | "save";
  /** Whether a tool of a stage may be offered now: every stage before it is done, and the run is still going. */
  open(stage: ToolStage): boolean;
  /** Mark a stage done (a tool call of that stage succeeded). */
  done(stage: AuthoringStage, tool: string): void;
  get draft(): D | undefined;
  get lastCheck(): DraftCheck | undefined;
  get fixes(): number;
  get result(): { fn: string; version: number; signature: string; about: string; reused: boolean; call: string; preview?: z.core.util.JSONType | undefined; } | undefined;
  /** The tools called, in order, with their stage: what tests and status lines read. */
  get log(): readonly { stage: AuthoringStage; tool: string; }[];
  /** Keep a draft and its check; a failed check after an earlier draft counts as a fix. */
  checked(draft: D, check: DraftCheck): void;
  finish(result: AuthoredFn): void;
  /** Give up: the fix cap was reached with this check. Every tool is withdrawn. */
  stop(check: DraftCheck): void;
  /** The check it gave up on, when it did. */
  get stopped(): DraftCheck | undefined;
  /** Neither saved nor given up. */
  get going(): boolean;
}
```

#### `AuthoringStage`

type · `import { AuthoringStage } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

```ts
type AuthoringStage = (typeof AUTHORING_STAGES)[number];
```

#### `AUTHORING_STAGES`

const · `import { AUTHORING_STAGES } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The fixed workflow, in order. `fix` is entered when a check fails and left when a draft checks clean.

```ts
const AUTHORING_STAGES: readonly ["find", "inspect", "draft", "check", "fix", "save"];
```

#### `ToolStage`

type · `import { ToolStage } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The stages a tool belongs to (`check` and `fix` are the built-in `draft` tool's; `save` the built-in `save`).

```ts
type ToolStage = 'find' | 'inspect' | 'draft';
```

#### `SkippableStage`

type · `import { SkippableStage } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The stage an agent may leave out: `find`, when it is handed what it builds on.

```ts

```

#### `DraftCheck`

interface · `import { DraftCheck } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What a check of a draft found: the problems (one per line; empty when it is clean) and a preview when clean.

```ts

  readonly problems: readonly string[];
  readonly preview?: AuthoredFn['preview'];
  /** Extra text for the model about the draft (the SQL it makes, the type it produces). */

}
```

#### `Disclosure`

class · `import { Disclosure } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What has been seen, loaded and searched, per level (`type`, `fn`, `table`, …): product's progressive disclosure
bookkeeping. Aware names have appeared somewhere (a search result, a reference); loaded names are fully shown.

```ts
class Disclosure {
  /** A level's search ran, even with no results: a search proving absence is when creating becomes right. */
  markSearched(level: string): void;
  searched(level: string): boolean;
  markAware(level: string, names: readonly string[]): void;
  /** Loading makes a name aware too. */
  markLoaded(level: string, names: readonly string[]): void;
  isLoaded(level: string, name: string): boolean;
  /** Names seen but not loaded yet, in the order they were seen: what a load tool may load. */
  awareUnloaded(level: string): string[];
  loaded(level: string): string[];
}
```

#### `authoredFn`

const · `import { authoredFn } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What an authoring fn answers the chat model with: the saved fn, what it does, how to call it again, and a short

#### `AuthoredFn`

type · `import { AuthoredFn } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

## 12. Settings and attachments

### Settings

A **setting** is a typed control an admin (or, if allowed, the person or a fn) sets per actor: a user, a group, an
agent, a system or a hub MCP. `setting({ description, type, appliesTo, merge, editableBy? })`:

- `type`: the value's gin type (the admin's form is drawn from it).
- `appliesTo`: the actor kinds it can be set on (`user`, `group`, `agent`, `system`, `mcp`).
- `merge`: how a user's value combines with their groups': `user-wins`, `union`, `intersect` or `most-restrictive`.
- `editableBy`: besides admins, `self` (the person for themselves) and `fns` (fns may set it).

A run reads the effective value for its person with `await ctx.setting(notes.settings.editing)`, typed, or `undefined`
when nothing is set; always handle `undefined` with a safe default.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
settings: {
  editing: setting({
    description: 'Whether removing a note asks first.',
    type: g.obj({ confirmRemove: g.bool() }),
    appliesTo: ['user', 'group'],
    merge: 'most-restrictive',
  }),
},
```

### Attachments

An **attachment** is data a fn adds to the assistant message its run belongs to, drawn by the system's renderer for
it (a diff, a chart, a card). Declare it with `attachment({ description, data })`; a run attaches with
`ctx.attach(notes.attachments.note, data, title)`, and the data is checked against the type (wrong data doesn't
compile). Without a renderer the chat shows the data as a plain card. To attach a link instead (a resource, a URL, a
stored file or another run), use `ctx.link({ resource })`, `{ url, title }`, `{ file, title }` or `{ run, title }`.

## 13. Context and commands

**Context** is text for the LLM: a guide, a skill, a reference, the state of something. The hub suggests it when a
message is about it, an agent can always have it (`agent({ context })`), it can be injected into agents' system
prompts (`inject`), and a person loads it with a `/command`. `context({ description, … })`:

- **Static** when it has `content`: the text is in the contract. `{{name}}` in the content is filled from the
  command's args.
- **Dynamic** when it has no `content`: the server makes the text each time it is loaded, so it can be current (a folder
  tree, the newest notes). The server **must** implement it under `context`, as `async (args, ctx) => text`.
- `command: { name, args? }` makes it a `/name` command. Each arg is `arg(hint, { required? })`, required unless
  `{ required: false }`; the implementation's `args` are typed from them (`{ tag?: string }`).
- `fns`: fns that come with it, loaded into the toolset when it is loaded; `suggestFns`: fns suggested with it.
- `imports`: other context it brings along.
- `suggest` and `visible`: who it may be suggested to and who can find it in searches (`both`, `user`, `llm`, `none`).
- `title`: what people (and an injected item's heading) call it; its description when absent. `tags`: free labels.
- `inject: { into, order?, requires?, mode? }` puts it in the system prompts of the agents it targets, in their
  `{{injectedContext}}` section, once an admin approves it (a new or changed injection waits in the system's Pending
  review as its own card, flagged `injects`); an admin can switch it off per agent. Each `into` entry is an agent's ref
  (`crm.fns.reviewer`; only `hub/llm` fns compile) or a pattern holding `*`: `*` alone is every agent, otherwise
  `system/local` where each `*` is any run of characters on its own side of the `/` (it crosses `.`, never `/`; case
  counts; `**` is refused). Build patterns from refs so a rename breaks the build: `agents.startingWith(hub.fns.chat)`
  is `hub/chat*`, `agents.of(crm)` is `crm/*`, `agents.all` is `*`. A string without `*` doesn't compile. An exact name
  that is no agent is refused when the system declares it; a pattern that hits no agent yet is accepted, with a warning
  on its review card. `order`: lower comes first (default 100; the hub's own items are 10 to 30). Its `fns` join the
  agents' starting tools; `requires` names fns its text mentions without bringing, and it is left out while they can't
  be used. `mode: 'tools' | 'static'` only when the text is true in one chat mode.
- An agent says how it takes its injected section: `agent({ injected: { detail: 'summary', maxTokens: 600 } })`, or
  `injected: 'none'`. A hook says what its block means with `hook({ explains })`, injected with its system's items.
- For the graph contextor (the one small local models use): `weight` (0 to 2, default 1) is how much it counts,
  `summary` a short form sent when the budget allows only a little, `parent` and `next` place it in a hierarchy or a
  chain of pieces, and `links` are weighted edges (`{ to, relation, weight? }`) to other context, fns, named types or
  resources (`{ uri, section? }`). `imports` and `brings` make items travel together; `suggests`, `related`, `about`,
  `parent` and `next` pass on part of an item's score.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
context: {
  guide: context({
    description: 'How to keep notes here: search before adding, short titles, lowercase tags.',
    content: 'Search with notes/find before adding a note, so you do not add one twice. Keep titles short and tags lowercase.',
    fns: [fns.find, fns.add],
    // for the graph contextor: how much it counts, a short form for tight budgets, and what it relates to
    weight: 1.2,
    summary: 'Search before adding; short titles; lowercase tags.',
    links: [{ to: types.Note, relation: 'about' }, { to: fns.tidy, relation: 'suggests', weight: 0.3 }],
  }),
  // no `content`: dynamic, so the server makes the text; the command is `/notes [tag]`
  recent: context({
    description: 'The newest notes, optionally with one tag.',
    command: { name: 'notes', args: { tag: arg('Only notes with this tag', { required: false }) } },
  }),
},
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
context: {
  // the newest few, by query (newest change first), never all of them
  recent: async (args, ctx) => {
    const page = await ctx.data.find(Note, { query: { filters: tagFilter(args.tag), limit: s.NOTES_FIND_LIMIT } });
    if (page.items.length === 0) { return args.tag === undefined ? 'There are no notes yet.' : `No note has the tag "${args.tag}".`; }
    return page.items.map((note) => markdownOf(note.value)).join('\n\n---\n\n');
  },
},
```

A dynamic context's `ctx` is the system context (config, LLM, resources, types, log, data folders) plus who is asking
(`ctx.user`, `ctx.time`, and the session when there is one). Keep context focused: a short guide that names its fns
beats a long manual, and the hub budgets what it sends to small models.

**A context folder.** Static context can live in markdown files under the system's `context/` folder instead of the
contract: each file starts with a YAML header between `---` lines holding any context option (`ContextFileHeader`:
`key`, `title`, `description`, `summary` (required), `fns`, `imports`, `choices`, `parent`, `next`, `inject`, `tags`,
`visible`, …), and the rest of the file is the content. `key` is a stable, readable name people never see
(`notes.guide.adding`); it is never taken from the path, so a file can move or be renamed and the hub keeps updating the
same record with its links, stats and embeddings.

```md
---
key: guide
title: Notes guide
description: Where to start with notes.
summary: The index of the notes guide; pick the part you need.
choices:
  - to: notes/guide.adding
    when: You are adding or editing a note.
---
Notes are plain markdown. Use fn:notes/add to add one, or read [adding notes](context:notes/guide.adding).
```

`harness system build` checks the folder and fails with `file:line:col` problems on a header that doesn't match, a
duplicate key, a link to a fn or context that doesn't exist (in the header, and `context:` / `fn:` references in the
body), a context that imports itself in a cycle, and a missing summary. `serve` sends the files as the system's context;
with `HARNESS_CONTEXT_WATCH=true` (development) it watches the folder and sends each saved change to the hub. Keep each
item to one idea with a short summary, start with a shallow index whose `choices` are worded as decisions, and let a
small model walk the graph a step at a time instead of reading one long list.

#### `ContextDef`

interface · `import { ContextDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
interface ContextDef<A, Dynamic extends boolean> {
  readonly description: string;
  /** What people (and the injected section's heading) call it; its description when absent. */
  readonly title: string | undefined;
  readonly dynamic: Dynamic;
  readonly content: string | undefined;
  readonly command: { readonly name: string; readonly args: Readonly<Record<string, ArgDef<boolean>>> } | undefined;
  readonly imports: readonly AnyContextRef[];
  readonly fns: readonly AnyFnRef[];
  readonly suggestFns: readonly AnyFnRef[];

  /** Who can find it in searches. Default `both`. */

  /** Put it in these agents' system prompts; its `fns` join their starting tools. */
  readonly inject: InjectInput | undefined;
  readonly tags: readonly string[];
  /** How much it counts when the graph contextor scores it: 0 leaves it out, 2 doubles it. Default 1. */
  readonly weight: number | undefined;
  /** A short form shown when the budget allows only a little of it; its description otherwise. */
  readonly summary: string | undefined;
  /** Its parent in a hierarchy (a guide over its chapters). */
  readonly parent: AnyContextRef | undefined;
  /** The next piece of a chain (a long text in ordered pieces). */
  readonly next: AnyContextRef | undefined;
  readonly links: readonly ContextLinkDef[];
  /** Phantom: the command's args. Never set at runtime. */
  readonly argsType?: A;
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience).

#### `AnyContextDef`

type · `import { AnyContextDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyContextDef = ContextDef<unknown, boolean>;
```

#### `ContextImpl`

type · `import { ContextImpl } from '@harness/sdk'`

A dynamic context's implementation: its content for these args.

```ts
type ContextImpl<A, Cfg = unknown> = (args: A, ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg> & Partial<AskerCtx> & Pick<AskerCtx, 'user' | 'time'>) => Promise<string>;
```

#### `ContextLinkDef`

interface · `import { ContextLinkDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A weighted edge from a context item to something it relates to, which the graph contextor follows: `imports` and
`brings` travel together; `suggests`, `related`, `about`, `parent` and `next` pass on some of an item's score.

```ts
interface ContextLinkDef {
  readonly to: ContextLinkTarget;
  readonly relation: DeclarableRelation;
  /** How strongly, from 0 to 1. Default 0.5. */
  readonly weight?: number;
}
```

Refers to: [`DeclarableRelation`](#core-declarablerelation).

#### `ContextLinkTarget`

type · `import { ContextLinkTarget } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a context link points at: another context item, a fn, a named type, or a resource (or one of its sections).

```ts
type ContextLinkTarget = AnyContextRef | AnyFnRef | AnyTypeRef | { readonly uri: ResourceUri; readonly section?: string };
```

Refers to: [`ResourceUri`](#core-resourceuri).

#### `arg`

function · `import { arg } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a `/command` argument. Required unless `{ required: false }`.

```ts
function arg<const Req extends boolean = true>(hint: string, options?: { required?: Req; }): ArgDef<Req>;
```

#### `ArgDef`

interface · `import { ArgDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

An argument of a `/command`.

```ts
interface ArgDef<Req extends boolean> {
  readonly hint: string;
  readonly required: Req;
}
```

#### `ArgsOf`

type · `import { ArgsOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The args object of a command.

```ts
type ArgsOf<A extends Record<string, ArgDef<boolean>>> =
Simplify<{ -readonly [K in keyof A as A[K]['required'] extends true ? K : never]: string } & { -readonly [K in keyof A as A[K]['required'] extends true ? never : K]?: string }>;
```

#### `ContextRef`

interface · `import { ContextRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A typed handle to context. A `/command`'s args are typed. In a template string it writes `context:<its key>`, rendered as the key.

```ts
interface ContextRef<N extends QualifiedName, A> {
  readonly ref: 'context';
  readonly name: N;
  /** Phantom: the command's args. Never set at runtime. */
  readonly args?: A;
  /** `context:<its key>`, for model-facing text (`withRefText`). */
  toString(): string;
}
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `AnyContextRef`

type · `import { AnyContextRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyContextRef = ContextRef<QualifiedName, unknown>;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `InjectInput`

interface · `import { InjectInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

Where an item of context is injected: the system prompts of the agents it targets, in their `{{injectedContext}}`
section. It waits for an admin's approval like any change to the system's contract, and an admin can switch it off
for any agent.

```ts
interface InjectInput {
  /** The agents it goes into: refs and patterns (`agents.startingWith(hub.fns.chat)`, `'crm/*'`). At least one. */
  readonly into: readonly [AgentTarget, ...AgentTarget[]];
  /** Lower comes first. Default 100; the hub's own items are 10 to 30. */
  readonly order?: number;
  /** Fns its text names without bringing them: it is left out unless they can be used. Its `fns` are required too. */
  readonly requires?: readonly AnyFnRef[];
  /** Only in this chat mode. Leave it out unless the text is true in one mode only. */
  readonly mode?: 'tools' | 'static';
}
```

#### `AgentTarget`

type · `import { AgentTarget } from '@harness/sdk'`

One entry of `inject.into`: an agent's ref (`hub.fns.chat`; only `hub/llm` fns fit), or a pattern.

```ts
type AgentTarget = AgentRef | AgentPattern;
```

#### `AgentPattern`

type · `import { AgentPattern } from '@harness/sdk'`

Agents by a pattern over their qualified names. `*` alone is every agent; any other pattern is `system/local`, where
each `*` stands for any run of characters (none included) on its own side of the `/`: it crosses `.`, never `/`.
Case counts, and `**` is refused. A string without `*` doesn't compile: one agent is named by its ref.

```ts
type AgentPattern = '*' | `${string}*${string}/${string}` | `${string}/${string}*${string}`;
```

#### `agents`

const · `import { agents } from '@harness/sdk'`

Patterns built from refs, so renaming an agent or a system breaks the build instead of the match.

```ts
const agents: { readonly all: "*"; readonly of: <const S extends string>(system: { readonly name: S extends `${string}/${string}` ? never : S; }) => `${S}/*`; readonly startingWith: <const N extends QualifiedName>(agent: AgentRef<N>) => `${N}*`; };
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

## 14. Hooks

A **hook** adds information to each user message before the LLM sees it (the git branch, the pinned notes, the open
ticket). Declare it with `hook({ description, order?, timeout? })`: lower `order` runs first (default 100), and after
`timeout` ms (default 1000) the message goes on without it, so keep hooks fast.

The server implements it as `async (input, ctx) => contributions`. `input` is the message (`HookInput`: session, turn,
text, user, time); each contribution is one of:

- `{ type: 'text', tag, text }`: a tagged block the LLM can tell apart from the person's words;
- `{ type: 'suggest-context', items: [{ context, why? }] }` and `{ type: 'suggest-fns', items: [{ fn, why? }] }`:
  suggestions, by ref;
- `{ type: 'load', context?, fns? }`: load these now.

Return `[]` when there is nothing to add.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
hooks: { pinned: hook({ description: 'Adds the titles of pinned notes to each message.', order: 60, timeout: 300 }) },
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
hooks: {
  pinned: async (input, ctx) => {
    // the pinned ones only, filtered by the hub, a few at most
    const pinned = await ctx.data.find(Note, { query: { filters: [{ field: 'pinned', op: 'is', value: true }], limit: s.NOTES_HOOK_TITLES } });
    const blocks: HookContribution[] = [];
    if (pinned.items.length > 0) { blocks.push({ type: 'text', tag: 'pinned-notes', text: `Pinned notes: ${pinned.items.map((note) => `"${note.value.title}"`).join(', ')}.` }); }
    if (/\bnotes?\b/i.test(input.text)) { blocks.push({ type: 'suggest-context', items: [{ context: notes.context.guide, why: 'The message is about notes.' }] }); }
    return blocks;
  },
},
```

### Reference: hooks

#### `hook`

function · `import { hook } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a hook. Defaults: order 100, timeout 1000 ms. `explains` says what its block (`<fs_git>`) means to the model;
a new or changed `explains` waits for an admin's review like any injection.

```ts

```

Refers to: [`Duration`](#core-duration).

```ts
}
```

Refers to: [`Duration`](#core-duration).


Refers to: [`HookInput`](#core-hookinput).

```ts
/** Load these now. */
| { type: 'load'; context?: AnyContextRef[]; fns?: AnyFnRef[] };
```


Refers to: [`HookBlock`](#core-hookblock).

#### `HookRef`

interface · `import { HookRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

## 15. Mentions

A **mention provider** lets people point at things the system knows with `@` in the chat (a file, a database row, a
note). Declare it with `mention({ title, description, target, of? })`: `target` is the gin type of the key that gets
stored with the message (keep it small and stable: an id, a path), and `of` names the type of the things it points at,
so chips and cards use that type's display and cards offer its methods.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
mentions: {
  // `@` in the chat finds notes; chips and cards show them as Note (its label, icon, color and its pin method)
  notes: mention({ title: 'Notes', description: 'Any note, by its title.', target: g.obj({ id: g.text() }), of: types.NoteEntry }),
},
```

The server implements three functions, each getting the person who is asking (`ctx.user`, `ctx.session`), so results
are limited to what they may see:

- `search(query, ctx)`: options for what was typed after `@`, each `{ label, hint?, icon?, target }`. The chip shows
  `label`; `target` must fit the declared type.
- `hover(target, ctx)`: the hover card, asked for when hovered so it is current (`{ title, subtitle?, fields?, body?,
  open?, value? }`), or `undefined` when the thing no longer exists. Give `value` as the `of` type to offer its methods.
- `llm(target, ctx)`: the text the LLM gets for the mention: what the thing is, where it lives, and how to reach it
  with the system's fns.

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
mentions: {
  notes: {
    async search(query, ctx) {
      // the hub's search: by words and meaning, best first, one small page
      const page = await ctx.data.find(Note, { search: query, query: { limit: 20 } });
      return page.items.map((note) => ({ label: note.value.title, hint: note.value.tags.join(', '), icon: 'note', target: { id: note.id } }));
    },
    async hover(target, ctx) {
      const note = await noteById(ctx.data, target.id);
      if (note === undefined) { return undefined; }
      // returning the value as a NoteEntry lets the card offer its `pin` method
      return { title: note.value.title, subtitle: note.value.tags.join(', '), body: note.value.text.slice(0, 280), value: entryOf(note) };
    },
    async llm(target, ctx) {
      const note = await noteById(ctx.data, target.id);
      if (note === undefined) { return `Note ${target.id} no longer exists.`; }
      return `Note "${note.value.title}" (id ${note.id}) in the notes system:\n\n${markdownOf(note.value)}`;
    },
  },
},
```

In `search`, `ctx.resources.search(query, { roots?, limit? })` searches the hub's resources as the person typing:
the hub ties it to their `@` search, so a provider can offer pages or files they may see (the web system does). It
refuses anywhere else.

A custom hover card is a renderer under `mentions` in **The client**; without one the hub draws the `hover` answer.

### Reference: mentions

#### `mention`


Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

```ts
}
```

## 16. Actions

An **action** is a typed message the system's renderers send it ("pin", "approve", "load more"). Declare it with
`action({ description, input, returns?, state?, expires? })`: without `returns` it returns nothing, `state` declares
session state fields it reads and writes (like a fn's), and `expires` drops it if it can't be delivered within that
many ms (default: never).

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
actions: {
  pin: action({ description: 'Pin or unpin a note from its card.', input: g.obj({ id: g.text(), pinned: g.bool() }), returns: g.bool() }),
},
```

The hub **stores every action before delivering it**, so nothing is lost: if the system is offline it waits and is
delivered, in order, when the system connects. A renderer sends one with `view.system.send.pin({ id, pinned })` and gets a
`Delivery` whose `status` is live and whose `result` resolves with the typed result. The server implements each as
`async (input, ctx) => result`; `ctx` has the asker (`ctx.user`, `ctx.session`), the run whose renderer sent it
(`ctx.run`, when there is one), the delivery id, session state, and the run services.

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
actions: {
  pin: async (input, ctx) => {
    const note = await noteById(ctx.data, input.id);
    if (note === undefined) { return false; }
    await ctx.data.save(Note, { ...note.value, pinned: input.pinned }, { id: note.id });
    return true;
  },
},
```

To start a run from a renderer instead (a "run again" button), use `view.system.run.<fn>(args)`: the new run shows in
the chat like any other, with its own approval, status, cost and undo.

### Reference: actions

#### `action`

function · `import { action } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts

```

Refers to: [`Duration`](#core-duration).

#### `ActionDef`

interface · `import { ActionDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

delivering it, so nothing is lost while the system is offline.

Refers to: [`Duration`](#core-duration).

## 17. Model providers

A system can bring **model providers**: models whose calls run in the system (a GPU box, a private API, a gateway the
hub can't reach). Declare each in the contract with `provider({ description, models })`: every model's id, abilities
(aeye capability names such as `chat`, `tools`, `vision`, `embedding`), context window, prices in USD per million
tokens, and whether it reads a prompt cache. The implementation gives a function that makes the aeye `Provider` for the
current config (made again when the config changes).

```ts
// shared/
.with({ providers: { local: provider({ description: 'Models on our GPU box.', models: [
  { id: 'small', capabilities: ['chat', 'tools', 'streaming'], contextWindow: 32_768, pricing: { input: 0, output: 0 }, caching: false },
] }) } })
// server/
serve(contract, { providers: { local: (ctx) => new OpenAIProvider({ apiKey: ctx.config.key, baseURL: ctx.config.url }) } }, options);
```

While the system is connected the hub registers each provider as `<system>/<provider>` (here `gpu/local`), so its models
can be picked like any other; chat calls stream over the link and embeddings are answered by the provider's `embed`. A
call for a model the contract doesn't declare is refused. The hub prices and records every call from the declared
prices, as it does for its own providers.

This is also how a system brings an **embedder**: a model with `capabilities: ['embedding']` (or `imageEmbedding` for
pictures; `crossModalEmbedding` when text and pictures share one space), its vectors' `dimensions` and the most
characters it reads (`maxInput`) is an embedder the hub's index can use, named `<system>/<model id>`. The old
`embedders` section and `embedder()` builder are gone: a contract or implementation that still has `embedders` is
refused when built or served, with a message pointing here.

**Runtime providers.** When an admin chooses the models while the system runs (the models a local server has and an
admin offers), declare the provider with `models: 'runtime'` (at most one per contract) and set its models, whole, once
`serve` returned and whenever they change: `await running.models(contract.providers.server, models)`, each model as
`provider()` checks one. Each instance sends it named after itself (`<system>/<instance>`, here `local/desk`), so a
fleet has one provider per instance and the hub routes each one's calls there; its implementation is still the one
function under `providers`. Free models move without another review once the system went live; a price waits for an
admin. The Ollama system is one (`systems/ollama`).

```ts
// shared/
.with({ providers: { server: provider({ description: 'The models an admin offers here.', models: 'runtime' }) } })
// server/main.ts
const running = await serve(contract, impl, options);
await running.models(contract.providers.server, [{ id: 'small:latest', capabilities: ['chat'], contextWindow: 8_192, pricing: { input: 0, output: 0 }, caching: false }]);
```

### Reference: model providers

#### `provider`

function · `import { provider } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a provider: `provider({ description: 'Models on our GPU box', models: [{ id: 'small', capabilities: ['chat'],
contextWindow: 8192, pricing: { input: 0, output: 0 }, caching: false }] })`. Each model is checked now, so a wrong
one fails as the contract is built.

```ts

```

```ts
  description: string;

   * Every model it serves; a call for any other model is refused. `'runtime'`: the running system sets them
```

