## 21. The run context and services

Every implementation gets a context (`ctx`) typed by what it implements.

**A fn's run (`FnCtx`)** has, besides the services below:

- `ctx.config`: the system's config as it was when the run started (a run keeps that value even if it changes).
- `ctx.run`: who the run is for and where it sits (`RunInfo`): its id, fn, turn and session; `user` (id, name, email,
  role, groups, time zone, locale); `time` (`now`, the zone, and the local time with its offset); this system and
  instance, and `system.from` when another system's fn called it; the caller, the attempt and the priority.
- `ctx.fns`, `ctx.handlers`, `ctx.progress`, `ctx.state`, `ctx.form`: typed by the fn's declaration.
- `ctx.done`, `ctx.invalid`, `ctx.fail`, `ctx.pause`, `ctx.cancelled`; `ctx.undoWith`, `ctx.onPause`, `ctx.allow`,
  `ctx.resume`; `ctx.signal` (**Outcomes**).
- `ctx.budget`, for information: `remaining()` (the least money and time left across every budget that applies),
  `limitedBy()`, and `spend(cost, what)` to report a cost the hub can't see (a paid API call). The hub enforces LLM
  spend itself.
- `ctx.expose(fnRef, detail?)`: show the agent another fn with this result (a search showing the fn that opens what it
  found), so it can use it next.

**Services** every run, undo and action gets:

- `ctx.llm`: model calls, always through the hub, which picks the model, enforces budgets and records every call:
  `text({ prompt, system?, model? })`, `object({ prompt, output: ginType })` (an answer checked against a gin type),
  `stream(...)`, `embed(texts)`, `image(...)`, `transcribe(...)`, `speak(...)`. Never call a model provider directly.
- `await ctx.ai()`: the way to use AI beyond that. An `@aeye/ai` instance whose provider is the hub, with the hub's real
  models listed (capabilities, prices, limits) and one `role/<role>` model per hub role, so aeye's selection, tools,
  structured output, prompts, agents, images, speech and embeddings work as usual. Every ctx has it (runs, hooks,
  lifecycle, actions); outside any ctx (a timer, a background job) `hubAi(running)` gives the same, paid by the
  system's own budget. The hub still decides access and budgets and records every call.

  ```ts
  const ai = await ctx.ai();
  const summarize = ai.prompt({
    name: 'summarize',
    description: 'Summarizes a note in one line.',
    content: 'Summarize this note in one line:

{{note}}',
    input: (input: { note: string }) => input,
  });
  const line = await summarize.get('result', { note: args.text }, { metadata: { model: 'role/chat' } });
  ```

  In tests, `fakeHubAi({ models, reply })` (from `@harness/sdk/server/testing`) gives the same type with scripted answers.
- `ctx.files`: file operations the system allows (`read`, `readText`, `write`, `list`, `remove`, and `share(path)` to
  upload to the hub and get a `ContentRef`).
- `ctx.fetch`: `fetch` through the system's outbound rules.
- `ctx.resources`: resources from any system the person may see (`stat`, `text`, `links`, `sections`, `search`).
- `ctx.types.definitions(names)`: named types of any system, with what they reference.
- `ctx.attach(attachmentRef, data, title)` and `ctx.link(link)`: add to the assistant message.
- `ctx.setting(settingRef)`: the effective value of a setting for the run's person.
- `ctx.log`: structured logs, `ctx.log.info('notes.added', { id })`.
- `ctx.instance`, `ctx.dataDir`, `ctx.cacheDir`: which instance this is and its folders (**Config, events, lifecycle and
  data**).

**Everything else** (hooks, mentions, actions, dynamic context, events, lifecycle) gets a `SystemCtx`: the system's
name and current config, `llm`, `resources`, `types`, `log`, a `signal`, and the instance and its folders. Mentions,
actions and dynamic context also get who is asking (`AskerCtx`: `session`, `user`, `time`); actions also get the run
services, the delivery and session state. An undo gets an `UndoCtx`: config, run info, signal and the services.

**Proposed runs and the decision model.** `ctx.propose(fn, args, { preview, approver?, key? })` (`Proposals`, on run
and system contexts) adds a run of one of this system's own fns that waits for approval, with the preview already
worked out: from a run it is that run's child and waits for its person (`approver: 'person'`), from anywhere else
(a schema change found at start) for an admin (`'admins'`). Pass a fn ref for typed args, or a runtime fn's local name
with JSON args. The same `key` (by default a hash of the fn, the args and the proposing run) never makes a second run.
Outside a run a proposal can also wait for one person: `for` names them, and `key` (required then) must start with
the id of a record of this system they own, such as a Claude session, or of a run of this system they started
(`proposalKeyFor(id, what)`; the hub refuses anything else), and `approver` then defaults
to `'person'`. `chat` puts the card in that harness chat too (it is always in the inbox). `ask: { type, into }` makes
the card a form: the approver fills a value of the gin type `type`, and the run goes ahead with it at `args[into]`
(a question Claude asks, an MCP elicitation). The system hears how the run settled through the `run` event
(`events.run`): `done` when approved, `cancelled` when declined, with the approver's words in the status text.
`ctx.decide(question, candidates, { max })` (`Decisions`, runs only) asks the admin's decision model to pick
candidates to reuse (each `{ id, text, score }`); `by: 'off'` or `'failed'` means go by the scores yourself.

### Reference: the run context and services

#### `FnCtx`

interface · `import { FnCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a run gets besides its args. Typed by its fn: only its deps can be called, only its handlers exist, only its
progress steps can be reported, and the outcome helpers take its result, undo and resume types.

```ts
interface FnCtx<T extends FnTypes, Cfg = unknown> extends Services, Proposals, Decisions, Fleets, FnSearch {
  /** The system's config, as it was when this run started. */
  readonly config: Cfg;
  /** Which run this is, whom it acts for, and where it sits in the tree. */
  readonly run: RunInfo;
  readonly budget: BudgetInfo;
  /** Aborts on cancel or timeout. */
  readonly signal: AbortSignal;
  readonly fns: Callers<T['deps']>;
  readonly handlers: HandlersInRun<T['handlers']>;
  readonly progress: ProgressReporter<T['progress']>;
  readonly state: SessionState<T['state']>;
  /** Wait for the run's renderer to submit its form. Only fns that declare a `form` type have it. */
  readonly form: [T['form']] extends [never] ? undefined : () => Promise<T['form']>;
  /**
   * The person's connection this run acts as (a per-person system's fn, `connect` in the contract): its credentials
   * and settings, which the hub picked (the caller's own, else one shared with them). `undefined` otherwise.
   */
  readonly connection: RunConnection | undefined;
  /**
   * The approval this run went ahead on, for a fn with `approval`: who approved it and the preview they saw, so the
   * run can check it does what was approved (an install runs only the hashes on its card). `undefined` otherwise.
   */
  readonly approved: ApprovedRun | undefined;
  /**
   * Hand the hub refreshed credentials for this run's connection, or say they can't be refreshed (`expired`, with
   * why in plain words). Resolves once the hub has them; throws when the run has no connection.
   */
  updateConnection(update: ConnectionUpdate['update']): Promise<void>;
  /**
   * Give a fn of this system (one this run added, a saved form) to the run's person, with who else may use it. They
   * own it from then on and can change that on the fn's page. Resolves once the hub has it.
   */
  shareFn(fn: string, share: ShareRule): Promise<void>;
  /**
   * Which of the named fns of this system the run's person may use now, by the hub's current visibility (groups
   * included). Ask this rather than a copy of who was given a fn: its owner or an admin can change that at any time.
   */
  usableFns(fns: readonly string[]): Promise<string[]>;
  /** Set when this run is resuming after a pause or a hub restart. */
  readonly resume: Resume<T['resume']> | undefined;
  /** Called when the run is asked to pause: return the state to resume from. Only fns with a resume type can pause. */
  onPause(hook: () => T['resume'] | Promise<T['resume']>): void;
  /** Narrow what can be done to this run right now ("no cancelling during the commit"). */
  allow(operations: Partial<AllowedOperations>): void;
  /** Record an undo payload before finishing (after the first irreversible step). The last one wins. */
  undoWith(payload: T['undo']): void;
  done(result: T['result'], extra?: { undo?: T['undo']; cost?: Usd }): Outcome<T>;
  /**
   * The args were wrong in a way the fn's types can't say (a path outside the root, an end before the start): the
   * caller's mistake, not a failure. An agent gets the problems back and tries again; the chat shows the run calmly as
   * "Thinking harder", never as an error. Give a message, or problems by path (a dotted path into the args, `''` for the whole).
   */
  invalid(problems: string | readonly InputProblem[], extra?: { cost?: Usd }): Outcome<T>;
  /**
   * Show the agent another fn with this result (`fs/search` showing `fs/read`): its card at `detail` (default
   * `signature`) follows the tool reply, and it stays near the top of the next selection. Applies when the run is done.
   */
  expose(fn: AnyFnRef, detail?: Detail): void;
  fail(cls: ErrorClass, message: string, extra?: { code?: string; data?: Json; undo?: T['undo']; cost?: Usd }): Outcome<T>;
  pause(state: T['resume'], extra?: { cost?: Usd }): Outcome<T>;
  cancelled(extra?: { partial?: T['result']; undo?: T['undo']; cost?: Usd }): Outcome<T>;
}
```

Refers to: [`AllowedOperations`](#core-allowedoperations), [`ApprovedRun`](#core-approvedrun), [`ConnectionUpdate`](#core-connectionupdate), [`Detail`](#core-detail), [`ErrorClass`](#core-errorclass), [`InputProblem`](#core-inputproblem), [`Json`](#core-json), [`RunConnection`](#core-runconnection), [`RunInfo`](#core-runinfo), [`ShareRule`](#core-sharerule), [`Usd`](#core-usd).

#### `Services`

interface · `import { Services } from '@harness/sdk'`

What every run, undo and action gets from the hub.

```ts
interface Services extends SystemPlace, AiSource, AdminNotices {
  /** LLM calls through the hub, counted against the run's budget. */
  readonly llm: Llm;
  readonly files: Files;
  /**
   * Plain fetch (`serve`'s `fetch` option, else the global one). It checks no address: a process the hub or fs hosts
   * is held to its `grants.network` hosts by its supervisor's proxy, and a system that fetches a URL someone else
   * named (a page, an API service's base URL) checks every hop itself with `addressVerdict` and `followRedirects`.
   */
  readonly fetch: typeof fetch;
  /** Resources from any system the actor may see. */
  readonly resources: Resources;
  /** Named types from any system, with what they reference. */
  readonly types: Types;
  /** Records of stored types, as the run's person. */
  readonly data: Data;
  /** Attach something to the assistant message this run belongs to. The data is checked. */
  attach<A>(attachment: AttachmentRef<QualifiedName, A>, data: A, title: string): void;
  /** Attach a link to a resource, URL, file or run. */
  link(link: Link): void;
  /** The effective value of an actor setting for the run's user (merged with their groups). */
  setting<V>(setting: SettingRef<QualifiedName, V>): Promise<V | undefined>;
  readonly log: Log;
}
```

Refers to: [`Link`](#core-link), [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `SystemCtx`

interface · `import { SystemCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
interface SystemCtx<Cfg = unknown> extends SystemPlace, Proposals, Fleets, FnSearch, AiSource, AdminNotices, InstanceConnects {
  readonly system: string;
  /** The system's current config. */
  readonly config: Cfg;
  readonly llm: Llm;
  readonly resources: Resources;
  readonly types: Types;
  /** Records of stored types, as the system itself (no run, so no person: `person` types are refused). */
  readonly data: Data;
  readonly log: Log;
  readonly signal: AbortSignal;
}
```

#### `SystemPlace`

interface · `import { SystemPlace } from '@harness/sdk'`

Which instance of its system this process is, and the folders it keeps things in. An installed system's folders
are outside its package, so upgrades and rollbacks keep them; `dataDir` is exported with the instance's data.

```ts
interface SystemPlace {
  /** This instance's name: `main`, or its name in a fleet (`laptop`). */
  readonly instance: InstanceName;
  /** The system's own data (files, a SQLite database): what it stores there and how is its own business. */
  readonly dataDir: string;
  /** What can be made again (client builds, indexes): never exported, and may be cleared. */
  readonly cacheDir: string;
}
```

Refers to: [`InstanceName`](#core-instancename).

interface · `import { UndoCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
}
```

#### `BudgetInfo`

interface · `import { BudgetInfo } from '@harness/sdk'`

What is left of the budgets that apply to this turn, for information. The hub enforces LLM spend itself.

```ts
interface BudgetInfo {
  /** The least that is left across every budget that applies. */
  remaining(): { cost: Usd; time: Duration };
  /** Which budget is the tightest right now (`session`, `user`, `group: Design team`). */
  limitedBy(): string;
  /** Report a cost the hub can't see (a paid API call), so budgets stay exact. */
  spend(cost: Usd, what: string): void;
}
```

Refers to: [`Duration`](#core-duration), [`Usd`](#core-usd).

interface · `import { RunStatusView } from '@harness/sdk'`

A live view of a run's status, as a renderer and a caller see it.

```ts
interface RunStatusView {

  readonly allowed: Pick<Operations, 'cancel' | 'pause' | 'background'>;
}
```

Refers to: [`Operations`](#core-operations), [`RunStatus`](#core-runstatus).

#### `Llm`

interface · `import { Llm } from '@harness/sdk'`

Model calls. They always go through the hub, which picks the model, enforces budgets and records every call, of
every modality, as a `ModelCall` attributed to the run.

```ts
interface Llm {
  text(input: { prompt: string; system?: string; model?: ModelChoice }): Promise<string>;
  /** An answer checked against a gin type. */
  object<T>(input: { prompt: string; output: GinType<T>; system?: string; model?: ModelChoice }): Promise<T>;
  stream(input: { prompt: string; system?: string; model?: ModelChoice }): AsyncIterable<string>;
  /** One vector per text, in order. */
  embed(texts: string[]): Promise<number[][]>;
  /** Images made from a prompt, stored with the hub: pass the refs on, or read them with `ctx.resources`. */
  image(input: ModelInput<'image'>): Promise<ModelOutput<'image'>>;
  /** The words spoken in stored audio, with timed segments when the model gives them. */
  transcribe(input: ModelInput<'transcribe'>): Promise<ModelOutput<'transcribe'>>;
  /** Spoken audio of a text, stored with the hub. */
  speak(input: ModelInput<'speech'>): Promise<ModelOutput<'speech'>>;
}
```

Refers to: [`ModelChoice`](#core-modelchoice).

#### `ForwardModality`

type · `import { ForwardModality } from '@harness/sdk'`

The modalities that go through the hub's `model` endpoint (chat has its own, `llm`).

```ts
type ForwardModality = ModelForward['modality'];
```

Refers to: [`ModelForward`](#core-modelforward).

#### `ModelInput`

type · `import { ModelInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a non-chat model call takes, by modality: core's `ModelForward`, without what the runtime fills in.

```ts
type ModelInput<M extends ForwardModality> = Omit<Extract<ModelForward, { modality: M }>, 'modality' | 'run'>;
```

Refers to: [`ModelForward`](#core-modelforward).

#### `ModelOutput`

type · `import { ModelOutput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a non-chat model call gives back, by modality: core's `ModelForwardResult`, with its `ModelCall` id and cost.

```ts
type ModelOutput<M extends ForwardModality> = Omit<Extract<ModelForwardResult, { modality: M }>, 'modality'>;
```

Refers to: [`ModelForwardResult`](#core-modelforwardresult).

interface · `import { Resources } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
interface Resources {

  text(uri: ResourceUri, sections?: SectionKey[]): Promise<string>;

  links(uri: ResourceUri): Promise<ResourceLink[]>;

  /**
   * Search resources by words and meaning. Only in a mention provider's `search`: the hub ties it to the person's `@`
   * search and searches as them. Elsewhere it refuses (`LinkGap`); a fn declares `hub.fns.search_resources` as a dep.
   */
  search(query: string, options?: { roots?: ResourceUri[]; limit?: number }): Promise<ResourceHit[]>;
}
```

```ts
interface Log {
```

#### `Proposals`

interface · `import { Proposals } from '@harness/sdk'`

Adding a run that waits for approval. The run is of one of this system's own fns; the hub makes it, shows the
preview to its approver, and runs it like any queued run once approved. A decline settles it `cancelled` with the
reason. Inside a run the new run is that run's child, in its turn and session.

```ts
interface Proposals {
  /** Propose a run of a fn of this system's contract, with typed args. */
  propose<A>(fn: FnRef<QualifiedName, A, unknown, Handlers>, args: NoInfer<A>, options: ProposeOptions): Promise<ProposedRun>;
  /** Propose a run of a fn this system added at runtime, by local name, with its args as JSON. */
  propose(fn: string, args: Json, options: ProposeOptions): Promise<ProposedRun>;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json), [`ProposedRun`](#core-proposedrun), [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `ProposeOptions`

interface · `import { ProposeOptions } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a proposed run waits on.

```ts
interface ProposeOptions {
  /** What the run would do, as whoever approves it sees it: the SQL, the rows it touches, a type diff. */
  readonly preview: ApprovalPreview;
  /**
   * Who answers: the person of the run proposing it (the default inside a run, and outside one when `for` names
   * them), or an admin (the default outside a run otherwise, such as a schema change found at start).
   */
  readonly approver?: ProposalApprover;
  /** Makes a retried proposal the same run. Default: a hash of the fn, its args and the proposing run. */
  readonly key?: string;
  /**
   * Outside a run, the person the proposal waits for (the owner of a Claude session this system keeps a record of).
   * The hub accepts it only when that person owns a record of one of this system's stored types naming `key`, or a
   * run of it, so pass `key` too (`proposalKeyFor`); without one, `propose` throws.
   */
  readonly for?: ActorId;
  /** The harness chat whose thread shows the card too (it is always in the approver's inbox). */
  readonly chat?: SessionId;
  /**
   * A form the approver fills before approving (a question, an MCP elicitation): its value, checked against
   * `ask.type`, is set as `args[ask.into]` of the run that then goes ahead.
   */
  readonly ask?: ProposalAsk;
}
```

Refers to: [`ActorId`](#core-actorid), [`ApprovalPreview`](#core-approvalpreview), [`ProposalApprover`](#core-proposalapprover), [`ProposalAsk`](#core-proposalask), [`SessionId`](#core-sessionid).

interface · `import { Decisions } from '@harness/sdk'`

Asking the admin's decision model to choose: reuse one of the candidates (a saved query, a saved form) or make

```ts

}
```

interface · `import { DecideOptions } from '@harness/sdk'`

What `decide` takes besides the question and candidates.

```ts
}
```

interface · `import { AiSource } from '@harness/sdk'`

aeye through the hub, in any context: `await ctx.ai()` is an `@aeye/ai` instance whose provider is the hub, with the
hub's models (and a `role/<role>` model per role) listed, so aeye's selection, tools, structured output, prompts,

#### `harnessAiOf`

function · `import { harnessAiOf } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The aeye `AI` behind a context's `llm`, for aeye prompts, tools and agents inside a run: `harnessAiOf(ctx.llm).prompt(...)`.
Its calls go through the hub on the same budget as the rest of `ctx.llm`. Throws for an `Llm` that isn't the
runtime's (a test fake).

```ts
function harnessAiOf(llm: Llm): HarnessAi;
```

#### `HarnessAi`

type · `import { HarnessAi } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The aeye `AI` a system gets from `ctx.ai()` and `hubAi(system)`.

```ts
type HarnessAi = ReturnType<typeof harnessAi>;
```

#### `HarnessProvider`

class · `import { HarnessProvider } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The aeye provider that forwards to the hub.

```ts
class HarnessProvider implements Provider<HarnessProviderConfig> {
  readonly name: "hub";
  readonly config: HarnessProviderConfig;
  constructor(config: HarnessProviderConfig);
  /**
  * The hub's models this system may use, as aeye models, and a stand-in for each role they fill (`role/chat`). Just
  * `hub/auto` when the hub lists none, so a call still reaches the hub, which then picks.
  */
  listModels(): Promise<ModelInfo[]>;
  checkHealth(): Promise<boolean>;
  /**
  * The model choice a call forwards: the one its harness metadata names, else the model aeye selected (a role's
  * stand-in becomes the role's model), else none (`hub/auto`: the hub picks).
  */
  choiceFor(metadata: unknown): ModelChoice | undefined;
  /** Stream one request through the hub. */
  forward(request: Request, model: ModelChoice | undefined, signal?: AbortSignal): AsyncGenerator<Chunk, Response>;
  createStreamer(): <C, M>(request: Request, _context: C, metadata?: M, signal?: AbortSignal) => AsyncGenerator<Chunk, Response>;
  createExecutor(): <C, M>(request: Request, _context: C, metadata?: M, signal?: AbortSignal) => Promise<Response>;
  embed(request: EmbeddingRequest, ctx?: AIContextAny): Promise<EmbeddingResponse>;
  /**
  * Typed answers from a decision model, through the hub (the `decide` role's model unless the call names one). Images
  * don't cross the link yet, so a request with images is refused rather than answered without them.
  */
  decide(request: DecisionRequest, ctx?: AIContextAny): Promise<DecisionResponse>;
  generateImage(request: ImageGenerationRequest, ctx: AIContextAny): Promise<ImageGenerationResponse>;
  transcribe(request: TranscriptionRequest, ctx: AIContextAny): Promise<TranscriptionResponse>;
  speech(request: SpeechRequest, ctx: AIContextAny): Promise<SpeechResponse>;
}
```

Refers to: [`ModelChoice`](#core-modelchoice).

#### `HarnessProviderConfig`

interface · `import { HarnessProviderConfig } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What a forwarding provider acts for: the hub (or a `ModelLink` standing in for it), how content moves to it, and the
run whose budget pays. Outside a run the system's own budget and limits pay, and the call is recorded as the system's.

```ts
interface HarnessProviderConfig {
  readonly hub: HubAccess | ModelLink;
  /** How audio goes up and images and speech come down by content ref. Default: over `hub` (none over a bare `ModelLink`). */
  readonly content?: ContentTransfer;
  readonly run?: RunId;
}
```

Refers to: [`RunId`](#core-runid).

#### `HarnessMetadata`

interface · `import { HarnessMetadata } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

aeye metadata a harness call carries: which model the hub should use.

```ts
interface HarnessMetadata {
  harness?: { model?: ModelChoice };
}
```

Refers to: [`ModelChoice`](#core-modelchoice).

#### `HubLlm`

class · `import { HubLlm } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

`ctx.llm`: a thin helper over the same hub-backed aeye `AI` as `ctx.ai()`, counted against the run's budget.

```ts
class HubLlm implements Llm {
  constructor(config: HarnessProviderConfig, signal?: AbortSignal);
  /** The aeye `AI` these calls go through, its models not listed: `ctx.llm` lets the hub pick unless a call names one. */
  get ai(): AI<AITypesInfer<{}, HarnessMetadata, { hub: HarnessProvider; }, {}>>;
  /** `ctx.ai()`: an aeye `AI` on the same hub, run and budget, with the hub's models listed. */
  listed(): Promise<HarnessAi>;
  text(input: { prompt: string; system?: string; model?: ModelChoice; }): Promise<string>;
  object<T>(input: { prompt: string; output: GinType<T>; system?: string; model?: ModelChoice; }): Promise<T>;
  stream(input: { prompt: string; system?: string; model?: ModelChoice; }): AsyncIterable<string>;
  embed(texts: string[]): Promise<number[][]>;
  image(input: ModelInput<"image">): Promise<ModelOutput<"image">>;
  transcribe(input: ModelInput<"transcribe">): Promise<ModelOutput<"transcribe">>;
  speak(input: ModelInput<"speech">): Promise<ModelOutput<"speech">>;
}
```

Refers to: [`ModelChoice`](#core-modelchoice).

#### `HubLlmError`

class · `import { HubLlmError } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

An LLM forward failed on the hub. `kind` says whose fault: the model, a tool, a budget limit or the provider.

```ts
class HubLlmError extends Error {
  readonly name: "HubLlmError";
  readonly kind: Extract<LlmForwardChunk, { type: 'error' }>['kind'];
  constructor(message: string, kind: Extract<LlmForwardChunk, { type: 'error' }>['kind']);
}
```

Refers to: [`LlmForwardChunk`](#core-llmforwardchunk).

#### `HubModels`

class · `import { HubModels } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Non-chat model calls over the link's `model` endpoint, on the run's budget: embeddings, images, transcription,
speech and decisions. The hub records each as one `ModelCall` and answers with its id and cost; large outputs come by content ref.

```ts
class HubModels {
  constructor(link: ModelLink, run?: RunId);
  embed(input: ModelInput<"embed">, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<ModelOutput<"embed">>;
  image(input: ModelInput<"image">, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<ModelOutput<"image">>;
  transcribe(input: ModelInput<"transcribe">, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<ModelOutput<"transcribe">>;
  speak(input: ModelInput<"speech">, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<ModelOutput<"speech">>;
  decide(input: ModelInput<"decide">, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<ModelOutput<"decide">>;
}
```

Refers to: [`ModelForward`](#core-modelforward), [`ModelForwardResult`](#core-modelforwardresult), [`RunId`](#core-runid).

#### `HUB_MODEL`

const · `import { HUB_MODEL } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The system-side stand-in for whatever model the hub picks: every chat capability and parameter (strict tools
included), so the system's registry never filters; no price; and no strict dialect, because the hub strictifies for
the model it picks and sends back the descriptors it used.

```ts
const HUB_MODEL: ModelInfo;
```

#### `wireRequest`

function · `import { wireRequest } from '@harness/sdk/server'`


Refers to: [`LlmRequest`](#core-llmrequest).

## 22. Calling other fns

A fn calls another fn (of its own system, another system or the hub) by declaring it as a **dep** with its ref, then
calling it through `ctx.fns`. Nothing else can be called, and the args, result and handlers are typed by the ref:

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
// calls other fns through its deps, by ref: its own system's `review` and the hub's `ask_user`
publish: fn({
  description: 'Share a note once the person approves it.',
  group: 'sharing',
  args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
  returns: g.obj({ published: g.bool(), note: g.optional(g.text()) }),
  deps: { review: fns.review, ask: hub.fns.ask_user },
}),
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
publish: {
  async run(args, ctx) {
    // a call is awaitable for its typed result; `key` names it, so a resumed run gets the answer back
    const decision = await ctx.fns.review({ id: args.id }, { key: 'review' });
    if (!decision.approved) { return ctx.done({ published: false, ...(decision.note === undefined ? {} : { note: decision.note }) }); }
    // `settled` never throws: it says how the call ended
    const where = await ctx.fns.ask({ question: 'Where should the note be shared?' }).settled;
    const note = where.status === 'done' ? where.result.answer : undefined;
    return ctx.done({ published: true, ...(note === undefined ? {} : { note }) });
  },
},
```

Every call goes to the hub and is queued like any run, so the hub sees the whole tree and applies permissions,
approvals and budgets. A call returns an `FnCall`, which is awaitable for the result (rejecting with `CallFailed` on
failure or cancel) and is also a handle: `run` (the child run's id), `settled` (never throws: `{ status: 'done',
result } | { status: 'failed', error } | { status: 'cancelled' }`), `cancel()`, `pause()` and `watch(listener)` for live
status and progress.

Options (`CallOptions`): `key` names the call within the run, so a resumed run gets a finished call's result back
instead of calling again (default: the dep name and a counter; give stable keys in fns that pause); `budget` gives the
child less than what is left; `background: true` doesn't wait (the call continues after this run ends); `handlers`
answers the dep's handlers. **A dep with a required handler can't be called without it**: the options with
`{ handlers: { … } }` are required.

A type's method is a dep like any fn: `deps: { reveal: fs.types.Entry.methods.reveal }`, called with the value as
`self`: `await ctx.fns.reveal({ self: args.entry })`.

When the run is paused while waiting on a call, the call rejects with `Suspended`: let it propagate (never catch it in a
general `catch`; rethrow anything you don't handle).

### Reference: calling other fns

#### `Callers`

type · `import { Callers } from '@harness/sdk'`

Callers for a fn's deps, by local name.

```ts
type Callers<D extends Deps> = {
  readonly [K in keyof D]: D[K] extends FnRef<QualifiedName, infer A, infer R, infer H> ? Caller<A, R, H> : never;
};
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `Caller`

type · `import { Caller } from '@harness/sdk'`

Calls a dep. When the dep has required handlers, the options (with those handlers) are required too.

```ts
type Caller<A, R, H extends Handlers> =
HasRequiredHandlers<H> extends false
  ? (args: A, options?: CallOptions<H>) => FnCall<R>
  : (args: A, options: CallOptions<H> & { handlers: HandlerArgs<H> }) => FnCall<R>;
```

#### `CallOptions`

interface · `import { CallOptions } from '@harness/sdk'`

Options for calling a dep.

```ts
interface CallOptions<H extends Handlers> {
  /** Names the call within this run. A resumed run gets a finished call's result back by key. Default: the dep name and a counter. */
  key?: string;
  /** Give the child less than what's left. */
  budget?: { cost?: Usd; time?: Duration };
  /** Don't wait: the call continues even if this run finishes. */
  background?: boolean;
  handlers?: HandlerArgs<H>;
  /** Which instance of a fleet runs the call (`laptop`); absent, the hub picks by the args' resource URIs, the session's pin or the system's default. */
  on?: InstanceName;
}
```

Refers to: [`Duration`](#core-duration), [`InstanceName`](#core-instancename), [`Usd`](#core-usd).

#### `FnCall`

interface · `import { FnCall } from '@harness/sdk'`

A call in flight. Await it for the result; it rejects with `CallFailed` on failure or cancel. When this run is
paused while waiting, it rejects with `Suspended`: let that propagate.

```ts
interface FnCall<R> extends PromiseLike<R> {
  readonly key: string;
  /** Resolves when the hub has created the child run. */
  readonly run: Promise<RunId>;
  /** Like awaiting the call, but never throws. */
  readonly settled: Promise<Settled<R>>;
  cancel(): Promise<void>;
  pause(): Promise<void>;
  /** Live status and progress of the child. */
  watch(listener: (update: RunUpdate) => void): () => void;
}
```

Refers to: [`RunId`](#core-runid), [`RunUpdate`](#core-runupdate).

#### `Settled`

type · `import { Settled } from '@harness/sdk'`

How a call ended, without throwing.

```ts
type Settled<R> =
| { status: 'done'; result: R }
| { status: 'failed'; error: FnError }
| { status: 'cancelled' };
```

Refers to: [`FnError`](#core-fnerror).

#### `CallFailed`

class · `import { CallFailed } from '@harness/sdk'`

Thrown when a called fn fails or is cancelled. `run` is the child run, when the hub reported it.

```ts
class CallFailed extends Error {
  readonly name: "CallFailed";
  readonly run: RunId | undefined;
  readonly error: FnError | { class: 'cancelled' };
  constructor(run: RunId | undefined, error: FnError | { class: 'cancelled' });
}
```

Refers to: [`FnError`](#core-fnerror), [`RunId`](#core-runid).

#### `HandlerFailed`

class · `import { HandlerFailed } from '@harness/sdk'`

Thrown when a handler the run called (`ctx.handlers.confirm(...)`) gave no answer: refused, failed or timed out.

```ts
class HandlerFailed extends Error {
  readonly name: "HandlerFailed";
  readonly handler: string;
  readonly error: FnError;
  constructor(handler: string, error: FnError);
}
```

Refers to: [`FnError`](#core-fnerror).

## 23. Config, events, lifecycle and data

### Config

A system declares what it needs before it can be used as `config: g.obj({ … })` (folders to serve, a database URL, an
API key). An admin sets the value in the hub (or a person does by asking in chat), checked against the type; until a
valid value is set the system shows as needing config and its fns, mentions and hooks are unavailable. The current
value is `ctx.config` in every context, typed; a run keeps the value it started with. Config lives in the hub's
database, never in a file of the system. Mark secrets plainly in the field docs; never log them.

The hub draws the config as a form from its gin type, on the review, system and pairing pages. A system whose config
needs more (a connection test, a picker that lists what the service has) draws its own: `system(name, {
configComponent: { component: 'connection', mode: 'replace' } })` names a component in its client's `configs`
(`client(contract, { configs: { connection: Component } })`), drawn instead of the form (`replace`) or under it
(`beside`). It gets `ConfigView` props: the saved `config`, its gin `type`, `validate(value)` (the problems, one per
line) and `change(value)` to hand the edited config to the page, plus `system` to send its actions. The page saves the
value, and the hub still checks it with the gin type before storing it.

### Events

`events` handles hub events by type, and the system is subscribed to exactly the types it handles: `message` (a chat
message), `run` (a run's status, progress, operations or cost changed), `resources` (resources changed), `system` (a
system connected, disconnected or was removed) and `fns` (fns were added or removed). Each handler is
`async (event, ctx) => void`, with the event typed by its `type`.

### Lifecycle

`lifecycle` handles moments in the system's life, each `async (ctx) => void` with the system context:

- `installed`: the first connect after being paired or installed, with a valid config;
- `started`: every connect with a valid config, once the contract is imported;
- `configured`: the config changed; `ctx.previous` is the value it replaced (or `undefined`). React if it matters
  (watch new folders, reconnect to a new database); runs already going keep their value;
- `stopping`: before it stops, with `ctx.reason` (`restart`, `upgrade`, `shutdown`, `removed`) and `ctx.timeout`;
- `upgraded`: the first start of a new version, with `ctx.from`;
- `uninstalled`: an admin removed it; clean up anything kept outside the hub.

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
lifecycle: {
  started: (ctx) => {
    ctx.log.info('notes.started', { instance: ctx.instance });
    return Promise.resolve();
  },
  configured: (ctx) => {
    ctx.log.info('notes.configured', { defaultTags: ctx.config.defaultTags.length, before: ctx.previous?.defaultTags.length ?? null });
    return Promise.resolve();
  },
},
```

### Data, cache and instances

The hub gives each installed instance its own folders, outside the package, so upgrades and rollbacks keep them:
`<hub data>/systems/<name>/instances/<instance>/{state,data,cache}`. Code reaches them through every context:

- `ctx.dataDir`: what is the system's alone (its keys, a working copy of something it syncs). Data kept for people is
  never here: it is hub records (**Stored types and the data system**). The hub exports this folder with the
  instance's config (`harness system export-data`) and installs it back.
- `ctx.cacheDir`: what can be made again (client builds, indexes). Never exported; may be cleared.
- `ctx.instance`: this instance's name, `main` for a system that runs one copy. Several copies of one system can be
  attached at once (a fleet: `fs@laptop`, `fs@desk`); fn names and refs stay the same, and the hub picks the instance
  for each call. Set `"instances": "many"` in `system.json` for a system meant to run as a fleet.

Open anything kept in `ctx.dataDir` lazily (one per folder) rather than at import time.
Outside the hub (a system started by hand) the folders default to `.harness/systems/<name>/<instance>/…`. Never write
into the system's own source folder at runtime.

### Reference: config, events, lifecycle and data

#### `Lifecycle`

interface · `import { Lifecycle } from '@harness/sdk'`

Moments in a system's life. All optional; each gets the system's context with its current config.

```ts
interface Lifecycle<Cfg> {
  /** The first time it connects after being paired or installed (with a valid config). */
  installed?(ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg>): Promise<void>;
  /** Every time it connects with a valid config and its contract is imported. */
  started?(ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg>): Promise<void>;
  /**
   * Someone changed the config. `previous` is the value it replaced, or undefined when there was none (the first
   * config it gets while already running). Runs already going keep the value they started with.
   */
  configured?(ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg> & { readonly previous: Cfg | undefined }): Promise<void>;
  /** Before it stops. The hub waits up to `timeout`. */
  stopping?(ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg> & { readonly reason: StopReason; readonly timeout: Duration }): Promise<void>;
  /** The first start of a new version; `from` is the version it replaced. */
  upgraded?(ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg> & { readonly from: string }): Promise<void>;
  /** An admin removed it; clean up anything it keeps outside the hub. */
  uninstalled?(ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg>): Promise<void>;
  /**
   * The hub is about to copy `ctx.dataDir` for a data export: flush what is buffered and checkpoint an open database
   * so the copy is whole. The hub waits up to `timeout`; runs keep going.
   */
  exporting?(ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg> & { readonly timeout: Duration }): Promise<void>;
}
```

Refers to: [`Duration`](#core-duration), [`StopReason`](#core-stopreason).

#### `EventHandlers`

type · `import { EventHandlers } from '@harness/sdk'`

Hub events a system handles, by event type. Subscriptions follow the handlers given.

```ts
type EventHandlers<Cfg> = {
  readonly [E in SystemEvent['type']]?: (event: Extract<SystemEvent, { type: E }>, ctx: SystemCtx<Cfg>) => Promise<void>;
};
```

Refers to: [`SystemEvent`](#core-systemevent).

## 24. Remote services: fleets, sign-in and connections per instance

A system that calls a remote service it was set up for (an MCP server) is a fleet: one instance per service, added from
the browser with its config (`instances: "many"` in `system.json`). A system that authors code calling many services
(the `api` system) runs one instance and keeps its services as records instead: see **Connections per key** below. Its sign-in is core's `RemoteAuth` in that config:
`none`, `bearer` (a token), `headers` (named header values) or `oauth`, each `per: 'shared'` (the instance's own config
secrets, the same for everyone) or `per: 'person'` (each person connects their own). List the secret paths in
`configSecrets` (core's `REMOTE_AUTH_SECRETS` under `auth`), so the hub seals them and never shows them again.

**Connections per instance.** Declare `connect` with `scope: 'instance'`: then every fn that runs on the instance acts
as the person, and each instance tells the hub how people connect to it with `ctx.instanceConnect(...)`
(`InstanceConnects`, on the system context and on the running system). Say it in `started` and `configured`; the SDK
keeps the latest and says it again on every reconnect. `instanceConnectOf(label, auth)` works it out from the auth:
OAuth (an admin's sign-in shared with everyone when `per: 'shared'`), a form for a per-person token or headers
(`connectForm`), or nothing to connect. With nothing to connect, calls need no connection; otherwise a person without one
finds the instance's fns unavailable until they connect on the Connections page.

**Connections per key.** Declare `connect` with `scope: 'key'` and `keys: { type: '<system>/<Type>' }`, a stored type of
yours whose records each carry `name` (the key), `label` and `auth` (core's `ConnectKeyRecord`; use `RecordAuth`, the
`TypedAuth` with no secret in it, since a record never holds one). The hub reads the records from its database and
shows a card per key on the Connections page, worked out with `keyConnectOf(label, auth)`: a per-person token or headers
is each person's own form, and a shared one is one admin's form, shared with everyone. A fn acts through its key:
`connectKey: { arg: 'service' }` on a fn that names the key in an arg, or `connectKey` in a kind's description (an api
fn's service): the run gets the person's (or the shared) connection to that key, or none, and then your system says
the key needs one (fail with `code: 'not-connected'`). `connectFinish` gets the `key`; read the shared form with `credentialsFromForm(auth, form, 'any')`.

**Credentials.** A form connection's `connectFinish` reads the filled form with `credentialsFromForm(auth, form)` into
core's `RemoteCredentials` (or a problem to show), which the hub keeps as the person's connection; `accountLabel` names
it without the secret. In a run, `ctx.connection.credentials` carries it back: `readCredentials` reads it and `credentialHeaders`
turns it into the headers to send; `sharedHeaders(auth)` gives the instance's own. OAuth tokens a run refreshes go back
with `ctx.updateConnection`. Never log a secret: `maskHeaders(headers, secretHeaderNames(auth))` is what a trace or a
log may show.

```ts
const auth = ctx.config.auth;
const credentials = ctx.connection === undefined ? undefined : readCredentials(ctx.connection.credentials);
if (usesConnections(auth) && credentials === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', 'Connect to this service first.'); }
const headers = credentials === undefined ? sharedHeaders(auth) : credentialHeaders(credentials);
```

The MCP client itself is `@ayepi/mcp` (`McpClient` over Streamable HTTP, HTTP + SSE or stdio with `spawnStdioClient`
from `@ayepi/mcp/node`, its OAuth client functions, and its server-request handlers for elicitation and roots); read
its `ayepi-mcp.md` before using it.

### Reference: remote services: fleets, sign-in and connections per instance

#### `InstanceConnects`

interface · `import { InstanceConnects } from '@harness/sdk'`

Saying how people connect to this instance of an instance-scoped system (`connect.scope: 'instance'`: an MCP server,
an API service). The hub shows a card per instance on the Connections page, and a fn that runs on the instance is
unavailable to a person without a connection when `flow` is set. Say it at start and whenever the config changes;
the latest is sent again on every reconnect.

```ts
interface InstanceConnects {
  instanceConnect(connect: z.input<typeof InstanceConnect>): Promise<void>;
}
```

Refers to: [`InstanceConnect`](#core-instanceconnect).

#### `instanceConnectOf`

function · `import { instanceConnectOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

How people connect to an instance, for `ctx.instanceConnect`: OAuth (shared through an admin's sign-in when
`per: 'shared'`), a form for a per-person token or headers, or nothing to connect (shared credentials, or none).

```ts
function instanceConnectOf(label: string, auth: RemoteAuth): z.input<typeof InstanceConnect>;
```

Refers to: [`InstanceConnect`](#core-instanceconnect), [`RemoteAuth`](#core-remoteauth).

#### `keyConnectOf`

function · `import { keyConnectOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

How people connect to a key of a key-scoped system (a record, which never holds a secret: an API service of the one
`api` instance), for the hub's Connections page: a per-person token or headers as each person's own form; a shared
token or headers as one admin's form, shared with everyone; OAuth as `instanceConnectOf`; nothing for no auth.

```ts
function keyConnectOf(label: string, auth: RemoteAuth): z.input<typeof InstanceConnect>;
```

Refers to: [`InstanceConnect`](#core-instanceconnect), [`RemoteAuth`](#core-remoteauth).

#### `connectForm`

function · `import { connectForm } from '@harness/sdk'`

The form to connect with a typed token or header values (`FormFor`); `undefined` for any other auth.


Refers to: [`RemoteAuth`](#core-remoteauth).

#### `FormFor`

type · `import { FormFor } from '@harness/sdk'`

Whose typed token or header values a connect form takes: `person`, only a per-person auth's (each person their own);
`any`, a shared one's too (one admin types it for everyone, for a key whose record holds no secret: `keyConnectOf`).

```ts
type FormFor = 'person' | 'any';
```

#### `usesConnections`

function · `import { usesConnections } from '@harness/sdk'`

Whether calls use a person's connection (their own, or the one an admin shares) rather than the instance's config.


#### `credentialsFromForm`

function · `import { credentialsFromForm } from '@harness/sdk'`

The credentials a filled connect form makes, or what is wrong with it.

#### `readCredentials`

function · `import { readCredentials } from '@harness/sdk'`

A connection's credentials, read; `undefined` when they aren't `RemoteCredentials`.

function · `import { credentialHeaders } from '@harness/sdk'`

Refers to: [`RemoteCredentials`](#core-remotecredentials).

#### `sharedHeaders`

function · `import { sharedHeaders } from '@harness/sdk'`

The headers the instance's own config sends: a shared token or shared header values; none otherwise.

```ts
function sharedHeaders(auth: RemoteAuth): Record<string, string>;
```

Refers to: [`RemoteAuth`](#core-remoteauth).

#### `accountLabel`

function · `import { accountLabel } from '@harness/sdk'`

A short label for an account connected with a token or headers ("Token ending 4f2a"), never the secret itself.

```ts
function accountLabel(credentials: RemoteCredentials): string;
```

Refers to: [`RemoteCredentials`](#core-remotecredentials).

#### `secretHeaderNames`

function · `import { secretHeaderNames } from '@harness/sdk'`

Header names a log or a trace must not show: `authorization`, every name a `headers` auth uses, and the usual
key headers. Compare in lower case.

```ts
function secretHeaderNames(auth: RemoteAuth): ReadonlySet<string>;
```

Refers to: [`RemoteAuth`](#core-remoteauth).

#### `maskHeaders`

function · `import { maskHeaders } from '@harness/sdk'`

Headers with the secret ones masked (`••••` and their last four characters), for a trace or a log.

```ts
function maskHeaders(headers: Readonly<Record<string, string>>, secret: ReadonlySet<string>): Record<string, string>;
```

## 25. The client: renderers, inputs, resource views and pages

A system can draw its things in the chat with **Vue 3 components**. `client(contract, renderers)` in `client/index.ts`
(default export) registers them, and each component's props are typed by the contract, so a component with the wrong
props doesn't compile. Props are reactive: a running fn's renderer updates as its progress, state and result come in.
Everything is optional; without a renderer the chat draws a sensible default.

| `renderers` key | One per | Props |
|---|---|---|
| `fns` | fn | `FnViewOf<typeof contract, '<fn>'>`: `run`, `status`, `args`, `result?`, `error?`, `progress?`, `state`, `submit` (fns with a `form`), and `system` |
| `kinds` | kind (used for its fns without their own renderer) | `FnView<UntypedFn> & WithSystem<…>` |
| `results` | `{ fns, kinds }`: a finished run's result, by fn or kind | `ResultViewOf<typeof contract, '<fn>'>` (a kind's: `ResultView<UntypedFn> & WithSystem<…>`): `run`, `fn`, `args`, `result`, `title`, `system` |
| `attachments` | attachment | `AttachmentViewOf<typeof contract, '<attachment>'>`: `data`, `title`, `system` |
| `inputs` | named type | `InputViewOf<typeof contract, '<Type>'>`: `modelValue`, `problems`, `readonly`, `system`; works with `v-model` |
| `mentions` | mention provider | `MentionView<Target> & WithSystem<…>`: `label`, `target`, `type?`, `hover` |
| `resources` | resource format (`markdown`, a system's own) | `ResourceView & WithSystem<…>`: `stat`, `section?`, `forms`, `text(form?)`, `bytes()`; given as a component, or `{ component, mode: 'replace' | 'tab', label? }` |

_From `scripts/docs/example/client/index.ts`:_

```ts
import { client } from '@harness/sdk';
import { notes } from '../shared/index.ts';
import FindView from './FindView.vue';
import NoteCard from './NoteCard.vue';
import NoteInput from './NoteInput.vue';
import NotesPage from './NotesPage.vue';
import ReviewForm from './ReviewForm.vue';
import WordCount from './WordCount.vue';

export default client(notes, {
  fns: { find: FindView, review: ReviewForm },
  attachments: { note: NoteCard },
  inputs: { Note: NoteInput },
  // a tab beside the hub's preview of every markdown resource; `mode: 'replace'` would draw instead of the preview
  resources: { markdown: { component: WordCount, mode: 'tab', label: 'Words' } },
  // the page the contract's `pages` names, at /s/notes/notes
  pages: { NotesPage },
});
```

In a single-file component, type the props with `defineProps<FnViewOf<typeof notes, 'find'>>()` and import the contract
with `import type`, so the renderer bundle doesn't carry it twice:

_From `scripts/docs/example/client/FindView.vue`:_

```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
/**
 * The `find` card: the search, a progress bar while it runs, then the notes found, each with a pin button that sends
 * the `pin` action to the system (it waits in the hub while the system is offline).
 */
import type { FnViewOf } from '@harness/sdk';
import { computed } from 'vue';
import type { notes } from '../shared/index.ts';

// args, result, progress and state are typed by the contract's `find`
const view = defineProps<FnViewOf<typeof notes, 'find'>>();

const searched = computed(() => view.args.query ?? view.state.lastQuery ?? `#${view.args.tag ?? ''}`);
const percent = computed(() => Math.round((view.progress?.fraction ?? 0) * 100));

function pin(id: string, pinned: boolean): void {
  void view.system.send.pin({ id, pinned }).result;
}
</script>

<template>
  <article class="find" :aria-busy="view.status === 'running'">
    <header class="head">Notes matching <strong>{{ searched }}</strong></header>
    <div v-if="view.status === 'running'" class="bar" role="progressbar" :aria-valuenow="percent" aria-valuemin="0" aria-valuemax="100">
      <div class="fill" :style="{ width: `${percent}%` }" />
    </div>
    <ul v-if="view.result" class="list">
      <li v-for="note in view.result.notes" :key="note.id" class="note">
        <span class="title">{{ note.title }}</span>
        <span class="tags">{{ note.tags.join(', ') }}</span>
        <button type="button" class="pin" :disabled="!view.system.online" :aria-pressed="note.pinned" @click="pin(note.id, !note.pinned)">
          {{ note.pinned ? 'Unpin' : 'Pin' }}
        </button>
      </li>
    </ul>
    <p v-if="view.result" class="total">{{ view.result.notes.length }} found{{ view.result.more ? ', more past these' : '' }}</p>
    <p v-if="view.error" class="problem">{{ view.error.message }}</p>
  </article>
</template>

<style scoped>
.find { display: grid; gap: var(--space-2); min-width: 0; }
.head { color: var(--text-dim); font-size: var(--text-sm); }
.bar { height: 4px; border-radius: 2px; background: var(--surface-2); overflow: hidden; }
.fill { height: 100%; background: var(--accent); transition: width var(--fast) var(--ease); }
.list { display: grid; gap: var(--space-1); margin: 0; padding: 0; list-style: none; }
.note { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: var(--space-2); min-width: 0; }
.title { flex: 1; min-width: 0; overflow-wrap: anywhere; }
.tags { color: var(--text-dim); font-size: var(--text-xs); }
.pin { min-height: 28px; border: 1px solid var(--border); border-radius: var(--radius-sm); background: var(--surface); color: var(--text); cursor: pointer; }
.total { margin: 0; color: var(--text-dim); font-size: var(--text-xs); }
.problem { margin: 0; color: var(--danger); }
</style>
```

**Talking back.** `view.system.send.<action>(input)` sends an action and returns a `Delivery` (`id`, live `status`, and
`result`); it waits in the hub while the system is offline, and `view.system.online` says which. `view.system.run.<fn>(args)`
starts a new run of one of the system's fns in the renderer's session, as a person would. A fn with a `form` gets
`view.submit(value)` (the notes `review` form, `client/ReviewForm.vue` in **Worked examples**, calls
`view.submit({ approved, note })` and shows the answer once the run is done).

**Results.** A result renderer draws only what a finished run gave, under the chat's own run line (its status, cost and
args stay the chat's); a fn renderer, which draws the whole run as it goes, wins over it. It may draw with the app's
result view, `inject(RESULT_VIEW)` (`ResultViewProps`: a result table or a widget's result with its chips, visuals, drill
and row actions; `keepLook` lets Settings keep the look picked there), and with the app's controls,
`inject(APP_COMPONENTS)` (the same `SharedComponents` a page has as `host.components`). In a chat, `inject(CHAT_HOST)` asks the agent
(`ask(text, send)`), waits for a run the renderer started to end (`settled(run)` gives a `RunOutcome`) and moves the app
(`navigate(path)`). The widgets system draws a `make_widget` proposal's card with Save this way, and a saved widget's
result by its kind.

**Inputs** are editors (and, read-only, viewers) of a named type, used wherever the chat draws a value of it: `!` forms,
handler prompts, config forms, run cards, mention cards. They work with `v-model`: emit `update:modelValue` with a whole
new value, and show `problems`. With `readonly` the same component only shows the value (the notes input,
`client/NoteInput.vue` in **Worked examples**, draws a card then).

**Resource viewers** are shared by format: a resource is shown with the viewer for its format, whichever system serves
it, and the hub has viewers for the common ones (text, markdown, code, images, PDF, JSON, CSV, folders). A system's
viewer either replaces the hub's preview for its format (`mode: 'replace'`) or adds a tab beside it (`mode: 'tab'`, named
`label`); a bare component replaces. `view.forms` lists the forms the hub made of the resource and `view.text(form)` reads
one (the notes system's `client/WordCount.vue` adds a Words tab to every markdown resource). Inside any
renderer, `inject(RESOURCE_VIEWER)` gives the component that shows any resource (`<ResourceViewer :uri="uri" compact />`).

**Look.** Renderers sit inside the chat, so use its design tokens (`var(--space-2)`, `var(--surface)`, `var(--border)`,
`var(--text-dim)`, `var(--accent)`, `var(--danger)`, `var(--radius)`, `var(--text-sm)`, …) in scoped styles, so they
follow light and dark themes, and keep them responsive (`min-width: 0`, wrapping, no fixed widths).

**Building.** `buildClient({ entry, outDir })` (from `@harness/sdk/build`) bundles `client/index.ts` with Vite, `.vue`
files and CSS included, keeping `vue` and `@harness/sdk` external so renderers share the chat's copies. Pass `outDir`
as `serve`'s `client` option and the system uploads the bundle when it connects; `harness system build` does the same
for a packaged system.

**Visuals for a registry.** A system adds components to a registry (its own or another system's, such as
`hub/visuals`) with `client(contract, { visuals: { 'hub/visuals': { bullet: { component, veto } } } })` (`VisualEntry`).
Each must be listed in the contract's `contributes` with a declared shape (`visual({ shape })`), or `client()` throws:
the shape alone judges and ranks a fit, the same in the hub, the widget agent and the browser. `veto(table, facts)` may
only take a fit away, with a reason; keep it cheap and pure. The chat's runtime lists the visuals a result may use
(`visuals(registry, table, facts)`, in load order), leaving out a vetoed one and one whose veto throws; the result view
ranks them with the built-in ones. How to draw one with ECharts is in **Visuals and widgets**.

**Pages.** A system can add whole pages to the app, each at `/s/<system>/<segment>`. Declare each in the contract's
`pages` (`segment`, `title`, `description?`, `icon?`, `nav`: `main` puts it in the sidebar, `hidden` leaves it for links
such as one item's page; `access`: `everyone` or `admin`; and `component`), and give the component in
`client(contract, { pages: { NotesPage } })`. `client()` throws when a declared page has no component, or a component
no page names. The hub lists a page while its system is installed and live with its renderers on (an admin reviews and
can switch off each page like any component), so the page and its sidebar entry appear and disappear with the system;
an old link to a page whose system is gone keeps its URL and says the system isn't installed.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
// a whole page in the app at /s/notes/notes, in the sidebar while the system is live; client({ pages }) draws it
pages: [{ segment: 'notes', title: 'Notes', description: 'Every note, searched by the hub.', icon: 'book', component: 'NotesPage' }],
```

A page's props are `PageViewOf<typeof contract>`: `page` (its segment), `path` (the URL's parts under it, so
`/s/notes/note/42` gives `['42']`), `system` (its own actions and fns, as every renderer gets) and `host`:

| `host.` | What it gives |
|---|---|
| `user` | the person looking at the page (`id`, `name`, `role`, …) |
| `hub` | the app's typed hub client: `host.hub.caller('records').call({ type: 'notes/Note', search })`; calls run as that person, so the hub's access rules apply, and search, filters and counts run in the hub's database |
| `query` | the URL query, live: `values`, `get(key)`, `set({ key: value }, { push? })` (undefined or '' removes a key) and `bind(key, fallback?)`, a two-way binding for `v-model`. Keep the page's search, filters, tab and open item here, so every state is a URL |
| `href(to)` / `go(to, { replace? })` | links and moves between the system's own pages: `{ page?, path?, query? }` (this page when `page` is left out) |
| `openChat({ title, run? })` | starts a new chat as the person, runs `run` (`{ fn, args }`) there first as the person's own run, and goes to it (the widgets page opens a saved widget this way) |
| `chatHref(session)` / `goChat(session)` | a link to, and a move to, one of the person's chats (an item's own chat) |
| `runs({ title })` | runs the page starts and waits on (`PageRuns`): each `run(fn, args, { on?, waiting? })` is the person's own run in a harness chat (made the first time, named by `title`, or `attach(session)`ed), so approval rules apply and the chat records it; it resolves only once the run settled with its outcome (`{ ok, output }` or `{ ok: false, problem }`, never early on the hub's `done`). The chat's `approvals` (with `argsOf(run)`) are answered with `approve`; `close()` when the page goes |
| `inbox` | the approvals waiting outside any chat, for runs the page knows wait on the person: `load(runs)`, `byRun(run)`, `decide` |
| `notify` / `format` / `live(refresh, every)` | the app's toasts (`ok`, `error`); money and times as the app writes them (`cost`, `relativeTime`, `fullTime`); and keeping the page live (every `every` ms while it is in view, once more when it comes back) |
| `components` | the app's own components, so a page looks like the rest of the app: `Frame` (the page frame: title, description, width, `actions` slot), `Input` (a search box or field), `Button`, `Select`, `Textarea`, `Dialog`, `Banner`, `Badge`, `Skeleton`, `Markdown`, `Icon`, `Empty`, `LoadState`, `FnPill`, `ResourceViewer` and `ValueEditor`; and the chat's own parts: `Reasoning`, `Typing`, `Approval` (an approval card, `inChat` when answered through `runs`), `Conversation` (`ConversationEntry`s drawn as the chat draws a thread: the person's bubbles, answers with reasoning folded, calls as run cards folded into one section) and `Composer` (`v-model`, `@send`, `@stop`). The claude system's pages are drawn this way |

A link a renderer or page builds from data (a URL a system, a model or a web page sent) goes through `safeHref(url)`
first: it gives back the URL when a link may go there (http and https, mail, places in the app, resource URIs) and
`undefined` for `javascript:`, `data:`, `//host` and the like. Show a refused URL as plain text, never as a link.

Components under the page read the same host with `inject(PAGE_HOST)`. The notes system's page searches its notes
in the hub with the words in `?q=`:

_From `scripts/docs/example/client/NotesPage.vue`:_

```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
/**
 * The Notes page (`/s/notes/notes`): every note, searched by the hub (never filtered here), with the search in the URL
 * (`?q=`) so a refresh or a shared link shows the same list. It draws with the app's own frame, search input, load state and markdown,
 * from `host.components`, so it looks like the rest of the app.
 */
import type { PageViewOf } from '@harness/sdk';
import { ref, watch } from 'vue';
import type { NotesContract } from '../shared/index.ts';

const view = defineProps<PageViewOf<NotesContract>>();
const { Frame, Input, LoadState, Markdown } = view.host.components;
// `?q=` bound two ways: typing changes the URL, and the URL fills the box
const search = view.host.query.bind('q');
const records = view.host.hub.caller('records', { lastOnly: true });

const notes = ref<readonly { id: string; title: string; text: string }[]>([]);
const loading = ref(true);
const problem = ref('');

async function load(): Promise<void> {
  loading.value = true;
  try {
    // the hub searches its database: words in the title and text, and by meaning
    const words = search.value.trim();
    const page = await records.call({ type: 'notes/Note', ...(words === '' ? {} : { search: words }) });
    notes.value = page.items.map((record) => {
      const value = record.value;
      const text = typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value) && typeof value['text'] === 'string' ? value['text'] : '';
      return { id: record.id, title: record.title, text };
    });
    problem.value = '';
  } catch (error) {
    problem.value = error instanceof Error ? error.message : 'The notes could not be read.';
  } finally {
    loading.value = false;
  }
}
watch(() => search.value, () => { void load(); }, { immediate: true });
</script>

<template>
  <Frame title="Notes" description="Every note, searched by the hub.">
    <Input v-model="search.value" class="search" type="search" icon="search" label="Search notes" hide-label clearable placeholder="Search notes" />
    <LoadState :loading="loading" :problem="problem" :ready="notes.length > 0" :empty="!loading && notes.length === 0" empty-title="No notes" @retry="load">
      <ul class="notes">
        <li v-for="note in notes" :key="note.id" class="note">
          <h2>{{ note.title }}</h2>
          <Markdown :source="note.text" />
        </li>
      </ul>
    </LoadState>
  </Frame>
</template>

<style scoped>
.search { max-width: 32rem; margin-bottom: var(--space-3); }
.notes { list-style: none; margin: 0; padding: 0; display: grid; gap: var(--space-3); grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(min(100%, 18rem), 1fr)); }
.note { min-width: 0; padding: var(--space-3); border: 1px solid var(--border); border-radius: var(--radius); background: var(--surface); }
.note h2 { margin: 0 0 var(--space-2); font-size: var(--text-md, 1rem); }
</style>
```

### Reference: the client: renderers, inputs, resource views and pages

#### `client`

function · `import { client } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a system's renderers in `client/`.

```ts
function client<N extends string, C extends ContractShape>(contract: SystemContract<N, C>, renderers: ClientRenderers<C>): ClientModule<C>;
```

#### `ClientRenderers`

interface · `import { ClientRenderers } from '@harness/sdk'`

A system's renderers, typed by its contract. Every renderer also gets `system` to send its system actions.

```ts
interface ClientRenderers<C extends ContractShape> {
  /** How calls of a fn are drawn in the chat. */
  fns?: { readonly [K in keyof C['fns']]?: Renderer<FnView<FnTypesOf<C, K>> & WithSystem<C>> };
  /**
   * How a finished run's result is drawn, by fn, or by kind for a fn of a kind with none of its own: only the result,
   * under the app's own run line (a fn renderer, which draws the whole run, wins over these).
   */
  results?: {
    readonly fns?: { readonly [K in keyof C['fns']]?: Renderer<ResultView<FnTypesOf<C, K>> & WithSystem<C>> };
    readonly kinds?: { readonly [K in keyof C['kinds']]?: Renderer<ResultView<UntypedFn> & WithSystem<C>> };
  };
  /** How a fn of a kind is drawn when the fn has no renderer of its own. */
  kinds?: { readonly [K in keyof C['kinds']]?: Renderer<FnView<UntypedFn> & WithSystem<C>> };
  attachments?: { readonly [K in keyof C['attachments']]?: Renderer<AttachmentView<AttachmentData<C, K>> & WithSystem<C>> };
  /** Inputs for the system's named types (used in `!` forms and handler prompts). */
  inputs?: { readonly [K in keyof C['types']]?: Renderer<InputView<ValueOf<C['types'][K]>> & WithSystem<C>> };
  /** Custom hover cards for mentions (otherwise the hub draws the `MentionHover`). */
  mentions?: { readonly [K in keyof C['mentions']]?: Renderer<MentionView<MentionTarget<C, K>> & WithSystem<C>> };
  /**
   * Viewers for resource formats (`markdown`, `csv`, a system's own), shared by format across systems. A bare
   * component replaces the hub's preview; `{ component, mode: 'tab', label }` adds a mode beside it.
   */
  resources?: Readonly<Record<string, BareViewer<Renderer<ResourceView & WithSystem<C>>> | ResourceViewerEntry<Renderer<ResourceView & WithSystem<C>>>>>;
  /**
   * Visuals this system adds to registries, by registry (`hub/visuals`) and name. Each must be listed in
   * the contract's `contributes`, which is how the hub and the web app know to load this bundle for that registry.
   */
  visuals?: Readonly<Record<QualifiedName, Readonly<Record<string, VisualEntry<C>>>>>;
  /**
   * Config components by name. The one the contract's `configComponent` names is drawn for the system's config; it must
   * be here, and every component here must be the one named.
   */
  configs?: Readonly<Record<string, Renderer<ConfigView<Infer<C['config']>> & WithSystem<C>>>>;
  /**
   * Page components by name: each page the contract declares (`system({ pages })`) names one here, and every one here
   * is named by a page.
   */
  pages?: Readonly<Record<string, Renderer<PageView & WithSystem<C>>>>;
}
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `ClientModule`

interface · `import { ClientModule } from '@harness/sdk'`

A built client module: a system's renderers, typed by its contract, and the contract itself (so the chat checks
action inputs and results with the system's own gin types). The chat UI loads it from the hub.

```ts
interface ClientModule<C extends ContractShape = ContractShape> {
  readonly system: string;
  readonly contract: SystemContract<string, C>;
  readonly renderers: ClientRenderers<C>;
}
```

#### `Renderer`

type · `import { Renderer } from '@harness/sdk'`

A renderer is a Vue component whose props are P.

```ts
type Renderer<P> = Component<P>;
```

#### `FnView`

interface · `import { FnView } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of a fn renderer: the run's typed args, result and progress.

```ts
interface FnView<T extends FnTypes> {
  run: RunId;
  status: RunStatus;
  args: T['args'];
  result?: T['result'];
  error?: FnError;
  progress?: ProgressState<T['progress']>;
  /** The fn's session state fields, live. Change them through an action. */
  state: Readonly<Partial<T['state']>>;
  /** Submit the fn's form to the waiting run. Only fns that declare a `form` type have it. */
  submit: [T['form']] extends [never] ? undefined : (value: T['form']) => Delivery<void>;
}
```

Refers to: [`FnError`](#core-fnerror), [`RunId`](#core-runid), [`RunStatus`](#core-runstatus).

#### `FnViewOf`

type · `import { FnViewOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of the renderer for one of a contract's fns: `defineProps<FnViewOf<typeof fs, 'bash'>>()`.

```ts
type FnViewOf<S extends AnyContract, K extends keyof S['defs']['fns']> = FnView<TypesOf<S['defs']['fns'][K]>> & WithSystem<S['defs']>;
```

#### `AttachmentView`

interface · `import { AttachmentView } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of an attachment renderer.

```ts
interface AttachmentView<T> {
  data: T;
  title: string;
}
```

#### `AttachmentViewOf`

type · `import { AttachmentViewOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of one of a contract's attachments.

```ts
type AttachmentViewOf<S extends AnyContract, K extends keyof S['defs']['attachments']> = AttachmentView<Infer<S['defs']['attachments'][K]['data']>> & WithSystem<S['defs']>;
```

#### `InputView`

interface · `import { InputView } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of a custom input. It works with `v-model` (`defineModel<T>()` in a `<script setup>`).

```ts
interface InputView<T> {
  modelValue: T | undefined;
  /** Problems with the current value, from the type's checks. */
  problems: string[];
  /**
   * Draw the value without letting it be changed (a run card's args, a result): the same component is the type's
   * editor and its viewer everywhere a value of the type is drawn.
   */
  readonly: boolean;
}
```

#### `InputViewOf`

type · `import { InputViewOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of the input for one of a contract's types.

```ts
type InputViewOf<S extends AnyContract, K extends keyof S['defs']['types']> = InputView<ValueOf<S['defs']['types'][K]>> & WithSystem<S['defs']>;
```

#### `MentionView`

interface · `import { MentionView } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of a custom mention hover card.

```ts
interface MentionView<T> {
  label: string;
  target: T;
  /** How the mentioned type is shown, if the provider declares one. */
  type?: TypeDisplay;
  /** What the system's `hover` returned; `undefined` when the thing no longer exists. */
  hover: MentionHover | undefined;
}
```

Refers to: [`MentionHover`](#core-mentionhover), [`TypeDisplay`](#core-typedisplay).

#### `ResourceView`

interface · `import { ResourceView } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of a resource viewer.

```ts
interface ResourceView {
  stat: ResourceStat;
  section?: SectionKey;
  /** The forms the hub has made of it (`markdown`, `table`, `description`, …), so a viewer can offer them. */
  forms: readonly FormKind[];
  /** Its text: the raw text, or one of its forms. */
  text(form?: FormKind): Promise<string>;
  bytes(): Promise<Uint8Array>;
}
```

Refers to: [`FormKind`](#core-formkind), [`ResourceStat`](#core-resourcestat), [`SectionKey`](#core-sectionkey).

#### `SystemClient`

interface · `import { SystemClient } from '@harness/sdk'`

What every renderer gets to talk to its own system.

```ts
interface SystemClient<C extends ContractShape> {
  /** Whether the system is connected now. Actions sent while it is offline wait in the hub. */
  readonly online: boolean;
  /** Send an action: `system.send.save({ draft })`. */
  readonly send: { readonly [K in keyof C['actions']]: C['actions'][K] extends ActionDef<infer I, infer R, object> ? (input: I) => Delivery<R> : never };
  /**
   * Run one of the system's fns in the renderer's session, as a person would with `!fn`: `system.run.bash(args)`. The
   * args are checked with the fn's gin type. It resolves with the new run's id once the hub has started it; the run
   * shows in the chat like any other, with its approval, status, cost and undo.
   */
  readonly run: { readonly [K in keyof C['fns']]: (args: FnTypesOf<C, K>['args']) => Promise<RunId> };
}
```

Refers to: [`RunId`](#core-runid).

#### `WithSystem`

interface · `import { WithSystem } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props every renderer of a system gets besides its view.

```ts
interface WithSystem<C extends ContractShape> {
  system: SystemClient<C>;
}
```

#### `Delivery`

interface · `import { Delivery } from '@harness/sdk'`

A message on its way: stored by the hub, delivered when the system is online. Its status is live.

```ts
interface Delivery<R> {
  /** The hub's id for it, once the hub has stored it. */
  readonly id: DeliveryId | undefined;
  readonly status: DeliveryStatus;
  /** Resolves when the system has handled it. */
  readonly result: Promise<R>;
}
```

Refers to: [`DeliveryId`](#core-deliveryid), [`DeliveryStatus`](#core-deliverystatus).

#### `ResourceViewerEntry`

interface · `import { ResourceViewerEntry } from '@harness/sdk'`

A system's viewer for a resource format with how it is offered: `replace` draws instead of the hub's preview for that
format; `tab` adds a mode named `label` beside the hub's own (Preview, Source, the forms).

```ts
interface ResourceViewerEntry<R> {
  readonly component: R;
  readonly mode: ResourceViewerMode;
  /** The mode's name in the viewer's switch. Default: the system's name. */
  readonly label?: string;
}
```

#### `BareViewer`

type · `import { BareViewer } from '@harness/sdk'`

A viewer given as a bare component. Vue's component type takes almost any object, so one shaped like an entry
(`{ component, mode }`) is kept out here and checked as an entry instead.

```ts
type BareViewer<R> = R & { readonly component?: never; readonly mode?: never };
```

#### `ResourceViewerMode`

type · `import { ResourceViewerMode } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type ResourceViewerMode = (typeof RESOURCE_VIEWER_MODES)[number];
```

#### `RESOURCE_VIEWER_MODES`

const · `import { RESOURCE_VIEWER_MODES } from '@harness/sdk'`

How a system's resource viewer sits beside the hub's: instead of its preview, or as one more mode (tab).

```ts
const RESOURCE_VIEWER_MODES: readonly ["replace", "tab"];
```

#### `RESOURCE_VIEWER`

const · `import { RESOURCE_VIEWER } from '@harness/sdk'`

Shows any resource with the viewer for its format, from the hub or any system. The chat provides it under this
key; inside a renderer, `inject(RESOURCE_VIEWER)` gives the component. The key is a global symbol, so a system's
separately built bundle sees the same one.

```ts
const RESOURCE_VIEWER: InjectionKey<Component<ResourceViewerProps>>;
```

#### `ResourceViewerProps`

interface · `import { ResourceViewerProps } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of `ResourceViewer`.

```ts
interface ResourceViewerProps {
  uri: ResourceUri;
  section?: SectionKey;
  /** Show a compact preview instead of the full view. */
  compact?: boolean;
}
```

Refers to: [`ResourceUri`](#core-resourceuri), [`SectionKey`](#core-sectionkey).

#### `INPUT_ANSWERS`

const · `import { INPUT_ANSWERS } from '@harness/sdk'`

Sends a form's answers as a hidden user message (one `input-answer` part, which the chat hides and the model reads
in `<input-answer>` tags). The chat provides it; like `RESOURCE_VIEWER`, the key is a global symbol.

```ts
const INPUT_ANSWERS: InjectionKey<InputAnswers>;
```

#### `VALUE_EDITOR`

const · `import { VALUE_EDITOR } from '@harness/sdk'`

The chat's gin value editor (`v-model` over the JSON value).

```ts
const VALUE_EDITOR: InjectionKey<Component<ValueEditorProps>>;
```

#### `ACCESS_INFO`

const · `import { ACCESS_INFO } from '@harness/sdk'`

Whether the hub has more than one person (a reactive getter), so a renderer offers who may use what it saves.

```ts
const ACCESS_INFO: InjectionKey<Readonly<AccessInfo>>;
```

Refers to: [`AccessInfo`](#core-accessinfo).

#### `SHARE_EDITOR`

const · `import { SHARE_EDITOR } from '@harness/sdk'`

The chat's visibility editor: Only me, Me and chosen people (with the hub's people-and-groups picker), or Everyone,
the same control as everywhere else in the app. The chat provides it on a hub with more than one person.

```ts
const SHARE_EDITOR: InjectionKey<Component<ShareEditorProps>>;
```

#### `ShareEditorProps`

interface · `import { ShareEditorProps } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of the chat's visibility editor, as a renderer binds them (`v-model` over the rule).

```ts
interface ShareEditorProps {
  modelValue: ShareRule;
  /** The editor's label ("Who can use it" when absent). */
  label?: string;
}
```

Refers to: [`ShareRule`](#core-sharerule).

#### `InputAnswer`

type · `import { InputAnswer } from '@harness/sdk'`

A form's answer as it was sent: the run's `input-answer` part, without its type tag.

```ts
type InputAnswer = Omit<Extract<MessagePart, { type: 'input-answer' }>, 'type'>;
```

Refers to: [`MessagePart`](#core-messagepart).

#### `InputAnswers`

interface · `import { InputAnswers } from '@harness/sdk'`

Sending a form's answers, and the answers already sent.

```ts
interface InputAnswers {
  /** Add a user message whose only part is this `input-answer`; rejects with the reason when it couldn't be sent. */
  send(answer: InputAnswer): Promise<void>;
  /** The answer sent for a run's form, if one was (read reactively). */

}
```

Refers to: [`RunId`](#core-runid).

#### `ValueEditorProps`

interface · `import { ValueEditorProps } from '@harness/sdk'`

The props of the chat's gin value editor, as a renderer binds them. `type` is a gin `TypeDef`; the editor reads any
definition (and says when it can't), so it takes `unknown`, which a renderer's `TypeDef` fits.

```ts
interface ValueEditorProps {
  type: unknown;
  modelValue: Json | undefined;
  readonly?: boolean;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json).

#### `buildClient`

function · `import { buildClient } from '@harness/sdk/build'`

Build a system's client bundle; returns it as `serve` would upload it.

```ts
function buildClient(options: BuildClientOptions): Promise<ClientBundle>;
```

#### `BuildClientOptions`

interface · `import { BuildClientOptions } from '@harness/sdk/build'`

What `buildClient` takes.

```ts
interface BuildClientOptions {
  /** The client entry: a module whose default export is `client(contract, renderers)`. */
  entry: string;
  /** Where the bundle goes (emptied first). */
  outDir: string;
  /** Minify the output. Default true. */

  /** Source maps. Default false. */
```
