#### `scripts/docs/example/server/env.ts`

```ts
/** The notes system's tunables, an `@ayepi/env` slice. Code reads them through `notesEnv.parse()` in `main.ts`. */
import { env } from '@ayepi/env';
import { systemFields, systemState } from '@harness/sdk/server';
import { z } from 'zod';

// #region env
export const notesEnv = env({
  ...systemFields(),
  /** The most notes `find` returns when no limit is given. */
  NOTES_FIND_LIMIT: z.number().int().min(1).default(10),
  /** The most pinned titles the `pinned` hook adds to a message. */
  NOTES_HOOK_TITLES: z.number().int().min(1).default(5),
  /** How many notes `tidy` reads per page. */
  NOTES_TIDY_PAGE: z.number().int().min(1).max(200).default(50),
}).add(systemState('notes'));
// #endregion env

/** The notes system's settings, resolved. */
export type NotesSettings = ReturnType<typeof notesEnv.parse>;
```

#### `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The notes system's implementation: every fn, method, kind, dynamic context, hook, mention and action the contract
 * declares. Notes are records of the stored type `Note`, kept and searched by the hub through `ctx.data`: the system
 * keeps no file of notes and never loads them all; every read is a query with a page, and search runs in the hub's
 * database. Its type is computed from the contract: leaving anything out, or adding anything undeclared, doesn't
 * compile.
 */
import { RecordId, type UndoOutcome } from '@harness/core';
import { encodeValue, parseValue, wireType, type Data, type HookContribution, type StoredRecord, type SystemImpl } from '@harness/sdk';
import { NoteEntry, StampArgs, notes, type BeforeValue, type NoteEntryValue, type NoteValue } from '../shared/index.ts';
import type { NotesSettings } from './env.ts';
import type { NotesConfig } from './index.ts';

// #region deps
/** Everything the implementation is built from. */
export interface NotesDeps {
  readonly settings: NotesSettings;
}
// #endregion deps

/** The stored type's ref, which every `ctx.data` call takes. */
const Note = notes.types.Note;

/** Tags as stored: trimmed, lowercase, each once. */
export function cleanTags(tags: readonly string[]): string[] {
  return [...new Set(tags.map((tag) => tag.trim().toLowerCase()).filter((tag) => tag !== ''))];
}

/** A stored note as fns return it: the record's id and time with the note. */
export function entryOf(record: StoredRecord<NoteValue>): NoteEntryValue {
  return { id: record.id, ...record.value, updatedAt: new Date(record.updatedAt) };
}

/** A note as markdown, for context and mentions. */
function markdownOf(note: NoteValue): string {
  return `# ${note.title}\n\n${note.text}\n\n${note.tags.map((tag) => `#${tag}`).join(' ')}`.trimEnd();
}

/** A record id from a fn's text arg; `undefined` when it can't be one (the caller's mistake). */
function recordId(text: string): RecordId | undefined {
  const parsed = RecordId.safeParse(text);
  return parsed.success ? parsed.data : undefined;
}

/** One note by its id, or `undefined` when there is none (or it isn't the asker's). */
async function noteById(data: Data, id: string): Promise<StoredRecord<NoteValue> | undefined> {
  const record = recordId(id);
  return record === undefined ? undefined : await data.get(Note, record);
}

/** The filter for notes with a tag: the hub runs it in its database. */
const tagFilter = (tag: string | undefined) => (tag === undefined ? [] : [{ field: 'tags', op: 'contains' as const, value: tag.toLowerCase() }]);

/** Build the notes implementation. */
export function createNotesImpl(deps: NotesDeps): SystemImpl<(typeof notes)['defs']> {
  const s = deps.settings;

  // #region undo-helper
  /** Put a note back as it was before a change: remove it if it didn't exist, else save it as it was. */
  async function restore(data: Data, before: BeforeValue): Promise<UndoOutcome> {
    const id = recordId(before.id);
    if (before.note === null) {
      return id !== undefined && (await data.remove(Note, id)) ? { status: 'undone', summary: `Removed note ${before.id} again.` } : { status: 'gone' };
    }
    // a note still there is saved over; a removed one comes back as a new record
    const exists = id === undefined ? undefined : await data.get(Note, id);
    await data.save(Note, before.note, exists === undefined ? {} : { id: exists.id });
    return { status: 'undone', summary: `Restored "${before.note.title}".` };
  }
  // #endregion undo-helper

  /** Save a new note with the config's default tags; `undefined` when one with that title exists already. */
  async function create(data: Data, config: NotesConfig, input: { title: string; text: string; tags: readonly string[] }): Promise<NoteEntryValue | undefined> {
    const title = input.title.trim();
    // one query with a filter and a limit of one: never a load of every note to look
    const same = await data.find(Note, { query: { filters: [{ field: 'title', op: 'is', value: title }], limit: 1 } });
    if (same.items.length > 0) { return undefined; }
    return entryOf(await data.save(Note, { title, text: input.text, tags: cleanTags([...config.defaultTags, ...input.tags]), pinned: false }));
  }

  const taken = (title: string) => `There is already a note titled "${title}". Pick another title, or change that note.`;

  return {
    fns: {
      // #region impl-add
      add: {
        async run(args, ctx) {
          const note = await create(ctx.data, ctx.config, { title: args.title, text: args.text, tags: args.tags ?? [] });
          // a real refusal, not a mistake in the args: `fail` with the `caller` class and a stable code
          if (note === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', taken(args.title), { code: 'taken' }); }
          ctx.attach(notes.attachments.note, note, `Added ${note.title}`);
          // the undo payload is typed by the contract's `undo` type
          return ctx.done(note, { undo: { id: note.id, note: null } });
        },
        undo: (payload, ctx) => restore(ctx.data, payload),
      },
      // #endregion impl-add
      // #region impl-find
      find: {
        async run(args, ctx) {
          // `find` declared `state: LastSearch`, so it sees `lastQuery` and nothing else
          const query = args.query ?? (await ctx.state.get()).lastQuery;
          if (query === undefined && args.tag === undefined) {
            // the caller's mistake: an agent gets the problem back and tries again
            return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'query', message: 'Give a query or a tag; there is no earlier search to repeat.' }]);
          }
          ctx.progress.set(0.1, 'Searching');
          // the hub searches by words and meaning and filters by tag in its database; one page comes back
          const limit = args.limit ?? s.NOTES_FIND_LIMIT;
          const page = await ctx.data.find(Note, {
            ...(query === undefined ? {} : { search: query }),
            query: { filters: tagFilter(args.tag), limit },
          });
          if (query !== undefined) { await ctx.state.set({ lastQuery: query }); }
          return ctx.done({ notes: page.items.map(entryOf), more: page.cursor !== undefined });
        },
      },
      // #endregion impl-find
      // #region impl-remove
      remove: {
        async run(args, ctx) {
          const note = await noteById(ctx.data, args.id);
          if (note === undefined) { return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'id', message: `There is no note ${args.id}.` }]); }
          // the effective setting for the run's person, merged with their groups'
          const editing = await ctx.setting(notes.settings.editing);
          if (editing?.confirmRemove === true) {
            // `confirm` is optional, so the compiler makes the fn check it is there
            if (ctx.handlers.confirm === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', 'Removing a note asks first, and this caller cannot answer.', { code: 'denied' }); }
            if (!(await ctx.handlers.confirm({ title: note.value.title }))) { return ctx.cancelled(); }
          }
          await ctx.data.remove(Note, note.id);
          return ctx.done({ removed: true }, { undo: { id: note.id, note: note.value } });
        },
        undo: (payload, ctx) => restore(ctx.data, payload),
      },
      // #endregion impl-remove
      // #region impl-tidy
      tidy: {
        async run(_args, ctx) {
          // a resumed run starts at the page it paused on
          let saved = ctx.resume?.state.saved ?? 0;
          let cursor = ctx.resume?.state.cursor;
          // asked to pause: say where to resume from; the run settles as paused and `ctx.signal` aborts
          ctx.onPause(() => ({ saved, ...(cursor === undefined ? {} : { cursor }) }));
          ctx.progress.step('scan', 'active');
          let merged = 0;
          // a page at a time, in a stable order: every note is seen once and never all held at once
          do {
            const page = await ctx.data.find(Note, { query: { sort: [{ field: 'title', dir: 'asc' }], limit: s.NOTES_TIDY_PAGE, ...(cursor === undefined ? {} : { cursor }) } });
            ctx.progress.step('merge', 'active');
            for (const note of page.items) {
              if (ctx.signal.aborted) { return ctx.cancelled({ partial: { merged } }); }
              const tags = cleanTags(note.value.tags);
              if (tags.join('\n') === note.value.tags.join('\n')) { continue; }
              ctx.progress.step('save', 'active');
              // `expect`: if someone changed the note meanwhile, the save fails instead of losing their change
              await ctx.data.save(Note, { ...note.value, tags }, { id: note.id, expect: note.version });
              merged += 1;
              saved += 1;
            }
            cursor = page.cursor;
          } while (cursor !== undefined);
          ctx.progress.step('scan', 'done');
          ctx.progress.step('merge', 'done', `${merged} changed`);
          ctx.progress.step('save', 'done', `${saved} saved`);
          return ctx.done({ merged });
        },
      },
      // #endregion impl-tidy
      // #region impl-review
      review: {
        async run(args, ctx) {
          const note = await noteById(ctx.data, args.id);
          if (note === undefined) { return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'id', message: `There is no note ${args.id}.` }]); }
          ctx.attach(notes.attachments.note, entryOf(note), note.value.title);
          // waits for the renderer's submit; the hub keeps it if the system is offline meanwhile
          return ctx.done(await ctx.form());
        },
      },
      // #endregion impl-review
      // #region impl-publish
      publish: {
        async run(args, ctx) {
          // a call is awaitable for its typed result; `key` names it, so a resumed run gets the answer back
          const decision = await ctx.fns.review({ id: args.id }, { key: 'review' });
          if (!decision.approved) { return ctx.done({ published: false, ...(decision.note === undefined ? {} : { note: decision.note }) }); }
          // `settled` never throws: it says how the call ended
          const where = await ctx.fns.ask({ question: 'Where should the note be shared?' }).settled;
          const note = where.status === 'done' ? where.result.answer : undefined;
          return ctx.done({ published: true, ...(note === undefined ? {} : { note }) });
        },
      },
      // #endregion impl-publish
    },
    // #region impl-method
    types: {
      NoteEntry: {
        pin: {
          async run(self, args, ctx) {
            const note = await noteById(ctx.data, self.id);
            if (note === undefined) { return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'self', message: `Note ${self.id} no longer exists.` }]); }
            await ctx.data.save(Note, { ...note.value, pinned: args.pinned }, { id: note.id });
            return ctx.done(args.pinned);
          },
        },
      },
    },
    // #endregion impl-method
    // #region impl-kind
    kinds: {
      stamp: {
        // problems beyond the payload's type
        check: (payload) => Promise.resolve(payload.tags.filter((tag) => tag !== tag.toLowerCase()).map((tag) => `Tag "${tag}" must be lowercase.`)),
        // everything else about a stamp fn, worked out from its payload
        describe: (payload) => Promise.resolve({
          args: wireType(StampArgs),
          returns: wireType(NoteEntry.shape),
          deps: [],
          operations: { cancel: true, pause: false, undo: false, background: true },
          // every fn needs an example; a kind works its fns' examples out from the payload
          examples: [{ args: { text: 'Shipped the release' }, note: `Saves a note titled "${payload.title}: Shipped the release".` }],
        }),
        async run(payload, args, ctx) {
          // a kind's args arrive as JSON the hub checked against `describe`; read them with the same gin type
          const input = parseValue(StampArgs, args);
          // titles are unique, so a stamp's note is titled by the stamp and the start of its text
          const title = `${payload.title}: ${input.text.slice(0, 40)}`;
          const note = await create(ctx.data, ctx.config, { title, text: input.text, tags: payload.tags });
          if (note === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', taken(title), { code: 'taken' }); }
          return ctx.done(encodeValue(NoteEntry.shape, note));
        },
      },
    },
    // #endregion impl-kind
    // #region impl-context
    context: {
      // the newest few, by query (newest change first), never all of them
      recent: async (args, ctx) => {
        const page = await ctx.data.find(Note, { query: { filters: tagFilter(args.tag), limit: s.NOTES_FIND_LIMIT } });
        if (page.items.length === 0) { return args.tag === undefined ? 'There are no notes yet.' : `No note has the tag "${args.tag}".`; }
        return page.items.map((note) => markdownOf(note.value)).join('\n\n---\n\n');
      },
    },
    // #endregion impl-context
    // #region impl-hook
    hooks: {
      pinned: async (input, ctx) => {
        // the pinned ones only, filtered by the hub, a few at most
        const pinned = await ctx.data.find(Note, { query: { filters: [{ field: 'pinned', op: 'is', value: true }], limit: s.NOTES_HOOK_TITLES } });
        const blocks: HookContribution[] = [];
        if (pinned.items.length > 0) { blocks.push({ type: 'text', tag: 'pinned-notes', text: `Pinned notes: ${pinned.items.map((note) => `"${note.value.title}"`).join(', ')}.` }); }
        if (/\bnotes?\b/i.test(input.text)) { blocks.push({ type: 'suggest-context', items: [{ context: notes.context.guide, why: 'The message is about notes.' }] }); }
        return blocks;
      },
    },
    // #endregion impl-hook
    // #region impl-mention
    mentions: {
      notes: {
        async search(query, ctx) {
          // the hub's search: by words and meaning, best first, one small page
          const page = await ctx.data.find(Note, { search: query, query: { limit: 20 } });
          return page.items.map((note) => ({ label: note.value.title, hint: note.value.tags.join(', '), icon: 'note', target: { id: note.id } }));
        },
        async hover(target, ctx) {
          const note = await noteById(ctx.data, target.id);
          if (note === undefined) { return undefined; }
          // returning the value as a NoteEntry lets the card offer its `pin` method
          return { title: note.value.title, subtitle: note.value.tags.join(', '), body: note.value.text.slice(0, 280), value: entryOf(note) };
        },
        async llm(target, ctx) {
          const note = await noteById(ctx.data, target.id);
          if (note === undefined) { return `Note ${target.id} no longer exists.`; }
          return `Note "${note.value.title}" (id ${note.id}) in the notes system:\n\n${markdownOf(note.value)}`;
        },
      },
    },
    // #endregion impl-mention
    // #region impl-action
    actions: {
      pin: async (input, ctx) => {
        const note = await noteById(ctx.data, input.id);
        if (note === undefined) { return false; }
        await ctx.data.save(Note, { ...note.value, pinned: input.pinned }, { id: note.id });
        return true;
      },
    },
    // #endregion impl-action
    // #region impl-lifecycle
    lifecycle: {
      started: (ctx) => {
        ctx.log.info('notes.started', { instance: ctx.instance });
        return Promise.resolve();
      },
      configured: (ctx) => {
        ctx.log.info('notes.configured', { defaultTags: ctx.config.defaultTags.length, before: ctx.previous?.defaultTags.length ?? null });
        return Promise.resolve();
      },
    },
    // #endregion impl-lifecycle
  };
}
```

#### `scripts/docs/example/server/index.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The notes system's server: the implementation (`impl.ts`) and the process (`main.ts`). Its notes are records the hub
 * keeps (`ctx.data`), so it has no store of its own: its data folder holds nothing shared, and tests run it against
 * the fake hub's data system.
 */
import type { ConfigOf } from '@harness/sdk';
import type { notes } from '../shared/index.ts';

/** The notes system's config, as every context receives it (`ctx.config`). */
export type NotesConfig = ConfigOf<(typeof notes)['defs']>;

export { notes } from '../shared/index.ts';
export { cleanTags, createNotesImpl, entryOf, type NotesDeps } from './impl.ts';
```

#### `scripts/docs/example/server/main.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The notes system's process: reads its settings, builds the implementation (its notes are hub records), builds its
 * renderers, and serves both to the hub (pairing the first time).
 */
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { buildClient } from '@harness/sdk/build';
import { loggedHost, serve } from '@harness/sdk/server';
import { notes } from '../shared/index.ts';
import { notesEnv } from './env.ts';
import { createNotesImpl } from './impl.ts';

// #region main
const settings = notesEnv.parse();
const { host, log } = loggedHost(notes.name);

const impl = createNotesImpl({ settings });

// the renderers, built into the state folder and uploaded to the hub on connect
const clientFolder = join(settings.HARNESS_SYSTEM_STATE, 'client');
await buildClient({ entry: fileURLToPath(new URL('../client/index.ts', import.meta.url)), outDir: clientFolder });

const running = await serve(notes, impl, {
  hub: settings.HARNESS_HUB,
  state: settings.HARNESS_SYSTEM_STATE,
  client: clientFolder,
  ...(settings.HARNESS_PAIRING_TOKEN === undefined ? {} : { pairingToken: settings.HARNESS_PAIRING_TOKEN }),
}, host);
running.onStatus((status) => { log.info('notes.status', { status }); });

const stop = () => {
  running.stop().finally(() => { process.exit(0); }).catch(() => undefined);
};
process.on('SIGINT', stop);
process.on('SIGTERM', stop);
// #endregion main
```

#### `scripts/docs/example/server/notes.test.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The notes system end to end over the SDK's real link runtime: `testHub` serves it to an in-memory hub (`FakeHub`),
 * which runs fns the way the real hub does, answers `ctx.data` from its in-memory data system and records what the
 * system sent.
 */
import { manifestOf } from '@harness/sdk';
import { testHub, type TestSystem } from '@harness/sdk/testing';
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { notes } from '../shared/index.ts';
import { notesEnv } from './env.ts';
import { cleanTags, createNotesImpl } from './impl.ts';

// #region test
const CONFIG = { defaultTags: ['Mine'] };

let kit: TestSystem<'notes', (typeof notes)['defs']> | undefined;
afterEach(async () => {
  await kit?.stop();
  kit = undefined;
});

/** Serve the notes system to a fake hub and wait until it is ready. */
async function start(): Promise<TestSystem<'notes', (typeof notes)['defs']>> {
  kit = await testHub(notes, createNotesImpl({ settings: notesEnv.parse() }), { config: CONFIG });
  return kit;
}

describe('notes over the link', () => {
  it('declares its stored type and its API groups', async () => {
    const manifest = await manifestOf(notes, { version: '0.1.0' });
    expect(manifest.types.find((type) => type.name === 'Note')?.records).toMatchObject({ shape: 'table', owner: 'shared' });
    expect(manifest.groups?.map((group) => group.name)).toEqual(['notes', 'sharing']);
    expect(manifest.fns.find((fn) => fn.name === 'review')?.group).toBe('sharing');
  });

  it('adds a note as a hub record with the default tags, and refuses a second with the same title', async () => {
    const notesKit = await start();
    const { result, hubCalls } = await notesKit.call('add', { title: 'Plan', text: 'Write the guide.', tags: ['Work'] });
    expect(result).toMatchObject({ title: 'Plan', tags: ['mine', 'work'] });
    expect(result.id).toMatch(/^rec_/);
    expect(hubCalls.attachments).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(notesKit.records('Note').map((record) => record.title)).toEqual(['Plan']);
    const again = await notesKit.run('add', { title: 'Plan', text: 'Again.' });
    expect(again.outcome).toMatchObject({ status: 'failed', error: { class: 'caller', code: 'taken' } });
  });

  it('finds by words and by tag in the hub, and answers nothing to search for as invalid input', async () => {
    const notesKit = await start();
    await notesKit.call('add', { title: 'Plan', text: 'Write the guide.', tags: ['work'] });
    await notesKit.call('add', { title: 'Milk', text: 'Buy oat milk.', tags: ['home'] });
    expect((await notesKit.call('find', { query: 'oat' })).result.notes.map((note) => note.title)).toEqual(['Milk']);
    expect((await notesKit.call('find', { tag: 'work' })).result.notes.map((note) => note.title)).toEqual(['Plan']);
    const outcome = await notesKit.run('find', {});
    expect(outcome.outcome).toMatchObject({ status: 'invalid-input', invalid: { problems: [{ path: 'query' }] } });
  });

  it('tidies tags a page at a time, saving only the notes that change', async () => {
    const notesKit = await start();
    await notesKit.call('add', { title: 'Plan', text: 'x', tags: [] });
    const id = notesKit.records('Note')[0]?.id;
    if (id === undefined) { throw new Error('add saved no record'); }
    // a tag written another way, as another system might have saved it
    notesKit.hub.data.save({ type: 'notes/Note', id, value: { title: 'Plan', text: 'x', tags: ['Work ', 'work'], pinned: false } });
    expect((await notesKit.call('tidy', {})).result).toEqual({ merged: 1 });
    expect(notesKit.records('Note')[0]?.value).toMatchObject({ tags: ['work'] });
  });
});
// #endregion test

describe('tags', () => {
  it('are cleaned: trimmed, lowercase, each once', () => {
    expect(cleanTags([' Work', 'work', '', 'Ideas '])).toEqual(['work', 'ideas']);
  });
});
```

#### `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`

```ts
/**
 * The notes system's contract: titled notes with tags, kept and searched by the hub as records of a
 * stored type (the data system), never in a file of the system's own. It is the authoring guide's worked example, so
 * it uses one of nearly everything a contract can declare: config, a stored type, a named type with a method, a
 * setting, an attachment, a kind, native fns in API groups (undo, handlers, progress, pause and resume, session
 * state, a form), context, a hook, a mention provider, an action and an agent.
 */
import {
  type Infer, type ValueOf, action, agent, arg, attachment, context, fn, g, handler, hook, hub, kind, linear, mention, setting, steps, system, type,
} from '@harness/sdk';

// #region note-type
/**
 * A note: a titled text with tags. A stored type (`records`): the hub keeps the notes, one record each, checks them
 * against this shape, versions every change and searches them, by words in the title and text and by meaning. Notes
 * are shared by everyone who can see the type (`owner: 'shared'`), so hooks and mentions read them too; a type whose
 * records are each one person's (`owner: 'person'`) is read in runs, as that person. Its record carries its id,
 * version and times; the value doesn't.
 */
export const Note = type({
  description: 'A note: a titled text with tags.',
  shape: g.obj({
    title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    text: hub.types.LongText,
    tags: g.list(g.text()),
    pinned: g.bool(),
  }),
  display: { label: 'Note', plural: 'Notes', icon: 'note', color: '#d9a400', title: 'title' },
  records: { shape: 'table', owner: 'shared', search: { text: ['title', 'text'], embed: true } },
});

/** A note as fns return it and cards show it: the stored note with its record id and when it last changed. */
export const NoteEntry = type({
  description: 'A saved note: its record id, when it last changed, and the note.',
  shape: g.obj({
    id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    text: hub.types.LongText,
    tags: g.list(g.text()),
    pinned: g.bool(),
    updatedAt: g.timestamp(),
  }),
  // chips and cards name a note by its title, with its id under it
  display: { label: 'Note', plural: 'Notes', icon: 'note', color: '#d9a400', title: 'title', subtitle: 'id' },
  methods: {
    // offered on a note's mention card; people and code can call it, the LLM can't (`visibility: 'user'`)
    pin: fn({ description: 'Pin or unpin this note.', args: g.obj({ pinned: g.bool() }), returns: g.bool(), visibility: 'user' }),
  },
});
// #endregion note-type

// #region shapes
/** What undo needs to put a note back: its record id and the note as it was, or null when there was none. */
export const Before = g.obj({ id: g.text(), note: g.nullable(Note.shape) });

/** The search `find` ran last in this session, kept in session state so a later `find` can repeat it. */
export const LastSearch = g.obj({ lastQuery: g.text() });

/** A person's answer to `review`: the form its renderer submits and the fn's result. */
export const Review = g.obj({ approved: g.bool(), note: g.optional(g.text()) });

/** The payload of a `notes/stamp` fn: the title and tags every note it adds gets. */
export const Stamp = g.obj({ title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }), tags: g.list(g.text()) });

/** The args of every `notes/stamp` fn: the note's text. */
export const StampArgs = g.obj({ text: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) });
// #endregion shapes

// #region contract
/** The notes system's contract. */
export const notes = system('notes', {
  description: 'Notes: write, find and tidy titled notes with tags.',
  // #region grants
  // notes are hub records (data), find keeps its last query (state), remove reads the editing setting (settings), and
  // share asks the person through hub/ask_user (fns, and that fn in calls); its agent runs on the hub, so it needs no ai
  grants: { hub: ['data', 'fns', 'settings', 'state'], calls: ['hub/ask_user'] },
  // #endregion grants
  // #region groups
  // its API in groups: the hub lists each group's fns under `notes/api.<group>` for a model to walk
  groups: [
    { name: 'notes', title: 'Notes', description: 'Add, find, tidy and remove notes.' },
    { name: 'sharing', title: 'Sharing', description: 'Review and publish a note.' },
  ],
  // #endregion groups
  // #region pages
  // a whole page in the app at /s/notes/notes, in the sidebar while the system is live; client({ pages }) draws it
  pages: [{ segment: 'notes', title: 'Notes', description: 'Every note, searched by the hub.', icon: 'book', component: 'NotesPage' }],
  // #endregion pages
})
  .with({
    // #region config
    // what the system needs before it can be used; an admin sets it in the hub, and `ctx.config` has it, typed
    config: g.obj({
      defaultTags: g.field(g.list(g.text()), { docs: 'Tags every new note gets.' }),
    }),
    // #endregion config
    types: { Note, NoteEntry },
    // #region setting
    settings: {
      editing: setting({
        description: 'Whether removing a note asks first.',
        type: g.obj({ confirmRemove: g.bool() }),
        appliesTo: ['user', 'group'],
        merge: 'most-restrictive',
      }),
    },
    // #endregion setting
    attachments: {
      note: attachment({ description: 'A note, drawn as a card.', data: NoteEntry.shape }),
    },
    // #region kind
    kinds: {
      stamp: kind({
        description: 'A fn that adds a note with a fixed title and tags; its args are the text.',
        applicability: 'Use for quick capture: a journal, an inbox, a standing list.',
        payload: Stamp,
        // declaring or changing a stamp fn at runtime needs no approval: it can only add notes
        approveChanges: false,
      }),
    },
    // #endregion kind
    fns: {
      // #region fn-add
      add: fn({
        description: 'Add a note.',
        instructions: 'Give it a short title. Tags are lowercase words; the config adds its default tags.',
        group: 'notes',
        args: g.obj({
          title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
          text: hub.types.LongText,
          tags: g.optional(g.list(g.text())),
        }),
        returns: NoteEntry.shape,
        // example calls agents see on its card and when they get its args wrong; a wrong example doesn't compile
        examples: [{ args: { title: 'Ideas', text: 'Rename the project, add a dark mode.', tags: ['work'] }, note: 'A short title and lowercase tags.' }],
        // declaring an undo type makes it undoable, and the server must implement `undo`
        undo: Before,
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Adding {args.title}…', many: 'Adding {n} notes…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying adding {args.title}…', many: 'Retrying {n} notes…' },
          done: { one: 'Added {output.title}', many: 'Added {n} notes' },
          failed: { one: 'Adding {args.title} failed', many: '{n} notes failed' },
        },
      }),
      // #endregion fn-add
      // #region fn-find
      find: fn({
        description: 'Find notes by words or meaning in their title or text, or by tag; the best matches first.',
        group: 'notes',
        args: g.obj({
          query: g.optional(g.text()),
          tag: g.optional(g.text()),
          limit: g.optional(g.num({ min: 1, max: 50, whole: true })),
        }),
        returns: g.obj({ notes: g.list(NoteEntry.shape), more: g.bool() }),
        // reads and writes `lastQuery` in the session's state; only fns that declare it see it
        state: LastSearch,
        progress: linear,
        // a read fn: it changes nothing, so policies can let it run without asking
        tags: ['read'],
        examples: [{ args: { query: 'release plan' } }, { args: { tag: 'work', limit: 5 }, note: 'The five newest notes tagged work.' }],
      }),
      // #endregion fn-find
      // #region fn-remove
      remove: fn({
        description: 'Delete a note.',
        group: 'notes',
        args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
        returns: g.obj({ removed: g.bool() }),
        undo: Before,
        handlers: {
          // optional: a caller that can't answer may still call; the fn must check before calling it
          confirm: handler({ description: 'Delete this note?', args: g.obj({ title: g.text() }), returns: g.bool(), required: false }),
        },
      }),
      // #endregion fn-remove
      // #region fn-tidy
      tidy: fn({
        description: 'Merge tags that differ only in case or spaces, across every note, a page at a time.',
        group: 'notes',
        args: g.obj({}),
        returns: g.obj({ merged: g.num({ min: 0, whole: true }) }),
        progress: steps({ scan: 'Read every note', merge: 'Merge the tags', save: 'Save the notes' }),
        // declaring a resume type makes it pausable: it returns this when paused and gets it back on resume
        resume: g.obj({ saved: g.num({ min: 0, whole: true }), cursor: g.optional(g.text()) }),
      }),
      // #endregion fn-tidy
      // #region fn-review
      review: fn({
        description: 'Ask the person to approve a note before it is shared. Returns their decision.',
        group: 'sharing',
        args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
        returns: Review,
        // the run waits in `await ctx.form()` until the renderer submits this
        form: Review,
        // once answered, the card turns into a plain message
        display: 'message',
      }),
      // #endregion fn-review
    },
    // #region hook
    hooks: { pinned: hook({ description: 'Adds the titles of pinned notes to each message.', order: 60, timeout: 300 }) },
    // #endregion hook
    // #region action
    actions: {
      pin: action({ description: 'Pin or unpin a note from its card.', input: g.obj({ id: g.text(), pinned: g.bool() }), returns: g.bool() }),
    },
    // #endregion action
  })
  // #region with-refs
  // declarations that use refs declared above go in a later `.with`, given a function of the refs so far
  .with(({ fns, types, kinds }) => ({
    // #region context-decl
    context: {
      guide: context({
        description: 'How to keep notes here: search before adding, short titles, lowercase tags.',
        content: 'Search with notes/find before adding a note, so you do not add one twice. Keep titles short and tags lowercase.',
        fns: [fns.find, fns.add],
        // for the graph contextor: how much it counts, a short form for tight budgets, and what it relates to
        weight: 1.2,
        summary: 'Search before adding; short titles; lowercase tags.',
        links: [{ to: types.Note, relation: 'about' }, { to: fns.tidy, relation: 'suggests', weight: 0.3 }],
      }),
      // no `content`: dynamic, so the server makes the text; the command is `/notes [tag]`
      recent: context({
        description: 'The newest notes, optionally with one tag.',
        command: { name: 'notes', args: { tag: arg('Only notes with this tag', { required: false }) } },
      }),
    },
    // #endregion context-decl
    // #region mention-decl
    mentions: {
      // `@` in the chat finds notes; chips and cards show them as Note (its label, icon, color and its pin method)
      notes: mention({ title: 'Notes', description: 'Any note, by its title.', target: g.obj({ id: g.text() }), of: types.NoteEntry }),
    },
    // #endregion mention-decl
    fns: {
      // a fn of the stamp kind: only its payload; the kind's `describe` works out the rest
      inbox: kinds.stamp.fn({
        description: 'Capture a thought in the inbox.',
        payload: { title: 'Inbox', tags: ['inbox'] },
        args: StampArgs,
        returns: NoteEntry.shape,
        group: 'notes',
      }),
    },
  }))
  // #region agent
  .with(({ fns, context: ctx }) => ({
    fns: {
      // an agent: a fn of the hub's `llm` kind with instructions, tools and context
      librarian: agent({
        description: 'Answers questions from the notes, quoting their titles.',
        args: g.obj({ question: g.text() }),
        returns: g.text(),
        instructions: 'Answer {{args.question}} from the notes. Quote the titles you used. Say so when no note covers it.',
        tools: [fns.find, hub.fns.ask_user],
        context: [ctx.guide],
      }),
      // #region fn-publish
      // calls other fns through its deps, by ref: its own system's `review` and the hub's `ask_user`
      publish: fn({
        description: 'Share a note once the person approves it.',
        group: 'sharing',
        args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
        returns: g.obj({ published: g.bool(), note: g.optional(g.text()) }),
        deps: { review: fns.review, ask: hub.fns.ask_user },
      }),
      // #endregion fn-publish
    },
  }));
  // #endregion agent
// #endregion with-refs
// #endregion contract

// #region value-types
/** The notes contract's type. */
export type NotesContract = typeof notes;
/** A note, as it is stored. */
export type NoteValue = ValueOf<typeof Note>;
/** A saved note, as fns return it. */
export type NoteEntryValue = ValueOf<typeof NoteEntry>;
/** What undo needs to put a note back. */
export type BeforeValue = Infer<typeof Before>;
/** A person's answer to `review`. */
export type ReviewValue = Infer<typeof Review>;
/** A stamp fn's payload. */
export type StampValue = Infer<typeof Stamp>;
// #endregion value-types
```

```json
  "server": {
    "entry": "server/main.ts",
    "external": {}

}
```

### The fs system's contract

The fs system (`systems/fs`) gives agents files and a shell over chosen folders. Its contract is the reference for a
real system's declarations: a named type with a method, a setting, an attachment, fns with status words, undo, state,
progress and handlers, a hook, a mention provider, static and dynamic context, and an agent.

#### `systems/fs/shared/index.ts`, lines 1-189

```ts
/**
 * The fs system's contract (`shared/`): files and a shell over chosen folders, served as resources, a restart so
 * changes to its own code go live, and hosting: systems written here are installed from the chat, run on this machine
 * from a bundle an admin approved, and supervised. Other systems import this to call its fns with typed refs; its server
 * implements it and its client draws it.
 */
import { ChangeSet, SensitivityRule } from '@harness/core';
import { type Infer, type ValueOf, agent, agents, arg, attachment, context, fn, ginAs, g, handler, hook, hub, linear, mention, setting, system, type } from '@harness/sdk';
import { HostedState, HostedStatus, InstallArgs, InstallResult, RestartArgs, StatusArgs, StopArgs, StopResult } from './hosting.ts';

export * from './hosting.ts';

/** A file or folder under one of the roots. */
export const Entry = type({
  description: 'A file or folder under one of the roots.',
  shape: g.obj({
    path: g.text(),
    type: g.enum(['file', 'folder', 'link']),
    size: g.num({ min: 0, whole: true }),
    modifiedAt: g.timestamp(),
  }),
  display: { label: 'File', plural: 'Files', icon: 'file', color: '#8a8f98', title: 'path', subtitle: 'type' },
  methods: {
    // offered on a file's mention card; people and code can call it, the LLM can't (visibility)
    reveal: fn({
      description: 'Show it in the file manager.',
      instructions: 'Opens the system\'s file manager at the file or folder, on the machine fs runs on. True when the file manager opened.',
      args: g.obj({}),
      returns: g.bool(),
      examples: [{ args: {}, result: true }],
      effects: { mode: 'read', free: true },
      visibility: 'user',
    }),
  },
});

/** The shell's session state: the working folder `cd` sets and `bash` uses. */
export const Shell = g.obj({ cwd: g.text() });

/** What undo needs to put a file back: its earlier content, or nothing if it didn't exist. */
export const Previous = g.obj({ path: g.text(), previous: g.nullable(hub.types.ContentRef) });

/** One folder the system serves and works in. */
export const Root = g.obj({
  path: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
  name: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
  writable: g.bool(),
  index: g.enum(['all', 'on-read', 'none']),
});

/** How many undo copies fs keeps: per file (the newest), and in all (the oldest go first). */
export const SnapshotLimits = g.obj({
  perFile: g.field(g.num({ min: 1, whole: true }), { docs: 'Undo copies kept per file.' }),
  totalMb: g.field(g.num({ min: 1, whole: true }), { docs: 'Most space all undo copies use, in MB.' }),
});

/** What a command printed and how it ended. */
export const CommandResult = g.obj({ exitCode: g.num({ whole: true }), stdout: g.text(), stderr: g.text() });

/** What a command changed: the files, with a diff for each small text file in git (core `changes.ts`). */
export const Changes = ginAs(ChangeSet);

/** What `bash` returns: what the command printed, how it ended, and the files it changed when they could be found. */
export const BashResult = g.obj({ exitCode: g.num({ whole: true }), stdout: g.text(), stderr: g.text(), changes: g.optional(Changes) });

/** How many sensitive files a listing or search left out (`server/sensitive.ts`). */
const hidden = () => g.field(g.optional(g.num({ min: 0, whole: true })), { docs: 'Sensitive files left out (gitignored, env files, keys, credentials). Read one by its path if the task needs it; the person is asked first.' });

/** The shell setting: whether commands may run at all, and which command prefixes run without asking. */
export const ShellPolicy = g.obj({ allowed: g.bool(), safe: g.list(g.text()) });

/** The diff attachment: a file's text before and after a change. */
export const DiffData = g.obj({ path: g.text(), before: g.text(), after: g.text() });

/** The files mention's target: a root by name and a path inside it, with `/` separators. */
export const FileTarget = g.obj({ root: g.text(), path: g.text() });

/** How fns take a path: absolute inside a root, or relative to the first root (or to a root named first). */
const PATH_DOCS = 'A path inside one of the folders this system serves (its roots): relative to the first root (`docs/a.md`), starting with a root\'s name (`<root name>/docs/a.md`), or absolute inside a root. `/` and folders outside the roots are refused; `.` is the first root.';
const path = () => g.field(g.text({ minLength: 1 }), { docs: PATH_DOCS });

/** The shortest timeout `bash` accepts, in milliseconds; a shorter one is refused as invalid input. */
export const COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MIN = 1_000;

/** The fs system's contract. */
export const fs = system('fs', {
  description: 'Files and a shell over chosen folders, served as resources. Can restart itself.',
  about: [
    'Files and a shell over a few folders of the person\'s machine that an admin chose (its roots); nothing outside them can be reached, and `/` is not a root: paths are relative to the first root (`notes/todo.md`) or start with a root\'s name.',
    'Its fns read, list and search files (fs/read, fs/list, fs/glob, fs/grep), change them (fs/write, fs/edit, fs/remove: each may ask the person first, and each can be undone) and run bash commands (fs/bash, fs/cd).',
    'Reach for it to look at or change the person\'s files, and to keep a lasting document they can open later (a note, a list, a summary) as a markdown file. It defines no fn kinds.',
    'A file the person attached to a chat message is not an fs file: it is a hub resource, read with the hub\'s resource fns. Its fns for installing and hosting systems (fs/install_system, fs/system_status, fs/restart_system, fs/stop_system, fs/restart) are for admins and system authors.',
    'Load its guide (fs/guide) before the first file change.',
  ].join(' '),
  // its authoring agents use models; installs wait for an admin and pair what they start; it keeps each person's cwd,
  // reads the shell setting, serves and announces resources, and shares the fns it writes
  grants: { hub: ['ai', 'approvals', 'fns', 'state', 'settings', 'resources', 'search'], calls: ['hub/search_resources'] },
  // its API groups: the hub indexes its fns by them (`fs/api`, `fs/api.<group>`)
  groups: [
    { name: 'files', title: 'Files', description: 'Read, write, edit, delete and search files under the roots, and ask questions about the code.' },
    { name: 'shell', title: 'Shell', description: 'Run commands (builds, tests, git) in a working folder; each command lists the files it changed.' },
    { name: 'systems', title: 'Systems', description: 'Install, check, restart and stop the systems this machine hosts, and restart fs itself.' },
  ],
})
  .with({
    // set by an admin in the hub; the server gets it typed when it starts
    config: g.obj({
      // the folders it serves and works in; nothing outside them is reachable
      roots: g.list(Root),
      // its own source folder, so it can change itself and restart
      self: g.optional(g.text()),
      // which files are sensitive and what reads and writes of them do (server/sensitive.ts); left out: the defaults
      // (gitignored files, env files but the example, keys, credentials, tokens and passwords in content, and writes of
      // files that lead to running code); [] trusts all but writes into .git, which always ask
      sensitive: g.optional(g.list(ginAs(SensitivityRule))),
      // before build 8: globs of files never served as resources (`!` serves one again); turned into rules when
      // `sensitive` is absent
      secrets: g.optional(g.list(g.text())),
      // the env variables bash commands get besides PATH, home, temp and locale (server/shell.ts `commandEnv`); left
      // out: none, so fs's own keys never reach a command
      commandEnv: g.field(g.optional(g.list(g.text({ minLength: 1 }))), { docs: 'Env variables commands also get, by name. Others are never passed on.' }),
      // how many undo copies fs keeps (server/snapshots.ts); left out: SNAPSHOT_DEFAULTS
      snapshots: g.field(g.optional(SnapshotLimits), { docs: 'How many undo copies to keep per file, and how much space they may use in all.' }),
    }),
    types: { Entry },
    settings: {
      shell: setting({
        description: 'Which commands run without asking.',
        type: ShellPolicy,
        appliesTo: ['user', 'group'],
        merge: 'most-restrictive',
      }),
    },
    attachments: {
      diff: attachment({ description: 'A change to a file, drawn as a diff.', data: DiffData }),
    },
    fns: {
      read: fn({
        group: 'files',
        description: 'Read a text file under the roots, whole or a window of its lines. Gives its text, how many lines it has, and whether more follows.',
        instructions: 'Read a file before you change it or say what is in it; never describe a file you have not read. Paths are relative to the first root (`src/app.ts`) or start with a root\'s name when there are several. For a long file read a window with `offset` (the first line, counted from 0) and `limit` (how many lines); `truncated: true` means more follows, and `partial` says which lines are here, how many were left out and how to read the rest: read on before you say what the rest holds. Binary files (PDFs, images) are refused: read those with hub/resource_read or hub/resource_send. A file attached to a chat message is not here: it is a hub resource.',
        examples: [
          { args: { path: 'README.md' }, note: 'The whole file.' },
          { args: { path: 'src/server/main.ts', offset: 120, limit: 60 }, note: 'Lines 121 to 180 of a long file.' },
        ],
        effects: { mode: 'read', free: true },
        // the hub asks which sensitive files a call touches first (server/sensitive.ts)
        flags: true,
        args: g.obj({ path: path(), offset: g.optional(g.num({ min: 0, whole: true })), limit: g.optional(g.num({ min: 1, whole: true })) }),
        returns: g.obj({ text: g.text(), lines: g.num(), truncated: g.bool(), partial: g.optional(g.text()) }),
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Reading {args.path}…', many: 'Reading {n} files…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying reading {args.path}…', many: 'Retrying {n} file reads…' },
          done: { one: 'Read {args.path}', many: 'Read {n} files' },
          failed: { one: 'Reading {args.path} failed', many: '{n} file reads failed' },
        },
        tags: ['read'],
      }),
      write: fn({
        group: 'files',
        description: 'Write a whole file under the roots, creating it (and its folders) if needed. Gives whether it was created or replaced.',
        instructions: 'Use it for a new file (a note, a list, a summary the person wants to keep as a document), or to replace a whole small file. To change part of an existing file use fs/edit, so the rest stays as it is. It may ask the person first; the old content is kept, so the write can be undone. Tell the person the path you wrote, so they can find it.',
        examples: [{ args: { path: 'notes/todo.md', text: '# To do\n\n- Write the tests\n' }, result: { created: true }, note: 'A new file.' }],
        effects: { mode: 'write', free: true },
        // the hub asks which sensitive files a call touches first (server/sensitive.ts)
        flags: true,
        args: g.obj({ path: path(), text: hub.types.LongText }),
        // two changes to one file in the same step run in the model's order (server/impl.ts fileClaims)
        dynamicClaims: true,
        returns: g.obj({ created: g.bool() }),
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Writing {args.path}…', many: 'Writing {n} files…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying writing {args.path}…', many: 'Retrying {n} file writes…' },
          done: { one: 'Wrote {args.path}', many: 'Wrote {n} files' },
          failed: { one: 'Writing {args.path} failed', many: '{n} file writes failed' },
        },
        undo: Previous,
      }),
      edit: fn({
        group: 'files',
        description: 'Change part of a file: replace exact text with new text. Gives how many places were replaced.',
        instructions: 'Read the file first (fs/read) and copy `find` exactly as it is there, spaces and line breaks included, with enough around it to be unique; the edit fails when it matches nothing or (without `all`) more than once. Set `all` to replace every match (renaming a word). It can be undone.',
        examples: [
          { args: { path: 'src/config.ts', find: 'timeout: 30_000,', replace: 'timeout: 60_000,' }, result: { replacements: 1 } },
          { args: { path: 'docs/guide.md', find: 'colour', replace: 'color', all: true }, result: { replacements: 4 }, note: 'Every match.' },
        ],
        effects: { mode: 'write', free: true },
        // the hub asks which sensitive files a call touches first (server/sensitive.ts)
        flags: true,
```

#### `systems/fs/shared/index.ts`, lines 190-370

```ts
        args: g.obj({ path: path(), find: g.text({ minLength: 1 }), replace: g.text(), all: g.optional(g.bool()) }),
        // two changes to one file in the same step run in the model's order (server/impl.ts fileClaims)
        dynamicClaims: true,
        returns: g.obj({ replacements: g.num() }),
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Editing {args.path}…', many: 'Editing {n} files…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying the edit to {args.path}…', many: 'Retrying {n} edits…' },
          done: { one: 'Edited {args.path}', many: 'Edited {n} files' },
          failed: { one: 'Editing {args.path} failed', many: '{n} edits failed' },
        },
        undo: Previous,
      }),
      list: fn({
        group: 'files',
        description: 'List what a folder under the roots holds: each file and folder with its type, size and when it changed.',
        instructions: 'Use it to see what a folder holds before guessing names. `.` is the first root; `/` is not a folder you can list (it is outside the roots), so start from `.` or a root\'s name. `depth` 2 or more lists inside subfolders too (up to 5); to find files by name anywhere use fs/glob. `hidden` counts sensitive files left out.',
        examples: [{ args: { path: '.' }, note: 'The root\'s top level.' }, { args: { path: 'src', depth: 2 } }],
        effects: { mode: 'read', free: true },
        // the hub asks which sensitive files a call touches first (server/sensitive.ts)
        flags: true,
        args: g.obj({ path: path(), depth: g.optional(g.num({ min: 1, max: 5, whole: true })) }),
        returns: g.obj({ entries: g.list(Entry.shape), hidden: hidden() }),
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Listing {args.path}…', many: 'Listing {n} folders…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying listing {args.path}…', many: 'Retrying {n} folder lists…' },
          done: { one: 'Listed {args.path}', many: 'Listed {n} folders' },
          failed: { one: 'Listing {args.path} failed', many: '{n} folder lists failed' },
        },
        tags: ['read'],
      }),
      glob: fn({
        group: 'files',
        description: 'Find files by name or path with a glob pattern (`*.md`, `src/**/*.ts`). Gives the matching paths.',
        instructions: 'Use it to find files by name or path when you don\'t know where they are. The pattern is relative to the root (every root when none is given); a pattern without `/`, like `*.md`, matches file names at any depth; `**` matches any folders. Folders the system ignores (node_modules, .git) are left out.',
        examples: [
          { args: { pattern: '*.md' }, note: 'Every markdown file.' },
          { args: { pattern: 'src/**/*.test.ts', root: 'app' }, note: 'Tests under src in one root.' },
        ],
        effects: { mode: 'read', free: true },
        // the hub asks which sensitive files a call touches first (server/sensitive.ts)
        flags: true,
        args: g.obj({ pattern: g.text(), root: g.optional(g.text()) }),
        returns: g.obj({ paths: g.list(g.text()), hidden: hidden() }),
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Finding {args.pattern}…', many: 'Running {n} path searches…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying the search for {args.pattern}…', many: 'Retrying {n} path searches…' },
          done: { one: 'Found {args.pattern}', many: 'Ran {n} path searches' },
          failed: { one: 'Finding {args.pattern} failed', many: '{n} path searches failed' },
        },
        tags: ['read'],
      }),
      grep: fn({
        group: 'files',
        description: 'Search the text inside files for a regular expression, line by line. Gives each matching line with its path and line number.',
        instructions: 'Use it to find which files mention something before reading them. The pattern is a JavaScript regular expression (the syntax of `new RegExp(pattern)`, no flags), tested on each line: escape `.`, `(`, `?` and the like with a backslash. It is case-sensitive, and inline flags such as `(?i)` are invalid in this syntax: for either case write a character class (`[Gg]rocer`) or both spellings (`Milk|milk`). A match never spans two lines. Narrow big searches with `glob` (`*.md`, `src/**/*.vue`) and `limit`. Each match gives the path and line, so read around it next with fs/read.',
        examples: [
          { args: { pattern: 'function createHub\\(', glob: '*.ts' }, note: 'Where a function is defined.' },
          { args: { pattern: 'TODO|FIXME', glob: 'src/**', limit: 50 } },
          { args: { pattern: '[Ff]lour', glob: '*.md' }, note: 'Either case: a character class, since `(?i)` is not allowed.' },
        ],
        effects: { mode: 'read', free: true },
        // the hub asks which sensitive files a call touches first (server/sensitive.ts)
        flags: true,
        args: g.obj({ pattern: g.text(), root: g.optional(g.text()), glob: g.optional(g.text()), limit: g.optional(g.num({ min: 1, whole: true })) }),
        returns: g.obj({ matches: g.list(g.obj({ path: g.text(), line: g.num(), text: g.text() })), hidden: hidden() }),
        progress: linear,
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Searching for {args.pattern}…', many: 'Running {n} searches…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying the search for {args.pattern}…', many: 'Retrying {n} searches…' },
          done: { one: 'Searched for {args.pattern}', many: 'Ran {n} searches' },
          failed: { one: 'Searching for {args.pattern} failed', many: '{n} searches failed' },
        },
        tags: ['read'],
      }),
      cd: fn({
        group: 'shell',
        description: 'Set the working folder that later fs/bash commands in this chat run in. Gives the folder now set.',
        instructions: 'Set it once instead of passing `cwd` to every fs/bash call; it lasts for this chat (undoing the turn puts it back). The folder must be inside a root.',
        examples: [{ args: { path: 'packages/web' }, result: { cwd: 'packages/web' } }],
        effects: { mode: 'read', free: true },
        args: g.obj({ path: path() }),
        returns: g.obj({ cwd: g.text() }),
        // shared with bash through session state; the LLM never has to pass it around
        state: Shell,
        display: 'hidden',
      }),
      bash: fn({
        group: 'shell',
        description: 'Run a bash command on the machine fs runs on, in the working folder set with fs/cd unless `cwd` is given. Gives its exit code, what it printed (stdout, stderr) and the files it changed.',
        instructions: 'The shell is bash (Git Bash on Windows when it is installed), not PowerShell or cmd: write POSIX commands (`ls`, `grep`, `&&`, forward slashes). Use it to build, test, run git or a program the files need; to look at files prefer fs/read, fs/grep and fs/glob, and to change them fs/write and fs/edit (those can be undone; what a command does cannot). Commands your shell setting does not mark safe ask the person first. Give a `timeout` for long builds. Check `exitCode` and `stderr`: a non-zero exit is a failed command, not a failed call. `changes` lists the files the command changed (with diffs in git), so you need not run git diff to see them.',
        examples: [
          { args: { command: 'git status --short' }, note: 'What changed.' },
          { args: { command: 'npm test -- --run src/app.test.ts', timeout: 120_000 }, note: 'One test file, with two minutes.' },
          { args: { command: 'ls -la', cwd: 'docs' } },
        ],
        // a command can do anything a shell can, deleting included; it spends no model money itself
        effects: { mode: 'delete', free: true },
        // the hub asks which sensitive files a call touches first (server/sensitive.ts)
        flags: true,
        args: g.obj({ command: g.text(), cwd: g.optional(g.text()), timeout: g.field(g.optional(g.duration()), { docs: `How long the command may run, in milliseconds (30000 is 30 seconds); at least ${String(COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MIN)}. Leave empty for the default.` }) }),
        returns: BashResult,
        state: Shell,
        progress: linear,
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Running {args.command}…', many: 'Running {n} commands…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying {args.command}…', many: 'Retrying {n} commands…' },
          done: { one: 'Ran {args.command}', many: 'Ran {n} commands' },
          failed: { one: '{args.command} failed', many: '{n} commands failed' },
        },
        handlers: {
          confirm: handler({
            description: 'Run this command? Your shell setting asks first.',
            args: g.obj({ command: g.text(), reason: g.text() }),
            returns: g.bool(),
            required: false,
          }),
        },
      }),
      remove: fn({
        group: 'files',
        description: 'Delete one file under the roots. Gives whether it was removed.',
        instructions: 'Only for a file the person asked to delete, or one you made and no longer need. Folders are refused. It may ask the person first; the content is kept, so undoing the turn puts the file back.',
        examples: [{ args: { path: 'notes/scratch.md' }, result: { removed: true } }],
        effects: { mode: 'delete', free: true },
        // the hub asks which sensitive files a call touches first (server/sensitive.ts)
        flags: true,
        args: g.obj({ path: path() }),
        returns: g.obj({ removed: g.bool() }),
        // two changes to one file in the same step run in the model's order (server/impl.ts fileClaims)
        dynamicClaims: true,
        status: {
          active: { one: 'Deleting {args.path}…', many: 'Deleting {n} files…' },
          retry: { one: 'Retrying deleting {args.path}…', many: 'Retrying {n} deletes…' },
          done: { one: 'Deleted {args.path}', many: 'Deleted {n} files' },
          failed: { one: 'Deleting {args.path} failed', many: '{n} deletes failed' },

        undo: Previous,

      restart: fn({
        group: 'systems',

        instructions: 'Only after changing this system\'s own code (its `self` folder) and checking it builds. Say why in `reason`; runs in progress stop.',
        examples: [{ args: { reason: 'Added the tree command; reload it.' } }],
        effects: { mode: 'write', free: true },
        args: g.obj({ reason: g.text() }),
        returns: g.obj({ restarting: g.literal(true) }),

      install_system: fn({
        group: 'systems',
        description: 'Build, check and start a system folder on this machine, and connect it to the hub, once an admin approves.',
        instructions: [
          'After writing or changing a system folder: builds, checks and starts it on this machine and connects it to the hub. Call it again after each change; it restarts the system with the new code.',
          'Before anything is asked, the folder is typechecked with its own tsconfig.json, bundled and scanned. When that fails the call comes back as invalid input with `file:line:col message` lines: fix each one with fs/edit and call again.',
          'A good build becomes a card for an admin with what the system adds, its scan and its hashes; nothing runs until they approve. If it exits before it connects, the call comes back with its exit code and the end of its output, so you can fix the crash.',
          'The name in system.json must be the contract\'s name. `hub`, and the name of a system that runs somewhere else, are refused unless `replace` is set (the card then says what it replaces).',
          'Once it is running, load its fns with hub/fns_load to call them. When it needs config, ask the person for it (its page has the form).',
        ].join(' '),
        examples: [

            args: { path: 'systems/random' },
            result: { system: 'random', instance: 'main', version: '0.1.0', status: 'connected', fns: ['random/random'], folder: 'C:/work/harness/systems/random' },
            note: 'A new system, approved and running.',

          { args: { path: 'systems/random' }, note: 'Again after a change: the card shows the diff, and the new code replaces the running one.' },
          { args: { path: 'systems/notes', instance: 'laptop', replace: true }, note: 'Replaces a `notes/laptop` that runs elsewhere.' },
        ],
        args: InstallArgs,
        returns: InstallResult,
        // what runs is new code: an admin looks at every install, and the chat must be in write mode or higher
        approval: { preview: true, remember: false, approver: 'admins' },
        effects: { mode: 'write', free: true },
        operations: { cancel: false, background: false },

          retry: { one: 'Installing {args.path} again…', many: 'Installing {n} systems again…' },
          done: { one: 'Installed {args.path}', many: 'Installed {n} systems' },
          failed: { one: 'Installing {args.path} failed', many: '{n} installs failed' },
        },
        tags: ['admin', 'systems'],
      }),
```

```ts
/** The fs contract's type. */
export type FsContract = typeof fs;

/** A file or folder, as fns return it. */
export type EntryValue = ValueOf<typeof Entry>;
/** What a command printed and how it ended. */
export type CommandOutput = Infer<typeof CommandResult>;
/** The diff attachment's data. */
export type DiffValue = Infer<typeof DiffData>;
/** The shell setting's value. */
export type ShellPolicyValue = Infer<typeof ShellPolicy>;
/** How many undo copies fs keeps. */
export type SnapshotLimitsValue = Infer<typeof SnapshotLimits>;
/** The undo copies kept when the config names no limits: 20 per file, 500 MB in all. */
export const SNAPSHOT_DEFAULTS: SnapshotLimitsValue = { perFile: 20, totalMb: 500 };
/** The files mention's target. */
export type FileTargetValue = Infer<typeof FileTarget>;
/** What undo needs to put a file back. */
export type PreviousValue = Infer<typeof Previous>;
/** What `bash` returns. */
export type BashOutput = Infer<typeof BashResult>;
```

## 31. Runtime internals

The rest of what the SDK exports: the pieces `serve` is built from (the manifest builder, keys, the outcome journal, the
link transport, the LLM wire, the MCP face, files and process control, client bundles). A system rarely uses these
directly; they are here so nothing is undocumented, and for tests and tools that need a seam.

The network guard is here too. Every supervised system's process (fs's hosted systems and the hub's installed
instances) gets an `AllowlistProxy` of its own for its `grants.network`, is pointed at it with `guardedEnv`, and
preloads `NETWORK_GUARD` (`--require`), the socket guard (`guardNetwork`, also `@harness/sdk/server/socket-guard`) that
holds every socket to the proxy and the hub. A refused host prints a `networkRefusedLine`, which the supervisor reads
back (`refusedOfLine`) and reports to the hub; `setAllowed` changes what a running proxy lets through.

The hash an approved install binds its pairing grant to (`Hello.contractHash`): the manifest's contract parts, the same

#### `ManifestOptions`

interface · `import { ManifestOptions } from '@harness/sdk'`

What the serve runtime adds to a contract to make its manifest.

```ts
interface ManifestOptions {
  /** The system's version (its package version). */
  version: string;
  /** Hub events its implementation handles. */
  events?: readonly SystemEventType[];
  /** Whether its implementation serves resources. */
  resources?: boolean;
  client?: ClientAssets;
  /**
   * The instance this process is: a runtime provider (`provider({ models: 'runtime' })`) is sent named after it, with
   * `runtimeModels` (the models the running system set). Without one, the manifest has no runtime provider.

}
```

#### `ProviderServer`

class · `import { ProviderServer } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Serves `providerChat`, `providerEmbed`, `providerDecide` and `providerModels` with the system's own aeye providers.

```ts
class ProviderServer {
  constructor(parts: ProviderServerParts);
  /** Stream a chat call: the provider's chunks, then the usage it reported. */
  chat(data: ProviderChat, signal: AbortSignal): AsyncGenerator<ProviderChunk>;
  /**
  * Embed inputs with an embedding model of a provider: aeye's one `inputs` list when it has images, else its `texts`
  * shorthand (so a text-only provider reading `texts` keeps working). An image is an http(s) or data URL, which every
  * aeye provider reads as an `EmbeddingImage`.
  */
  embed(data: ProviderEmbed, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<ProviderEmbedResult>;
  /** Answer questions about an input with a decision model of a provider (aeye's `decide`). */
  decide(data: ProviderDecide, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<ProviderDecideResult>;
  /**
  * The declared models a provider serves now, from its aeye `listModels`, with the limits it serves them with; a
  * provider that can't list says so (`listed: false`), and the hub then offers every declared model.
  */
  models(data: ProviderModels): Promise<ProviderModelsResult>;
}
```

#### `ProviderServerParts`

interface · `import { ProviderServerParts } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What the provider server needs.

```ts
interface ProviderServerParts {
  /** The contract's provider declarations by local name. */
  readonly defs: Readonly<Record<string, ProviderDef>>;
  /** The implementation's provider factories by local name. */
  readonly impls: Readonly<Record<string, (ctx: SystemCtx) => Provider>>;
  /** The system context now, on the system's own signal (throws when there is no valid config). */
  readonly ctx: () => SystemCtx;
  /** The current config version; a provider is made again when it changes. */
  readonly configVersion: () => number | undefined;
  /** The contract's runtime provider, if it has one: its local name, this instance's name and its models now. */
  readonly runtime?: { readonly local: string; readonly instance: () => string; readonly models: () => readonly ProviderModelDecl[] } | undefined;
}
```

#### `CrashPolicy`

class · `import { CrashPolicy } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The restart rule for supervised processes, one per supervisor, keyed by instance: an asked-for stop or a clean exit
(for `on-failure`) stops; the restart code restarts at once; a crash restarts after `backoff`, doubling with each
crash in the last hour, and more than `maxPerHour` crashes in an hour gives up (the caller may roll back).

```ts
class CrashPolicy {
  constructor(options: CrashPolicyOptions);
  /** Decide what follows `exit` of the instance `key`, run under `policy`; `stopping` when the stop was asked for. */
  decide(key: string, policy: RestartPolicy, exit: Pick<ProcessExit, "code">, stopping: boolean): ExitDecision;
  /** Forget an instance's crashes (after a rollback, a new version or an uninstall). */
  forget(key: string): void;
}
```

#### `CrashPolicyOptions`

interface · `import { CrashPolicyOptions } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

How `CrashPolicy` decides.

```ts
interface CrashPolicyOptions {
  /** The longest wait between restarts (`HARNESS_SUPERVISE_BACKOFF_MAX`). */
  readonly maxWait: number;
  /** The exit code that asks for an immediate restart (`RESTART_CODE` unless the supervisor sets another). */
  readonly restartCode: number;
  readonly clock?: (() => number) | undefined;
}
```

#### `ExitDecision`

type · `import { ExitDecision } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What to do after a process exits.

```ts
type ExitDecision =
| { readonly action: 'stopped' }
| { readonly action: 'restart-now' }
| { readonly action: 'restart'; readonly wait: number; readonly crashes: number }
| { readonly action: 'give-up'; readonly crashes: number };
```

type · `import { LlmDescriptors } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The strict-mode descriptors the provider chose while building a request, as they travel back to the system.

```ts
type LlmDescriptors = Omit<Extract<LlmForwardChunk, { type: 'descriptors' }>, 'type'>;
```

Refers to: [`LlmForwardChunk`](#core-llmforwardchunk).

#### `UNSENDABLE_CONTENT`

const · `import { UNSENDABLE_CONTENT } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What stands in for message content the wire can't carry, when placeholders are allowed.

```ts
const UNSENDABLE_CONTENT: "[binary content]";
```

function · `import { messageFromWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A message from the wire as an aeye message.

```ts
function messageFromWire(message: LlmMessage): Message;
```

#### `requestFromWire`

function · `import { requestFromWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A request from the wire rebuilt as an aeye request: tool and output schemas back to zod.


Refers to: [`LlmRequest`](#core-llmrequest).

#### `isObjectSchema`

function · `import { isObjectSchema } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Whether a zod schema describes objects. It reads the schema's definition rather than using `instanceof`, so it
holds for schemas made by another copy of zod (gin's).

```ts
function isObjectSchema(schema: z.ZodType): schema is z.ZodType<object, object>;
```

#### `descriptorsOf`

function · `import { descriptorsOf } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The strict-mode descriptor ids the provider chose while building a request, so the system decodes the same way.

```ts
function descriptorsOf(request: Request): LlmDescriptors;
```

#### `applyDescriptors`

function · `import { applyDescriptors } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Write the descriptors the hub chose into the request a prompt holds, so it decodes the strict wire shape.

```ts
function applyDescriptors(request: Request, descriptors: LlmDescriptors): void;
```

#### `usageToWire`

function · `import { usageToWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

aeye usage as the hub meters it.

```ts
function usageToWire(usage: Usage | undefined): LlmUsage;
```

#### `usageFromWire`

function · `import { usageFromWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Metered usage as aeye usage, with the call's real cost when it is known.

```ts

```

#### `chunkToWire`

function · `import { chunkToWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

One streamed aeye chunk as it travels to a system.

```ts
function chunkToWire(chunk: Chunk): LlmChunk;
```

#### `chunkFromWire`

function · `import { chunkFromWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A streamed chunk from the wire as an aeye chunk.

```ts
function chunkFromWire(chunk: LlmChunk): Chunk;
```

#### `ChunkWriter`

class · `import { ChunkWriter } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A stream's chunks as they travel, one writer per stream: a tool call's growing arguments go as `toolCallMore`, only
the text since the call's last chunk, so a call of n bytes costs n on the link, not n² (aeye's `toolCallArguments`
holds the whole so far on every chunk). Arguments that don't extend what was sent go whole, as `toolCallArguments`.

```ts
class ChunkWriter {
  write(chunk: Chunk): LlmChunk;
}
```

#### `ChunkReader`

class · `import { ChunkReader } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A stream's chunks from the wire as aeye's, one reader per stream: `toolCallMore` is added onto the call's
arguments, and every chunk of a call holds the same call object (as aeye's providers do), so the chunks a stream
keeps share one copy of the arguments.

```ts
class ChunkReader {
  read(wire: LlmChunk): Chunk;
}
```

#### `responseToWire`

function · `import { responseToWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A whole response as it is saved with its interaction. An unfinished response is saved as stopped.

```ts
function responseToWire(response: Response, finished: boolean): LlmResponse;
```

#### `decisionInputToWire`

function · `import { decisionInputToWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A decision call's input as it crosses the wire (`model` and `providerDecide`): text as it is, a conversation as wire
messages (refused when a part can't cross).

```ts
function decisionInputToWire(input: string | Message[]): DecisionInput;
```

#### `decisionInputFromWire`

function · `import { decisionInputFromWire } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

A decision call's input from the wire, as aeye's `DecisionRequest.input`.

```ts
function decisionInputFromWire(input: DecisionInput): string | Message[];
```

#### `addressVerdict`

function · `import { addressVerdict } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Whether `url` may be fetched under `rules`. A literal address and a local name are judged without DNS; a name is
refused when any of its addresses is private, when it resolves to nothing, or when the lookup fails.

```ts
function addressVerdict(url: URL, rules: AddressRules, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<AddressVerdict>;
```

#### `followRedirects`

function · `import { followRedirects } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Fetch `input`, following redirects by hand: every hop is checked first, and a redirect's body is dropped.

```ts
function followRedirects(input: string, init: RequestInit, options: RedirectOptions): Promise<FollowedResponse>;
```

#### `isLocalName`

function · `import { isLocalName } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

True when a hostname names this machine or its network whatever DNS says.

```ts
function isLocalName(hostname: string): boolean;
```

#### `isPrivateAddress`

function · `import { isPrivateAddress } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

True when an IP address (v4 or v6, bracketed or not) is not on the public internet. Names are not addresses: false.

```ts
function isPrivateAddress(address: string): boolean;
```

#### `systemLookup`

const · `import { systemLookup } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The machine's resolver: every address, in the order DNS gave them.

```ts
const systemLookup: HostLookup;
```

#### `REDIRECT_STATUSES`

const · `import { REDIRECT_STATUSES } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The statuses that redirect.

```ts
const REDIRECT_STATUSES: ReadonlySet<number>;
```

#### `AddressRefusal`

type · `import { AddressRefusal } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Why a URL may not be fetched: not http(s); a private address or local name; a name that didn't resolve.

```ts
type AddressRefusal = 'not-http' | 'private' | 'unresolved';
```

#### `AddressRules`

interface · `import { AddressRules } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

What an address check needs.

```ts
interface AddressRules {
  readonly lookup: HostLookup;
  /** Let private addresses and local names through (http and https are still the only schemes). */
  readonly allowPrivate: boolean;
}
```

#### `AddressVerdict`

type · `import { AddressVerdict } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Whether a URL may be fetched; a refusal says why (and, for `unresolved`, what the lookup threw).

```ts
type AddressVerdict =
| { readonly ok: true }
| { readonly ok: false; readonly refusal: AddressRefusal; readonly cause?: unknown };
```

#### `FollowedResponse`

interface · `import { FollowedResponse } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The response a redirect chain ended at (not a redirect), and the URL it came from.

```ts
interface FollowedResponse {
  readonly response: Response;
  readonly url: URL;
}
```

#### `HostLookup`

type · `import { HostLookup } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

Resolves a hostname to its addresses (every A and AAAA record). A seam, so tests answer without DNS.

```ts
type HostLookup = (hostname: string, signal?: AbortSignal) => Promise<readonly string[]>;
```

#### `RedirectOptions`

interface · `import { RedirectOptions } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

How `followRedirects` fetches.

```ts
interface RedirectOptions {
  /** The fetch underneath; it is always called with `redirect: 'manual'`. */
  readonly fetch: FetchFn;
  /** Checked before every hop is requested; throws to refuse it. */
  check(url: URL, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<void>;
  /** The most redirects followed. */
  readonly redirects: number;
  readonly problems: RedirectProblems;
}
```

#### `RedirectProblems`

interface · `import { RedirectProblems } from '@harness/sdk/server'`

The errors a redirect chain fails with, so each caller names them its own way.

```ts
interface RedirectProblems {
  /** A redirect without a location that parses against `at`. */
  noLocation(at: URL): unknown;
  /** More than `limit` redirects from `start`. */
  tooMany(start: URL, limit: number): unknown;
  /** What to throw when the fetch at `at` threw `error` (default: `error` itself). */
  failed?(error: unknown, at: URL): unknown;
}
```

## 32. Core types

The wire shapes from `@harness/core` that the SDK's declarations refer to, each resolved to plain TypeScript. Each is a
zod schema in core with its type inferred from it; import the type (and the schema, to parse) from `@harness/core`.

### From `packages/core/src/access.ts`

#### <a id="core-accessinfo"></a>`AccessInfo`

const, type · declared in `packages/core/src/access.ts`

Whether the hub has more than one person; the web shows visibility controls only when it does.

```ts
type AccessInfo = {
  multiUser: boolean;
};
```

#### <a id="core-sharerule"></a>`ShareRule`

const, type · declared in `packages/core/src/access.ts`

A visibility without its owner, for things whose owner is kept elsewhere (a connection's `owner`).

```ts
type ShareRule = {
  visibility: "private" | "shared" | "public";
  with: string[];
};
```

### From `packages/core/src/admin.ts`

#### <a id="core-hubmodelinfo"></a>`HubModelInfo`

const, type · declared in `packages/core/src/admin.ts`

A model as a system's aeye sees it through the hub (`models` on the hub link, `ctx.ai()` / `hubAi()`):
what `ModelInfo` says plus what aeye's selection needs, and the hub roles it fills. `capabilities` are aeye's
capability names. Only the models the system may use are listed; the hub still checks access and budgets per call.

```ts
type HubModelInfo = {
  id: string;
  provider: `${string}/${string}` | "openai" | "openrouter" | "ollama" | "aws";
  name: string;
  capabilities: string[];
  tier: "flagship" | "efficient" | "legacy" | "experimental";
  roles: ("ocr" | "chat" | "transcribe" | "dictate" | "speak" | "describe" | "decide")[];
  contextWindow?: number | undefined;
  pricing?: {
    input: number;
    output: number;
  } | undefined;
  maxQuestions?: number | undefined;
  maxRequestBytes?: number | undefined;
  maxOutputTokens?: number | undefined;
};
```
